> **Implements Pillar**: Pillar 3 (The World Watches) primary — every craft action publishes disturbance; Pillar 1 (Quiet Is Power) secondary — crafting is part of the noise budget; Pillar 4 (Rounds Not Saves) structural — no in-run inventory persistence.

## Overview

Crafting & Items is the squad's progression rail across a single 5–20 minute run. As **infrastructure** it owns the item schema, the deterministic recipe catalog, the per-player runtime inventory, and the server-authoritative craft and beacon-activate contracts that downstream systems consume — Resource Management's oxygen consumable, Resource Node's gather yields, HUD's inventory readout, and Player Controller's victory-state hand-off all flow through definitions written in this GDD. As **player loop** it is where the squad converts the noise they made — gathered raw materials — into the means of escape: the **Escape Beacon** is the single, named win-condition recipe of the MVP, and every other recipe sits between "more time alive" and "louder, faster, sooner." *(Honesty hedge, round-16: the aid suite's necessity — and the RESONANT tier's only non-Beacon sink, the PA-gated Dampener Coil — is a design target gated on the unwritten Resource Management + Predator AI contracts, F.4; pre-PA, RESONANT is functionally a Beacon-premium material. See C.5.6a.)* Players engage actively and directly, interacting with a crafting bench using the same tap-hold / proximity-gate pattern Player Controller already specified, queueing recipes that consume materials and spend craft-time, then placing and activating the beacon when the recipe completes. Craft actions publish disturbance via `DisturbanceService:Emit` (per ED.C.3.6 for the beacon, and via the four reserved emission enum slots for any other emitting recipes this GDD elects to introduce), making this system a primary contributor to Pillar 3 ("The World Watches"): there is no *quiet* way to craft, only a *quieter* way. Without this system there is no win condition, no progression arc, and no mechanical translation between "we gathered carefully" and "we escaped" — the game would collapse into a survival timer with no way out.

## Player Fantasy

**The Beacon is the loudest thing the squad will ever raise.** Every recipe in this game is a small bargain with the planet — *this much noise, in exchange for this much capability* — and the Escape Beacon is the bargain that ends the run. The instant a player's hand touches the activation switch, the ambient stem cuts, the Hunt stem rises, and the bioluminescent flora around the plinth saturate to maximum and pulse faster than the squad has ever seen them pulse. The squad has ~49 seconds of Hunt-floor lure window before the Beacon's signal decays — 49 seconds of holding the loudest thing they have ever made while the predator commits across the map toward them. The recipes are not blueprints. They are invocations. The bench is not a workstation. It is an altar the squad lights on fire to leave. This is Pillar 3 ("The World Watches") at its most direct: the moment the squad most clearly understands that *this planet has been listening the whole time*.

**The bench is exposure, not refuge.** Crafting in Terranova is the one place in the run where the squad chooses, together, to make noise on purpose, and the choice is never private. A teammate crouches at the bench, the lantern's pool tightened around their forearms; the progress ring fills in soft increments. The other squad members do not craft beside them. They face outward, watching the lantern-pool's edge, listening for the shift in the ambient stem, watching the squad meter tick up with every pulse the bench leaks. Two crafters at the same bench halve dwell time but double the spike. One crafter alone leaks longer. Someone has to choose, and the choice is the section of the loop that earns Pillar 2 ("The Squad Is the Experience") most directly — the bench is a coordination problem disguised as a UI screen. *(The shape of that choice scales with squad size — round-11 honesty note, game-designer B1: in a 3–4-player squad it is the spike-vs-speed decision above, double up to craft faster but louder or stagger to stay quieter; in a 2-player squad the effective bench cap is 1 by design (C.3.5, the round-2 "nobody watches" fix), so the choice there is the simpler but no-less-real "who crafts and who keeps watch." Either way someone commits to the bench and someone faces outward — the bench is never a private action.)* *(The same asymmetry carries into the finale — round-20 honesty note, game-designer G-B2: because `requiredHolders = ceil(peakAliveCount / 2)`, a 2-player squad needs only **one** holder within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` to keep the win condition, so the 2-player survival window is **asymmetric by design** — one player holds the loudest thing the squad has made while the other works the predator (drawing its commit, repositioning, spending aids to buy the holder room). This is the 2-player shape of the Pillar-2 fellowship test: not "both stand on the plinth," but "one holds, one keeps them alive." In 3–4-player squads the threshold rises to 2, so the hold becomes a literal co-stand. A hard floor of 2 holders regardless of squad size is deliberately **not** imposed — it would make the lone-survivor win (C.17 / E.30) impossible, and the asymmetric 2-player finale is a valid, intended experience, not a degenerate one.)*

**Every other recipe is a noise debt.** Beyond the Beacon, the three aid recipes this GDD defines exist so the squad can spend disturbance for a survival edge. Their real value sharpens during the survival window, and each is honest about what it is: the **Dampener Coil** buys **positional freedom** — it lets a player move with the lantern up without spiking the field, so the squad can reposition to break the predator's read on their cluster (and during the finale, that read matters more than ever, because the oxygen they spend is now itself loud — see below). The **Signal Anchor** is a **reactive, single-shot read** — a general-purpose hunt sensor that reports the predator's *first* approach within range, then goes quiet. **Its decisive value is pre-activation recon (round-15 reframe, game F2):** an opening read of where the predator is and which way it is moving, so the squad can choose *when* to light the Beacon and which approach to brace. Under the round-15 continuous-hold rule its in-finale value is honestly limited — a held squad cannot relocate to reposition the read, the predator's BC4 commit is already audible from the beacon itself, and it is single-shot — so it is **not** "decisive during the survival window"; its job is done mostly before activation. (It can still be placed during BC4 for a one-shot read, but that is a marginal use, not its purpose.) Its `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_LIFETIME = 45 s` is a property of the sensor, **not** tuned against the survival window (which is ED-derived and varies across `[35, 70] s`, so at the short end the Anchor can outlast the window and at the long end it cannot). What stops it becoming a fire-and-forget full-window tracker is the **single-shot rule** (C.7), not its lifetime: one piece of intel per Anchor, never a continuous minimap — true regardless of how its 45 s compares to a given run's window. *(Round-9: corrected the round-7 prose, which claimed the 45 s was "deliberately just shorter than the ~49 s window" — false across ED's `BEACON_HALF_LIFE ∈ [64,128] s` band, where the window is `[35,70] s`.)* And the **Oxygen Canister** keeps the squad breathing — but in the finale (BC4) every Canister spent emits, so even buying air is a bargain with the planet. The fantasy at the rim of these recipes is triage panic, briefly suspended: oxygen at 22%, three-second progress ring, the flora at the rim of the lantern pool brighter when the player stands than when they crouched, *something* in their inventory and a hard need to move. Pillar 1 ("Quiet Is Power") names the constraint these recipes operate inside. There is no quiet way to craft, only a *quieter* way — and during the finale there is no quiet way to *survive* either, only to spend the noise well. Completion is followed immediately by exposure, and the squad's job is to spend disturbance *well*, not to avoid it. *(The "predator commits across the map" pressure that makes these aids necessary lives in the Predator AI system; Crafting's aid-necessity is gated on the PA + Resource Management forward obligations in F.4.)*

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

#### C.1 — Material Types

Three raw materials, mapping 1:1 onto the three Resource Node gather tiers locked in ED's registry:

| Material | Symbol | Source node tier | Gather emission magnitude | Narrative role |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Biomass Shard | `BIOMASS` | Light node | `MAGNITUDE_GATHER_LIGHT = 0.15` (ED-locked) | Organic matter — flexible polymers, soft seals; used in survival consumables |
| Mineral Core | `MINERAL` | Medium node | `MAGNITUDE_GATHER_MEDIUM = 0.25` (ED-locked) | Dense crystal — energy-coupling elements, structural frames; used in energy-related recipes |
| Resonant Ore | `RESONANT` | Heavy node | `MAGNITUDE_GATHER_HEAVY = 0.40` (ED-locked) | Rare high-energy deposit — unstable compounds; powers the Beacon and advanced suppression tech |

The three-tier material list is intentionally aligned with the three gather magnitudes — gathering Beacon-critical material (RESONANT) is the loudest pre-activation thing the squad does, and the win condition's ingredients cost the most disturbance to assemble. **Scope of the Pillar-1 economic claim (round-20 ruling, game-designer G-B1):** this aligns *cost* with *loudness* so the win-condition recipe is the loudest thing to build — but it does **not** force a loud gather phase. A squad that routes carefully can assemble the Beacon mostly from low-magnitude Light/BIOMASS gathers (pooling the few RESONANT/MINERAL pulls across members) and stack Oxygen Canisters (BIOMASS) from the surplus, paying relatively little disturbance before activation. **That is intended and acceptable:** Pillar 1 (Quiet Is Power) in the gather phase *rewards* quiet routing — it does not *mandate* loud gathers — and the unavoidable loud beat is the finale (the Beacon activation + the ~49 s survival window, where there is no quiet way to survive, only a quieter way — Section B). The economic claim is therefore "the win-condition recipe is the loudest single thing the squad assembles, and the finale it unlocks is unavoidably loud," **not** "every competent run is loud throughout." A future forcing function (e.g. a minimum loud-gather requirement) is deliberately *not* added — it would risk a new trap/dominant line of the kind the Patch/Wrap/Relay cuts removed.

#### C.2 — Recipe Catalog

Four recipes including the Beacon. All recipes are deterministic (no RNG yields, per game-concept anti-pillar). All recipes are available from run start (no progression gating, per Pillar 4). Burst-at-completion magnitudes are constants registered in this GDD's section G:

> **Round-2 catalog revision (2026-05-30)**: the Composite Patch and Quiet Step Wrap recipes were **cut** in the round-2 patch. Both were gather/sprint-noise reducers flagged as trap recipes in round-1 (gather emissions mostly decay before they matter), and the round-2 redesign moves the run's decisive pressure to the post-activation Beacon survival window (C.5), where gather-phase noise reduction is irrelevant.
>
> **Round-5 catalog revision (2026-06-02)**: the **Squad Relay was cut** (CD ruling R4-2). It spent three review rounds on cut-watch and was, by round-4, economically dominated by stacked Oxygen Canisters under the `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` B1 hold — its only differentiator (a broadcast position-ping) is near-worthless once the squad must cluster on the beacon to win. The cut also shrinks the reconciliation surface the review flagged twice. The **three aid recipes that remain** are the Oxygen Canister and Dampener Coil (which bear directly on *surviving* the Beacon survival window) and the Signal Anchor (whose decisive value is *setting up* the window — pre-activation recon — not surviving it; round-15/16 reframe, C.7). Three freed emission/recipe slots (Patch, Wrap, Relay) are reserved for post-launch content (Open Questions OQ.12).
>
> **Round-19 (R18-2, user-approved) — Signal Anchor KEPT at 5 recipes, with the keep-justification written explicitly (round-18 flagged it as asserted-without-support):** the Anchor survives the same cut-scrutiny that removed Patch/Wrap/Relay on two grounds it does NOT share with those cuts. **(1) It is the squad's ONLY pre-activation read-the-threat tool (Pillar 1):** placed before lighting the Beacon, it reads the predator's opening position/approach so the squad chooses *when* to light and which way to brace — a tactical lever no other recipe provides, and not a "louder/faster/sooner" duplicate of an existing line (the trap-recipe pattern). **(2) It is a load-bearing BIOMASS/MINERAL material sink:** at `2/1/0` it gives BIOMASS a non-Beacon consumer beyond the Canister and MINERAL a non-Beacon consumer **independent of the PA-gated Coil** (G.3 material-sink note) — so cutting it would strand MINERAL on the Coil alone (collapsing MINERAL into the same single-point RESONANT risk). Unlike the Relay it is **not economically dominated** (it does a thing nothing else does, rather than a worse version of the Canister). **Concrete forward obligation (F.4 PA / OQ.15):** the Anchor's recon value is contingent on PA's predator having a *readable* pre-activation approach (a position/trajectory the Anchor's single-shot ping can meaningfully surface) — surfaced as a checkable expectation when PA lands, the honest fence on its keep-justification. If PA playtest shows the pre-activation read is valueless, the Anchor returns to cut-scrutiny; until then it is KEPT on the two grounds above.

| # | Recipe | BIOMASS | MINERAL | RESONANT | Craft time | Burst at completion | Effect (summary — full rules in C.6) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Oxygen Canister | 2 | 0 | 0 | 2 s | `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_XS = 0.10` (craft burst) + `MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE = 0.18` (per-use burst, **only while the beacon is in BC4** — round-7) | Single-use consumable; restores an oxygen pulse to the squad shared pool (exact value defined in Resource Management GDD). A survival-window staple — the cheapest way to buy oxygen during the loud finale. **Round-7 (Group-B bunker counter; emission position → beacon round-11 EC-3):** *using* a Canister while the Beacon is in BC4 emits `MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE` **at the beacon's world position** — every breath of oxygen the squad buys during the loud finale adds to the beacon's roar the predator is locked onto (C.6, C.11, G.4). Outside BC4 the use is silent (the disturbance was paid at craft). |
| 2 | Dampener Coil | 0 | 2 | 1 | 5 s | `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_MID = 0.18` | Time-limited; reduces equipped player's Light pulse magnitude by 50 % for `DAMPENER_DURATION = 60 s` OR until next bench visit, whichever comes first. Lets a player reposition with the lantern up during the survival window without spiking the field. |
| 3 | Signal Anchor | 2 | 1 | 0 | 5 s | `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_MID = 0.18` | Placed passive sensor (BasePart). **(Round-7 re-weight, was 1/1/1)** — BIOMASS-weighted so all three node tiers keep a non-Beacon consumer (Finding-7 BIOMASS-sink fix; cost-neutral at 3 gathers). Pings the squad HUD when the predator passes within `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_DETECTION_RADIUS = 30 studs` of the Anchor. Active for `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_LIFETIME = 45 s`. **The Anchor does NOT emit disturbance** — it is a sensor, not a decoy. See C.7. **Its decisive value is pre-activation reconnaissance (round-15 reframe, game F2; round-16 honesty fix):** an opening read of the predator's position/approach so the squad chooses *when* to light the Beacon and which way to brace. Under the continuous-hold rule its in-finale value is honestly limited (a held squad cannot relocate the read, the predator's BC4 commit is already audible, and it is single-shot) — it is **not** "decisive during the survival window." |
| ~~4~~ | ~~Squad Relay~~ | — | — | — | — | — | **CUT (round-5, R4-2, 2026-06-02)** — economically dominated by stacked Oxygen Canisters under the `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` B1 hold; broadcast position-ping differentiator near-worthless once the squad must cluster. Recipe number retired in place, **not reused** (D.4/D.5 precedent) — the Beacon stays recipe #5 so existing "recipe 5 = Beacon" citations remain valid. |
| 5 | Escape Beacon | 2 | 3 | 3 | 10 s | `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_HEAVY = 0.35` (completion burst) + `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE = 0.18` (one-time mid-craft pulse at `CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_TIME = 5 s` from craft start) + `MAGNITUDE_BEACON = 0.95` on activation per ED.C.3.6 | The win-condition recipe. Crafting completes the Beacon item into the squad shared pool; placement and activation are separate steps (C.5). Activation does **not** win instantly — it opens the survival window (C.5); the squad wins only by living through it. |

**Reserved-band compliance**: per ED.C.1.2, all non-Beacon emissions MUST have `initialMagnitude < 0.85`. The completion bursts (0.10–0.35) and the Beacon midpulse (0.18) clear this constraint by construction. The Beacon's activation emission of 0.95 sits inside the [0.85, 1.00] reserved Beacon-only band per ED.C.3.6.

**Total Beacon assembly cost**: 2 BIOMASS + 3 MINERAL + 3 RESONANT = 8 gather actions across the squad. Worked sanity check (3-player squad, 10 min run): the Beacon (8) **plus an illustrative aid loadout for the survival window** — say 2 Oxygen Canisters (4) + 1 Dampener Coil (3) + 1 Signal Anchor (3) = 18 total gather actions across 3 players over ~10 min (~6 each), inside achievable pace but no longer trivial. **This is an *illustrative* loadout, not a validated optimum** — the actual aid mix a winning run needs is **not validatable until the RM oxygen-drain floor + PA min-aggression contracts land** (OQ.13 / F.4); the 18-gather figure is presented to show the budget is reachable, not to assert it is the realistic line. **(Round-19 economy recommended — the gather cost is not the whole cost:** the 2 illustrative Canisters are not just 4 gather actions — if they are *used during the window* they each also emit `MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE = 0.18` at the beacon (C.6 / E.32), so the in-window oxygen line carries a **noise opportunity-cost on top of the gather cost** — every breath bought during the finale adds to the beacon's roar. The 18-gather reachability figure therefore understates the true cost of the survival loadout; the loud-but-worth-it calibration is OQ.13 / the F.4 RM+PA contracts.)* **(Round-13 Sanity-1 — not in tension with EC-1/H.106's "full `ITEM_STACK_MAX` stack":** these are two *different* scenarios. This 18-gather figure is an **illustrative reachability** check — "can a squad assemble *a* survival loadout in a 10-min run?" — and uses an illustrative 2-Canister buy. The F.4/H.106 inequality is a **worst-case floor-stress** check — "can even a full 10-Canister stack trivialize the window?" — and must hold against the *maximum* stack, not the illustrative buy. A squad assembling 2 Canisters (this check) and a squad hoarding 10 (the floor-stress check) are different lines; both must hold, and they do not contradict.)* *(Round-3: the prior sanity check costed only "1 Canister + Beacon = 10 gathers," which silently modelled the Beacon-rush line the survival window is designed to discourage; the proximity-hold window (C.5.6a / B1) and the RM/PA aid-necessity obligations (F.4) mean a winning run is expected to also assemble aid, so the illustrative gather budget is higher. Final calibration is OQ.13, pending the RM/PA contracts.)* The Beacon itself is still reachable in a 5-minute minimum run without grinding; the RESONANT requirement creates intentional Hunt-tier disturbance spikes during heavy-node gathering.

#### C.3 — Bench Interaction

The crafting bench is a level-design-placed `BasePart` in safe rooms. One bench per safe room; immobile (level-design owns placement; the bench is not destroyable nor relocatable in MVP). The interaction follows the proximity-gate pattern PC GDD C.7 already specified:

1. **Approach**: player's `HumanoidRootPart` enters within `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 4 studs` of the bench (PC-locked constant). Server fires `OnBenchArmed(benchId)` to the owning client; client renders a contextual prompt ("Press E / tap to open bench").
2. **Open**: player commits via single tap (touch) or `E` (PC) once the gate is armed. Server transitions the bench from `BS1 Bench-Idle` to `BS2 Bench-Occupied-Single` (state machine in C.8); records the player as `crafter[1]` in a new `CraftSession` record.
3. **Recipe selection**: bench UI lists the 4 recipes (round-5: Squad Relay cut). Each row shows material costs, craft time, expected completion-burst magnitude, and effect summary. Rows the squad cannot currently afford (insufficient combined personal-inventory materials) render *dimmed* but remain visible — players need the affordability legibility to plan future gathers.
4. **Commit craft**: player selects a recipe and confirms. Server validates: alive; within bench range; recipe valid; combined personal-pool material availability; if Beacon, `RunSession.beaconCrafted == false`. On success, server creates / updates the `CraftSession` and starts the per-Heartbeat progress timer (D.1).
5. **Multi-occupant** (effective cap `benchMaxCraftersEffective`, see below): a second player may join an in-progress craft session by approaching the bench and selecting the *same* recipe via the UI. Server transitions BS2 → BS3 if `craftProgress < CRAFT_JOIN_CUTOFF = 0.85` **AND** the bench's current crafter count is below `benchMaxCraftersEffective`. Each crafter contributes their own `sourcePlayerId` to the emission set. Dwell time scales as 1/N (D.2). Disturbance emission scales as **N concurrent independent emissions** at completion (D.3) — preserves attribution and ED.C.1.2 reserved-band compliance.
   - **Squad-scaled cap (round-2 fix for the 2-player "nobody watches" collision)**: the per-bench crafter cap is **not** the fixed constant `BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS = 2`. It is `benchMaxCraftersEffective = min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(squadSize / 2))`, computed once at run start from the RunSession's squad size (the count of players who started the run; not recomputed on death/disconnect, so the cap never shrinks mid-craft). `BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS = 2` is the hard ceiling. Concrete values: **squad 2 → cap 1** (one crafts, one keeps watch — the bench stays "exposure, not refuge" per Section B); **squad 3 → cap 2**; **squad 4 → cap 2**. Round-1 flagged the fixed cap=2 as a degenerate "nobody watches" state in 2-player squads (a supported config); scaling the cap with squad size resolves it while leaving 3–4-player coordination unchanged. **Accepted limitation (round-7, acknowledging the round-6 note):** the "nobody watches" fix is computed from the *starting* squad size and the cap never shrinks mid-run (so D.2's rate model and the BT2 guard stay stable across a mid-craft death). A squad that *starts* at 3–4 and drops to 2 alive mid-run therefore keeps `benchMaxCraftersEffective = 2`, so both survivors *could* craft together with nobody watching. This is a deliberate trade — recomputing the cap downward mid-run would disrupt an in-progress craft (a worse failure than a transient late-game two-crafter window) — and it is a *late-run* state the squad enters by attrition, not the start-of-run 2-player config the round-2 fix targeted. The transient is accepted; the cap-never-shrinks rule is the higher priority.
6. **Material deduction (atomic at completion)**: materials are NOT deducted at craft start. They are deducted from the combined personal pool atomically at completion, prioritizing in this order: (a) the crafting player(s)' own personal inventories, summed; (b) the rest of the alive squad's personal inventories in `UserId` order. **Dead players' personal inventories are LOCKED** — their materials cannot be auto-pooled until the player respawns. If the squad's combined personal inventory drops below recipe cost during the craft window (e.g., a concurrent craft at another bench consumes a shared material first), the server cancels the craft via `CraftCancelled(reason="insufficient-materials")`.
7. **Cancel**: any crafter may fire `RequestCraftCancel`. If others remain, BS3 → BS2 (or BS2 → BS1 if N=1 cancels). **No partial credit on cancel** — Pillar 4 (Rounds Not Saves) — `craftProgress` resets to 0.0 on full cancel.
8. **Bench cosmetic-boundary**: cosmetic skins on the bench texture / progress-ring style / recipe icons MUST pass the dual-clause test (no predator-AI input mods; no player-survival-decision mods). Forbidden: cosmetics that change craft time, change emission magnitude, change material cost, or hide / obfuscate the burst-at-completion event.

#### C.4 — Inventory Model (Hybrid)

Two-tier server-authoritative inventory. All inventory state is purely runtime — never written to DataStore (per Pillar 4 / no in-run save).

| Tier | Scope | Contents | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Personal** | Per-player; transient | Raw materials only (BIOMASS, MINERAL, RESONANT) | Forces gather → converge-at-bench: each player gathers their own; squad must physically travel to a bench to pool materials. Preserves Section B's "the bench is a coordination problem." |
| **Squad shared** | Per-RunSession; transient | Crafted items only (Oxygen Canister, Dampener Coil, Signal Anchor, Beacon — round-5: Squad Relay cut) | Any alive squad member can use any crafted item. Eliminates the "dead crafter locked the Beacon" soft-lock; preserves squad reactivity. |

**Schemas** (server-side):

```
PersonalInventory[playerId] = {
    biomass:      int,    -- 0..MATERIAL_STACK_MAX
    mineral:      int,    -- 0..MATERIAL_STACK_MAX
    resonant:     int,    -- 0..MATERIAL_STACK_MAX
    lastModified: number, -- _clock() at last mutation (analytics); production default workspace:GetServerTimeNow() per C.16
}

SquadInventory = {
    items:        { [itemId: string]: { count: int, craftedAt: number } },
    -- Beacon: itemId="Beacon"; count is 0 or 1 only (once-per-run enforced separately on the RunSession flag, not on this count)
    -- Consumables stack up to ITEM_STACK_MAX
}
```

**Caps**: `MATERIAL_STACK_MAX = 20` per material per player; `SQUAD_INVENTORY_TYPE_CAP = 8` distinct item types in squad pool; `ITEM_STACK_MAX = 10` per stack (consumables only — Beacon is single-instance). The MATERIAL_STACK_MAX cap creates "drop these at the bench before gathering more" tension once a heavy gatherer fills up.

**Equipped-item ownership**: the Dampener Coil (the only item with a localized equipped effect post-round-2) has an implicit owner — the player who equipped it — recorded at equip-time on the SquadInventory entry. Effects apply only to the owner. **Ownership cannot be transferred mid-effect** (would create exploit paths around the consumable rule). On equipped player's death, the active effect terminates immediately.

#### C.5 — Beacon Lifecycle

The Beacon is the only recipe whose post-craft lifecycle requires a separate state machine (full machine in C.9). Concept-level rules:

1. **Once-per-run constraint** is enforced on `RunSession.beaconCrafted: bool`, set at recipe-completion. **Per-squad, not per-player** — preventing two-Beacon spam exploits and rationalizing the "this is a squad event" framing of Section B.
2. **Two-step lifecycle**: completing the Beacon recipe places a single-instance `Beacon` item in the squad shared inventory (state BC2). Placement is a separate explicit interaction (`RequestBeaconPlace`) that any alive squad member can fire while holding the Beacon item; on success the Beacon item is removed from the shared pool and a `Beacon` BasePart is spawned at the validated placement position (state BC3). Activation is a third explicit interaction (`RequestBeaconActivate`) that any alive squad member within `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 4 studs` of the placed Beacon can fire (state BC4 — emission live). **Both placement and activation are tap-hold commits** (`TAP_HOLD_COMMIT_DURATION = 0.5 s`, PC-locked; B6 round-3 ruling) — the client only emits `RequestBeaconPlace` / `RequestBeaconActivate` after the hold completes. This matches the art-bible tap-hold lock for high-consequence actions and is consistent with the bench's two-tap recipe-commit (C.3); it is the friction model against an accidental, irreversible placement/activation. The hold is a client-side input gate only — the server still performs all validation below and never trusts the client's "hold complete" as authority over alive/range/flag state.
3. **No pickup after placement**: state BC3 is terminal-pre-activation; the Beacon BasePart cannot be returned to inventory. Bad placements stand. This serves Pillar 1 — even placement is a transaction the squad cannot undo without spending more noise.
4. **Cross-player rescue**: the placing player may die between BCT2 (place) and BCT3 (activate). Any alive squad member within range can activate. Server uses `RunSession.beaconPlacerId` for `sourcePlayerId` in the `Emit` call (preserves attribution per ED.C.3.6's `"BeaconPlaced"` action label semantics) — not the activator's ID.
5. **Placement validity (server-checked)**: the client-supplied position is a **direction hint, not a trusted coordinate** (round-2 B13) — the server validates in this fixed order: (a) **clamp** the requested point to within `BEACON_PLACE_MAX_RANGE = 8 studs` of the player's server-tracked position; (b) **LOS reachability raycast (round-3 B4)** — cast from the player's `HumanoidRootPart` position to the clamped candidate; if any wall/world geometry blocks the segment, **reject** (`BeaconRejected(reason="unreachable-placement")`). This closes the round-2/3 cross-wall exploit: clamp-distance + downward floor-raycast alone would let a player place a beacon/anchor *through* a wall into an adjacent unreachable room (the candidate is within 8 studs and has a floor beneath it, but the squad cannot stand near it to hold/activate). The LOS ray requires an unobstructed line from the placing player to the candidate; (c) **raycast downward** from the clamped candidate to find a valid floor; (d) **overlap check** vs. existing geometry / other beacons within a small bounding box. Invalid positions are rejected with `BeaconRejected(reason="invalid-placement")` (floor/overlap) or `"unreachable-placement"` (LOS). Specific surface-type rules (slope tolerance, level-designer-defined exclusion zones) are level-design's authoring responsibility — flagged in Section F. The same clamp → LOS → floor → overlap order applies to `RequestPlaceItem` (Signal Anchor — the only placeable aid item post-round-5).
6. **Active emission + window start**: at BCT3 (activate), server calls `DisturbanceService:Emit("Beacon", beaconWorldPosition, 0.95, beaconPlacerId)` per ED.C.3.6, fires `OnBeaconActivated` (the HUD/audio activation cue), fires `OnEscapeBeaconActivated` (the window-**start** signal — see C.5.6a below; this is **no longer the victory trigger**), records `RunSession.beaconActivationTime = _clock()` (R4-4; production default `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` per the C.16 seam), and **starts the survival-window timer as a per-`RunService.Heartbeat` comparison, NOT a `task.delay` callback** (B5 round-3). Window-end is detected by evaluating `now - RunSession.beaconActivationTime >= BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` on each Heartbeat while in BC4 (the same Heartbeat tick on which deaths are processed). This pin is load-bearing for determinism: a `task.delay(BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW)` callback could fire *between* Heartbeats and race the death handler non-deterministically, breaking the E.25/H.77 same-Heartbeat tie-break; a Heartbeat-polled comparison makes "did the window elapse this tick?" and "did the last member die this tick?" two evaluations in one deterministic, single-threaded tick. **T8 does NOT fire at BCT3.** Activation opens the survival window; it does not win the run. *(Round-2 change, 2026-05-30: pre-round-2 this rule fired T8 immediately at activation, which made the run end the instant the Beacon was activated and rendered every aid item dominated strategy — the round-1 Beacon-dominance finding. Victory now requires surviving the window.)*
   - **6a — Survival window (the win condition)**: activation starts the **Beacon survival window**, `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW ≈ 49 s`. This length is **derived from ED's beacon-decay math** — the interval the Beacon holds at or above `HUNT_THRESHOLD = 0.65` (`MAGNITUDE_BEACON = 0.95` decaying at `BEACON_HALF_LIFE = 90 s` crosses 0.65 at age ≈ 49 s), i.e. the predator's peak-threat commit window per ED.D.7 / the ED beacon worked example. **ED owns the length; this GDD references it** and registers `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` as a derived value (G.6), exactly as `CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_TIME` is derived. During the window the predator commits to the beacon at Hunt-floor and the squad must survive its peak pressure.
     - **Victory (continuous-hold + holder threshold — B1 round-13 fail-fast, round-15 SENTRY fix)**: the squad wins when the survival-window timer elapses **while the hold is unbroken** — i.e. on the window-end Heartbeat **at least `requiredHolders` squad members are alive AND within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs`** of `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition`, where `requiredHolders = min(requiredHoldersBaseline, #aliveMembers)` and `requiredHoldersBaseline = ceil(peakAliveCount/2)` is **frozen at activation from the run's high-water alive count** (round-16 R16-1 freeze + round-19 R18-1 ratchet — computed once at BCT3, not recomputed per tick and not taken from the activation snapshot; the per-tick `min` only clamps it down to the current alive count, so a respawn never raises the bar, **delaying activation until teammates die never lowers it** (the round-18 activate-late lane is closed), and a lone survivor always wins; C.10) — at which point the server fires `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` → Player Controller's T8 (squad escape → victory) transition (C.9 BCT4). **Unlike the round-3/round-9 design — which evaluated proximity *only* at the window-end tick and explicitly let members "freely leave and re-enter the radius mid-window" — the hold must now be maintained CONTINUOUSLY**: the instant **fewer than `requiredHolders`** alive members are within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`, the squad enters a **line-break** state (see Defeat). Two degenerate lines are closed: round-13's continuous rule closes **KITING** ("activate, scatter ~38 s, regroup the final ~10 s"), and round-15's `requiredHolders` closes **SENTRY** ("appoint ONE member to camp a corner ~28 studs out while everyone plays freely") — at radius 30 with a `>= 1` rule, that one sentry held the line for the whole squad, contradicting the Section B "hold the line" fantasy. Now **at least half the living squad** must stand the ground at the plinth through its **entire** loud finale, which is exactly the Section B fantasy ("holding the loudest thing they have ever made"). `requiredHolders=1` when one member is alive, so a lone survivor still wins by holding alone. `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` is a Crafting-owned tuning knob (G.6, registry); **12 studs is a tight last-stand on the plinth — "in-radius" ≡ "on the beacon", not "somewhere in the safe room"** — and that tight, central zone is what makes the 3 s line-break grace traversable (a displaced member re-enters in well under 3 s), resolving the round-14 grace-vs-mobile-recoverability tension by shrinking the radius rather than stretching the grace.
     - **Defeat — line-break (B1 round-13 fail-fast)**: the run ends in defeat — `RunEnded(defeat)` (C.9 transition BCT-DEFEAT) — when the hold is broken and not re-established in time. Precisely, on any BC4 Heartbeat the server builds the per-tick `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot (C.10); if that snapshot is **below the holder threshold** (`#windowAliveInRadius < requiredHolders`), one of two paths fires: (a) **wipe** — if `RunSession.aliveMembers` is empty after the tick's drains (the whole squad is dead/disconnected), defeat fires **immediately** (`reason="wipe"`), because no one is left to regroup, and **wins a same-tick tie over scatter** (B-1 round-15); or (b) **scatter / line-break** — if members are still alive but fewer than `requiredHolders` are within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`, a **line-break grace** of `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE ≈ 3 s` (G.6) begins. If enough alive members re-enter the radius (back to `>= requiredHolders`) before the grace elapses, the hold is re-established and the run continues (the grace resets); if the grace elapses with the snapshot still below threshold (and members still alive), defeat fires (`reason="scatter"`). The grace exists so a single predator knockback, a brief forced reposition, or position jitter at the radius boundary does not instantly lose the run — it is far too short (~3 s) to permit the KITING scatter line. Both sub-cases fire `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason)`; PC maps both to `RunEnded(defeat)` (C.12.1, unchanged — the reason enum stays `{"scatter", "wipe"}`). The HUD surfaces a binary **HOLDING / BROKEN** state with the grace countdown during a line-break (UI surface 13) so "hold the beacon" is legible and a line-break is never a silent rule. *(Round-13: replaces the round-3 window-end-only proximity check + the round-9 "advisory, kiting unpunished" framing — the hold is now continuous and evaluative; `reason="scatter"` is redefined from "window elapsed with all members fled" to "the hold was broken — members alive, fewer than `requiredHolders` in radius — for the full grace.")*
     - **Aid-suite necessity (B2 round-3; round-7 — Crafting now owns a structural counter):** the *intent* is that this window makes the three aid recipes **load-bearing** — a squad that rushed the Beacon with no aid preparation should face ~49 s of maximum predator pressure it cannot trivially bunker through. Coils to reposition with the lantern up, an Anchor to read the predator's approach, stockpiled Oxygen Canisters to survive the drain — each aid recipe should earn its cost here. **Round-7 (Group-B, user-approved Hybrid A+B):** the round-4 Squad-Relay cut left the Oxygen Canister as the sole oxygen line, and the round-6 panel found the resulting "stack ~10 Canisters and bunker quietly" line was the dominant *quiet* solution to the loud finale — ludonarrative dissonance the design must not ship. Crafting now owns **one structural counter** to that line: **in-window Canister use emits disturbance** (`MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE`, C.6 / C.11 / G.4), so the bunker line is **not free even before RM/PA land** — every breath of oxygen bought during the window is heard. The **remaining** necessity (that the squad genuinely *needs* to buy that oxygen, and that hiding is punished) is gated on two contracts owned by unwritten sibling GDDs, declared as **falsifiable, AC-bearing forward obligations** in F.4: (i) a **Resource Management oxygen-drain floor** during BC4 (`OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR`, with a binding inequality + AC), and (ii) a **Predator AI minimum aggression** during BC4 (`PREDATOR_BC4_MIN_COMMIT`, with a binding engagement AC). Until both land, *full* aid-suite necessity is a **design target** gated on those two ACs — but the bunker line is no longer free in their absence. *(Round-3 relocated this from asserted fact to forward obligation; round-7 adds the Crafting-owned structural counter and converts the obligations from prose targets into checkable numeric contracts.)* **Round-15 (F6 ruling — predator vs. a held squad):** the continuous-hold rule means a held squad **cannot flee** the finale (fleeing IS the line-break that loses the run), so the `PREDATOR_BC4_MIN_COMMIT` obligation is escalated from "a future-GDD AC" to a **binding interaction constraint** (F.4 PA row): during BC4 the predator MUST telegraph its beacon commit with enough reaction lead for a held squad to spend aids / micro-reposition inside the hold radius, and **MUST NOT one-shot a sole holder without a wind-up window** — otherwise BC4 is a guaranteed loss for a squad that correctly held. The aids exist to be spent against a *survivable-but-punishing* finale, not an unavoidable execution; "BC4 must be winnable by holding" is the binding floor on PA's BC4 design. **Honesty hedge surfaced here (round-16 economy ×3 — keep the fence visible at the point of the claim, not only buried in F.4):** this aid-necessity is a **design target gated on the unwritten RM + PA contracts (F.4)**, not a shipped guarantee. In particular the **RESONANT** node tier's only non-Beacon consumer is the **Dampener Coil**, whose positional-freedom value is itself PA-gated — so **pre-PA, RESONANT is functionally a Beacon-premium material** (its practical non-Beacon sink does not exist until PA makes a Coil worth crafting). The CD ruling (R16-4, user-approved) is to **keep RESONANT as a Beacon-justified premium material** (not recut the Coil now); the binding closure is the F.4 PA row (d) AC — "≥1 Dampener Coil worth crafting in a winning run at default tunings" — with the honest fallback of declaring RESONANT Beacon-only + recutting the Coil if PA/RM playtest cannot satisfy it (OQ.15). Until RM/PA land, the aid suite (and RESONANT's non-Beacon role) is honestly a *target*, not a fact.
7. **Decay (defensive fallback)**: natural source-expiry — ED detecting `m(t, age) <= MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02` (~501 s after activation per ED.D.1) — fires `OnBeaconDecayed` and removes the Beacon BasePart at BCT5. **This is now normally unreachable**: the run ends in victory or defeat at window-end (~49 s), ~10× sooner than the ~501 s natural decay, and beacon cleanup then runs through the run-end cleanup path (as in E.8), not BCT5. The decay state is retained only as a defensive fallback for any path that would otherwise leave a beacon source live past natural expiry. *(Round-2 note: pre-round-2 this was C.5.7 "Decay at BCT4" and was the squad's post-victory cleanup; it keeps the same C.5.7 number and the same decay semantics, only demoted to a fallback.)*
8. **Beacon cosmetic-boundary**: cosmetic skins on the Beacon BasePart's appearance MUST NOT change the placement BasePart's bounding volume, the beacon's emission position (always the BasePart center), the activation emission magnitude (locked at 0.95), or the visibility of activation feedback. Forbidden: cosmetics that would change the predator AI's read of the beacon's spatial signature, or change which players can identify a beacon as a beacon.

#### C.6 — Aid Item Effects

All aid items are **consumables or time-limited deployables** — never permanent — to mitigate the degenerate "stack permanent silence" strategy. Effect rules per item:

> **Round-2 cut (2026-05-30)**: the **Composite Patch** and **Quiet Step Wrap** items were removed from the catalog (C.2). Their effect bullets, the `D.4` / `D.5` formulas, the `EquippedEffects.compositePatch` / `.quietStepWrap` schema fields, the Composite-Patch ↔ Resource-Node accessor (C.13), and their edge cases/ACs/VA-moments are removed or stubbed.
>
> **Round-5 cut (2026-06-02)**: the **Squad Relay** was removed (R4-2 — its bullet is retired in place below). **The three aid items below (Oxygen Canister, Dampener Coil, Signal Anchor) are the full round-5 set.**

- **Oxygen Canister**: server-authored consumable. Any alive player can equip + use via `RequestUseItem("OxygenCanister")`. On use, server fires `OnOxygenPulseRequest(amount)` to Resource Management Service (RM owns the actual transfer math). **The `OnOxygenPulseRequest` dispatch to RM MUST be fire-and-forget (a signal emit, never a yielding `RemoteFunction`/awaited call) — round-16 network recommended:** the `RequestUseItem` handler must not yield (H.80 zero-yield), so it fires the RM signal and continues; RM applies the restore on its own path. Item removed from shared pool. Use is instant (no progress ring). During the Beacon survival window (C.5.6a) this is the squad's cheapest source of emergency oxygen.
  - **In-window disturbance (round-7 — the Group-B bunker counter):** canister *use* is **conditional on beacon state**. **Outside BC4** (`RunSession.beaconActivated == false` OR after window resolution) — use generates **no additional disturbance** (the disturbance was paid at craft completion), so pre-window triage stays cheap. **While the survival window is genuinely live** (`RunSession.beaconActivated == true && not RunSession.runOutcomeResolved`) — after the RM oxygen restore, the server fires `DisturbanceService:Emit("Craft", RunSession.beaconWorldPosition, MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE = 0.18, usingPlayerId)`. **(Round-16 network BLOCKING-1 fix — the guard is `not runOutcomeResolved`, NOT `beaconWindowSurvived == false`:** `beaconWindowSurvived` flips only on *victory*, so after a *defeat* (scatter/wipe) it stays `false` while the run is over — a `RequestUseItem` interleaving with the Heartbeat handler between ticks would then fire this Emit at a stale `beaconWorldPosition` into an already-ended run. `runOutcomeResolved` is set by **every** terminal — victory AND defeat — so gating on it closes that post-terminal orphaned-emission window. Verified by H.93.) The emission is attributed to the **using player** but fires **at the beacon's world position** (round-11 EC-3 ruling — *not* the using player's position). `RequestUseItem`'s payload carries no position, and `beaconWorldPosition` is a server-tracked `RunSession` field, so there is no client-supplied coordinate to spoof. This converts the "stack ~10 Canisters and bunker quietly through the window" line into "every breath of oxygen you buy adds to the beacon's roar the committing predator is already locked onto" — the Pillar-3 thesis applied to the finale (Section B's "every recipe is a small bargain with the planet"). The squad may still stockpile Canisters; they can no longer spend them silently while the predator is at peak commit. **EC-3 ruling (round-11 — beacon-vs-player emission position):** the round-7/9 design emitted at the *using player's* position ("reveal where you are"); round-10 economy flagged a **scatter-to-breathe** concern — a member who leaves the hold and breathes would emit noise *away* from the beacon, potentially baiting the predator off the held plinth and *softening* the finale the counter exists to harden. **CD ruling (user-approved round-11): emit at `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition` during BC4.** Rationale: during the finale the beacon is the squad's loud signal and oxygen spent to survive it concentrates *at the beacon* — this (a) structurally forecloses the scatter-to-breathe line (a breath can never pull the predator away from the held beacon), (b) preserves the bunker-counter intent (breathing while holding adds to the predator's read of the hold point), and (c) is fully server-authoritative (a `RunSession` field, not even a player position to track). A member who scattered out of radius and breathes still emits at the beacon, not at themselves — consistent with the win rule that only alive-and-in-radius members matter. This is the Crafting-owned half of aid-suite necessity (C.5.6a); the rest is the RM/PA forward obligations (F.4). It does **not** touch the win-condition completion/attribution path — it is a new emission on the already-validated `RequestUseItem` handler, and `usingPlayerId` (not the beacon placer) stays the `sourcePlayerId` so attribution is correct.
- **Dampener Coil**: equip via `RequestEquipItem("DampenerCoil")`. Reduces equipped player's Light pulse magnitude by 50 % for **`DAMPENER_DURATION = 60 s` real-time OR until next bench visit OR equipped player's death, whichever comes first**. Stacking rule: one per player. Multiple players may each equip their own Coil. Effect terminates at expiry; item is fully consumed. **The only remaining emission modifier in the game** — its interaction with PC stationary-attenuation is the sole entry in the canonical order-of-operations (D.6a). **Equip atomic-step order (round-3, prevents the double-equip / double-consume race):** the `RequestEquipItem` handler runs, in this fixed order with **no yield** between steps (per H.80): (1) validate alive + item in `SquadInventory` + player has no active Coil instance (the `EquippedEffects[playerId].dampenerCoil == nil` guard); (2) decrement the Coil from `SquadInventory` (consume-on-equip); (3) write `EquippedEffects[playerId].dampenerCoil = {equippedAt, expiresAt}`; (4) fire `OnEquippedEffectStarted`. Because step 1's guard and step 3's write are not separated by a yield, a same-Heartbeat double `RequestEquipItem` is sequenced by Luau's single-threaded scheduler — the second sees the active instance from step 3 and is rejected, so the item cannot be consumed twice.
- **Signal Anchor**: place via `RequestPlaceItem("SignalAnchor", position)`. Server validates position (clamp-to-range + raycast-to-floor + overlap check + **LOS reachability raycast** per C.5.5 / B4), spawns the Anchor BasePart, starts a `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_LIFETIME = 45 s` lifetime timer. The Anchor is a sensor — see C.7. Single-use placement; item is consumed on placement (not on lifetime expiry). Maximum **one Signal Anchor live in the world at a time per squad** (placing a second cancels the first). **Lifetime note (round-3; reframed round-15/round-16):** `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_LIFETIME = 45 s` is a property of the sensor, **NOT tuned against the survival window** (which is ED-derived and varies across `[35, 70] s`, so at the short end the Anchor outlasts the window and at the long end it cannot). What keeps the Anchor from becoming a fire-and-forget tracker is the **single-shot rule** (C.7) and the single-live-Anchor rule (E.16), not its lifetime. **Role (round-15 reframe, game F2 — round-16 fix to the stale round-3 "deliberately shorter, place it *during* the window" rationale):** the Anchor's decisive use is **pre-activation reconnaissance** — placed *before* lighting the Beacon to read the predator's opening position/approach. Under the continuous-hold rule a held squad cannot relocate to act on a *during*-window read, so the in-finale use is marginal (one-shot, the predator's commit is already audible from the beacon). The 45 s lifetime is generous enough for a pre-activation scout-and-light sequence without making the Anchor a perimeter sensor net. *(Round-3's "deliberately just shorter than the ~49 s window, place it during the finale" framing is retired — it was both false across ED's half-life band and inconsistent with the round-15 pre-activation-recon reframe.)*
- ~~**Squad Relay**~~: **CUT (round-5, R4-2, 2026-06-02).** The placed squad-oxygen relay was removed from the catalog (C.2) — dominated by stacked Oxygen Canisters under the B1 hold. Its `RequestActivateRelay` event (C.15), `ActiveDeployables.squadRelay` field (C.10), `OnOxygenTransferRequest` RM signal (F.2/F.4), VA Moments 24/25, UI surface 10, and H.23 are all retired in place. The squad-oxygen lifeline role during the window is now served by stockpiled **Oxygen Canisters** (the cheaper, non-dominated line). No surviving aid item uses a two-step place-then-activate consume; the **Signal Anchor** is the only remaining placeable, and it consumes on placement.

**Scaling-with-N rule for equipped/placed items**: the burst-at-completion magnitude in C.2 is the *total* emission for the recipe regardless of how many crafters. Multi-crafter scaling (D.3) doubles the *number* of emissions, not the per-emission magnitude. So two crafters making a Dampener Coil emit two Coil bursts at 0.18 each — the squad *receives* one Coil item, but the world hears the bench twice.

#### C.7 — Signal Anchor Sensor

The Signal Anchor is a passive sensor — the only crafted item that does NOT publish disturbance. This is a deliberate design choice: a Gather-pulse-emitting Anchor would be a decoy (which the user explicitly excluded from the MVP scope at the design-frame stage). The Anchor sense-but-don't-broadcast model preserves a useful coordination tool without violating the "no decoys / distractors" rule. **Role (round-15 reframe, game F2):** the Anchor's decisive value is **pre-activation reconnaissance** — an opening read of the predator's position/approach that informs *when* to light the Beacon and which way to brace. Under the continuous-hold rule (C.5.6a) it is honestly **not** decisive *during* the survival window (a held squad cannot relocate the read, the predator's BC4 commit is already audible, and it is single-shot); it can still be placed in BC4 for a one-shot read, but that is a marginal use. See Section B.

**Mechanic**:
1. On placement (C.6 above), server spawns the Anchor BasePart and registers it with `PredatorPerceptionService` (or equivalent server-side predator-tracking surface — the exact subscription channel is the Predator AI GDD's territory; this GDD assumes the predator's server-tracked position is queryable by Crafting Service).
2. Server runs a per-Heartbeat distance check (or equivalent throttled cadence — recommended `SENSOR_CHECK_HZ = 5` matching ED's `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ`) between the Anchor position and the predator's current server-tracked position.
3. When `dist3D(anchor, predator) <= SIGNAL_ANCHOR_DETECTION_RADIUS = 30 studs` for the first time within the Anchor's lifetime (single-shot trigger — the Anchor reports the predator's first proximity-cross, not every tick of proximity), server fires `OnSignalAnchorTripped(anchorId, anchorPosition)` to all alive squad clients. The HUD GDD owns the visual presentation (a directional indicator pointing to the Anchor's position; spec deferred to HUD GDD).
4. **Single-shot trigger** means each Anchor reports *one* detection event per lifetime. Subsequent predator approaches within range during the same Anchor's lifetime do NOT re-trigger. Rationale: a re-triggering Anchor becomes a continuous tracking device, which would render the predator-as-puzzle / Pillar 1 tension into a UI readout. Single-shot preserves the "the world watches" reciprocity — the squad gets one piece of intel, not a permanent sensor net.
5. **Lifetime expiry**: at `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_LIFETIME = 45 s` (or whichever comes first: trip, lifetime expiry, or run end), server removes the Anchor BasePart and fires `OnSignalAnchorExpired(anchorId)`.

**Cosmetic-boundary**: cosmetic skins on the Anchor's appearance MUST NOT change the detection radius, the trigger condition, or the visual indicator on the HUD. Forbidden: cosmetics that increase detection radius or convert the Anchor to a multi-shot sensor.

### States and Transitions

#### C.8 — Bench State Machine

Crafting bench states. The per-bench crafter cap is the squad-scaled `benchMaxCraftersEffective = min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(squadSize / 2))` (C.3.5), with hard ceiling `BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS = 2`. In a 2-player squad the effective cap is 1, so BS3 is unreachable and BT2 never fires (one player crafts, one watches); in 3–4-player squads the cap is 2 and BS3 is reachable. The state machine generalizes to N but only states for N ≤ 2 are MVP. No separate "interrupted" state — BT4 and BT5 cover crafter-departure cleanly.

| State ID | Name | Description |
|---|---|---|
| BS1 | Bench-Idle | No crafter present. Bench is available. `craftProgress = 0.0`; no `CraftSession` record. |
| BS2 | Bench-Occupied-Single | Exactly one crafter. `craftProgress` incrementing per D.1 at single-rate. |
| BS3 | Bench-Occupied-Multi | Up to `benchMaxCraftersEffective` crafters (= 2 only when squad size ≥ 3; **unreachable in a 2-player squad**). `craftProgress` incrementing per D.1 at 1/N-accelerated rate (D.2). |

**Transitions:**

| ID | Trigger | From | To | Side Effects |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BT1 | Player fires `RequestCraft(recipeId)`, server validation passes (alive; within `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 4 studs`; recipe in catalog; combined personal-pool has materials; if Beacon, `RunSession.beaconCrafted == false`) | BS1 | BS2 | Server: create `CraftSession {benchId, benchPosition=<bench's immutable world position, snapshotted now>, recipeId, crafters=[playerId], primaryCrafterId=playerId, craftProgress=0.0, startTime=now, lastCrafterCount=1, midpulseFired=false}` — `benchPosition` (round-2 B8) and `primaryCrafterId` (round-2 B7) are both set at BT1 and drive every D.3/D.7 emission; fire `OnCraftStarted(benchId, crafterId, recipeId, estimatedCompletionTime)` to squad |
| BT2 | Second player fires `RequestCraft(recipeId)` for the SAME recipe while bench in BS2; validation passes AND `craftProgress < CRAFT_JOIN_CUTOFF = 0.85` AND current crafter count (1) `< benchMaxCraftersEffective` (i.e. squad size ≥ 3) | BS2 | BS3 | Server: append second player to `CraftSession.crafters`; recalculate `craftRate` from current `craftProgress` at N=2 rate (D.2); update `lastCrafterCount = 2`; fire `OnCraftStarted` for the joining player + `OnBenchOccupancyChanged(benchId, 2)` to squad. *(In a 2-player squad `benchMaxCraftersEffective = 1`, so this guard fails and the join is rejected with `OnCraftRejected(reason="bench-full")` per E.2.)* |
| BT3 | `craftProgress >= 1.0` (D.1 reaches completion threshold) | BS2 or BS3 | BS1 | Server (atomic, in this order): (1) deduct materials from combined personal pool per C.3.6; (2) add crafted item to squad shared pool (or set `RunSession.beaconCrafted = true` for Beacon); (3) for each crafter `i`, call `DisturbanceService:Emit("Craft", emitPosition, recipeBurstMagnitude, crafterId_i)` per D.3, where **`emitPosition = RunSession.beaconWorldPosition` if the beacon is in a LIVE survival window (`beaconActivated == true and not runOutcomeResolved`), else `benchPosition`** (round-15 Ruling 3 — in-window craft bursts draw to the beacon, parallel to in-window Canister use E.32; closes the scatter-to-craft-quietly line, F5; see E.17. **Round-23 qa BLOCKING-1: the guard is `not runOutcomeResolved`, NOT the loose `not beaconWindowSurvived` form — a craft completing AFTER the window resolves [victory OR scatter/wipe defeat, so `runOutcomeResolved == true`] must NOT emit at a stale `beaconWorldPosition` into an ended run; this mirrors the in-window Canister guard C.6/C.11/E.32/H.93(c) exactly.**); (4) fire `OnCraftCompleted(benchId, recipeId, crafterIds)` to squad + `OnInventoryChanged` to relevant clients; (5) clear `CraftSession`; reset bench to BS1 |
| BT4 | One crafter departs (player death, disconnect, exits range > `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS + 2 studs` tolerance, OR fires `RequestCraftCancel`) while bench in BS3 | BS3 | BS2 | Server: remove departed crafter from `CraftSession.crafters`; recalculate `craftRate` from current `craftProgress` at N=1 rate (D.2); update `lastCrafterCount = 1`; fire `OnBenchOccupancyChanged(benchId, 1)` to squad. **Progress is preserved** (no setback for predator-driven mid-craft interruption). |
| BT5 | All crafters depart before completion (last remaining crafter triggers any of the BT4 conditions) | BS2 or BS3 | BS1 | Server: clear `CraftSession`; **`craftProgress` discarded** — no partial credit (Pillar 4: Rounds Not Saves); materials NOT deducted (craft did not complete); fire `OnCraftCancelled(benchId, recipeId, reason)` to squad |
| BT6 | Server-side detection of insufficient materials mid-craft (rare race with concurrent inventory mutations) | BS2 or BS3 | BS1 | Server: clear `CraftSession`; fire `OnCraftCancelled(benchId, recipeId, reason="insufficient-materials")`; identical to BT5 otherwise |

**Disconnect / death handling (buffered — round-5 R4-5):** departures do **NOT** mutate `CraftSession` or run state from inside the event callback. Both `Players.PlayerRemoving` (disconnect) and `Humanoid.Died` (death) callbacks, plus the 5 Hz proximity watcher's out-of-range detection (E.13), **append the departure to a per-Heartbeat buffer** (`_pendingDepartures`, a list of `{playerId, reason}`) via the shared `_enqueueDeparture` seam (round-7 A-3); the buffer is **drained inside the `CraftingService` Heartbeat handler** (C.16 `_step`), not in the event callback. This is the round-5 fix to the round-4 production-determinism finding (see the tie-break note below). **`RequestCraftCancel` is routed the same way (round-7 — resolving the round-6 buffering-ambiguity note):** the RemoteEvent is *received and validated* directly in its handler (it confirms the caller is a registered crafter), but the crafter-removal *effect* is enqueued via `_enqueueDeparture(playerId, "player-initiated")` and drained in the same Heartbeat handler — so the BT3-completion-vs-cancel tie-break (E.29 / H.81) resolves by the identical straight-line "completion check first, drain second" ordering as death/disconnect, not by whether the cancel RemoteEvent happened to arrive before or after the progress tick.

**Buffer scope + dispatch mechanism (round-5 R4-5; concrete mechanism round-7 A-3):** there are **two** departure-drain scopes, both fed from the *same* `Humanoid.Died` / `Players.PlayerRemoving` event connections: (1) a **bench scope** — one `_pendingDepartures` queue per active `CraftSession`, drained in the bench progress-tick to fire BT4/BT5 for departed crafters (C.8 tie-break); and (2) a **RunSession scope** — the beacon survival-window's departure handling, evaluated in the BC4 Heartbeat handler via the per-tick `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot (C.10) to fire wipe-defeat / resolve the window tie-break (C.9). **The dispatch mechanism is a single seam `_enqueueDeparture(playerId, reason)`** (round-7 — the round-6 panel flagged the dual-scope dispatch as "intent without mechanism," dispatchable by an implementer as a single-consumer queue that silently drops one scope). Both event connections call **only** `_enqueueDeparture`; that one function **fans the departure out to every active scope** — it appends the record to the active `CraftSession._pendingDepartures` queue **if** the player is in that session's `crafters` (for **any** crafter-departure reason — `death`, `disconnect`, `player-initiated` cancel, or out-of-**bench**-range), AND appends it to `RunSession._pendingDepartures` (the run/alive scope) **only when the reason is an actual run-departure — `reason ∈ {"death", "disconnect"}` — and a run is active** (round-11 Convergence #1 + build-gate C3 fix). **Reason-gating the alive scope is load-bearing:** a pre-BC4 death/disconnect must drain `RunSession.aliveMembers` (or the first BC4 snapshot counts a dead player), but a `RequestCraftCancel` (`"player-initiated"`) or an out-of-**bench**-range departure leaves the player **alive in the run** and MUST NOT touch the alive-set — otherwise a benign cancel during BC4 would spuriously empty `aliveMembers` and fire a false wipe. **Only `death`/`disconnect` remove a member from `aliveMembers`; bench-only reasons affect only the bench scope.** The idempotent drain makes whole-run enqueuing of death/disconnect safe. It is a fan-out, not a hand-off: there is no shared single consumer that one scope could empty before the other sees it; each scope owns its own named queue (C.10) and its own drain. **De-dup (round-9, Blocker #6):** each append is **idempotent per `{playerId, scope}`** — if an entry for this player already sits in that scope's queue this tick, the append is a no-op. This closes the `RequestCraftCancel` double-enqueue: two cancels in one Heartbeat both pass the in-handler validation (the caller is still a registered crafter at validation time, before the drain), but only the first reaches the queue, so the bench drain cannot remove the same player twice or double-drain an already-cleared session (H.104). **Both drains mutate the alive-set:** the RunSession-scope drain removes each departed player from `RunSession.aliveMembers` (C.10) — that removal is the *only* way a member leaves `aliveMembers`, which is why the `windowAliveInRadius` alive-predicate is decoupled from character teardown (C.9). The two drains are independent (a player can be both a crafter and a window participant — both must observe the departure). **Single Heartbeat connection (build-gate O2 fix; reordered round-13 for Conv-3/Conv-4):** there is exactly **one** `RunService.Heartbeat` connection bound at RunStarted (the C.16 `_step(dt)` seam); all per-tick work is its straight-line body — there is no second connection. The body runs, in this fixed order: **(1) top terminal guard (Conv-3 round-13)** — `if RunSession.runOutcomeResolved then return` as the **very first statement of `_step`**, so once any terminal outcome (or a `RunEnded` from any source, C.10) has resolved the run, NO further `_step` work runs — not the BC4 segment, not the bench tick, not the alive drain (this is the H.113 "ceases ALL `_step` run-scope work on `RunEnded`" guarantee, now reconciled with C.9: the guard is `_step`-top, not merely the first statement of the BC4 segment — closing the round-12 Conv-3 contradiction); **(2) window/alive segment** — **if in BC4** (`beaconActivated and not beaconWindowSurvived`), run the BC4 segment (C.9): snapshot → victory/line-break evaluation (may set `runOutcomeResolved`) → run-scope departure drain **then re-add drain** (step c — round-13: departures removed, then `_pendingReadds` re-added, then the wipe check on the post-both-drains alive-set) → hold-state (step d); **else (not in BC4)**, run the lightweight run-scope alive maintenance — the `_pendingDepartures` drain **then the `_pendingReadds` re-add drain** (round-13) — to keep the set current across the whole run (round-11 Convergence #1; re-add buffering round-13); **(3) post-segment guard (Conv-4 round-13 — outcome-first)** — `if RunSession.runOutcomeResolved then return`, so a terminal outcome the BC4 segment resolved **this tick** suppresses the same-tick bench BT3 completion (a non-Beacon craft that would have completed the exact tick the run ends does not — no item, no Craft emission, no `OnCraftCompleted`-vs-terminal race; the item is moot, the run is ending); **(4) bench progress-tick + completion check, then bench departure drain (C.8)** — the bench tie-break (completion-first, E.29/H.81) is internal to this step and unaffected by its new position after the window segment. **The run-scope alive drain therefore runs EXACTLY ONCE per tick (build-gate O3/A1 fix): outside BC4 at (2)'s else-branch; during BC4 at the BC4 segment's step (c), AFTER the `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot — NEVER both, and NEVER before the snapshot on a BC4 tick.** This is load-bearing for the E.25 tie-break: a same-tick death must not empty `aliveMembers` before the survival check, or it would flip a deserved victory to a wipe (the opposite of "favors the squad") — building the snapshot before step (c)'s drain guarantees snapshot-before-drain. *(Round-13 reorder: the window/alive segment now runs BEFORE the bench tick — the round-9 order had the bench tick at (1) and the BC4 segment at (3), which left the round-12 Conv-4 "BT3 on the terminal tick" question undefined; outcome-first resolves it.)* Each scope's "completion/survival first, drain second" ordering is local to its own handler segment. Verified by H.101 (a single departure event updates BOTH scopes). The seam is test-injectable (C.16) — a test drives `_enqueueDeparture` directly to model a same-tick departure into both scopes.
- Disconnect mid-craft: `Players.PlayerRemoving` enqueues `{player, "disconnect"}`. On the next `_step` drain the player is removed from `CraftSession.crafters`; BT4 fires (others remain) or BT5 (crafter alone). **Progress is not preserved** across the disconnect — the player rejoining cannot resume the craft.
- Death mid-craft: `Humanoid.Died` enqueues `{player, "death"}`. Same drain path — removes player from session; BT4 or BT5. The dead player's existing crafted items in the squad shared pool are unaffected. Their personal raw-material inventory is locked until respawn (per C.3.6 dead-player-locked rule).

**Concurrency / tie-break (BT3 completion vs. same-Heartbeat departure — round-3, re-specified round-5 R4-5):** the per-`RunService.Heartbeat` progress closed form (D.1) and the cancel/death/disconnect/range-violation paths can come due on the same Heartbeat (e.g. the sole crafter's `craftProgress` crosses 1.0 on the exact tick they fire `RequestCraftCancel`, die, **disconnect**, or the 5 Hz proximity watcher flags them out-of-range). **Tie-break rule: within the single `CraftingService` Heartbeat handler, the progress closed form + completion check is evaluated FIRST; the buffered-departure drain runs SECOND.** If the progress check brings `craftProgress >= 1.0`, BT3 fires and the craft completes (item granted, emission fired, session cleared) — and the subsequent same-tick drain finds no session for that bench and is a no-op. This favors the squad (they keep the item they earned), consistent with the C.9 window tie-break and E.9. The one exception is BT6 (insufficient-materials at the atomic deduction step *inside* BT3) — not a tie-break but a failure of completion itself, rolled back per BT6.

**Why buffering, not connection-priority (round-5 R4-5 — the round-4 fix):** round-4 flagged that the round-3 wording ("whichever the scheduler orders first wins; connect the progress-tick at a priority that realizes completion-first") is **not implementable on Roblox** — there is no `RunService.Heartbeat` connection-priority API, and `Humanoid.Died` / `Players.PlayerRemoving` fire on their own event timing, not under the progress-tick's control, so the "intended order" could not actually be guaranteed in production (only in the unit tests via C.16). Buffering removes the dependency on callback ordering entirely: because *all* departures are queued and drained **inside the one Heartbeat handler, after the completion check**, the completion-first order is a property of the handler's own straight-line code, not of which Roblox callback the engine happened to invoke first. The round-4 gap — disconnect (`Players.PlayerRemoving`) at the boundary was uncovered, only `Humanoid.Died` was discussed — is closed by routing **both** departure signals through the same buffer. See E.29 + H.81.

#### C.9 — Beacon State Machine

The Beacon's lifecycle from recipe-completion through emission decay. Single-instance per `RunSession` (once-per-run constraint enforced via `RunSession.beaconCrafted`).

| State ID | Name | Description |
|---|---|---|
| BC1 | Beacon-Unbuilt | Recipe not yet completed. No `Beacon` item in squad shared pool. `RunSession.beaconCrafted == false`. Initial state at run start. |
| BC2 | Beacon-InInventory | `Beacon` item exists in `SquadInventory` (`count = 1`). Not yet placed in world. `RunSession.beaconCrafted == true`; `RunSession.beaconPlaced == false`. |
| BC3 | Beacon-Placed-Inert | Beacon BasePart spawned at `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition`. Not emitting disturbance yet. `RunSession.beaconPlaced == true`; `RunSession.beaconActivated == false`. |
| BC4 | Beacon-Active-Window | `DisturbanceService:Emit` has been called; Beacon source is live in ED's source list, decaying per ED.D.1. **The survival window (C.5.6a) is counting down. T8 has NOT fired yet.** `RunSession.beaconActivated == true`; `RunSession.beaconWindowSurvived == false`. |
| BC5 | Beacon-Survived | Survival window elapsed with the hold intact — **at least `requiredHolders = ceil(#aliveMembers/2)` squad members alive AND within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs`** of the beacon (round-15 SENTRY fix; `requiredHolders=1` when one member is alive, preserving the lone-survivor win) — the hold having been maintained **continuously** through the window (B1 round-3; continuous-hold round-13; holder-threshold round-15 — a mid-window drop below `requiredHolders` would have fired BCT-DEFEAT line-break first, see BCT4). **T8 (squad escape → victory) fires on entry.** Run ends in victory; the Beacon BasePart is removed via the run-end cleanup path. `RunSession.beaconWindowSurvived == true`. Terminal (victory). |
| BC6 | Beacon-Decayed | Natural source-expiry in ED (`m(t, age) <= MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02`). BasePart removed from world. Terminal. **Defensive fallback — normally unreachable** (the run ends at window-end ~49 s, ~10× before the ~501 s natural decay). |

**Transitions:**

| ID | Trigger | From | To | Side Effects |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BCT1 | Beacon recipe craft-completion (BT3 with `recipeId == "Beacon"`) | BC1 | BC2 | Server (atomic): set `RunSession.beaconCrafted = true`; set `RunSession.beaconCrafterId = CraftSession.primaryCrafterId` (the BT1 initiator, stable across any BT4 departures — round-2 B7); add `Beacon` item with `count = 1` to `SquadInventory`; fire `OnInventoryChanged` to all squad clients |
| BCT2 | Any alive squad member completes a **tap-hold** (`TAP_HOLD_COMMIT_DURATION = 0.5 s`, B6) and the client fires `RequestBeaconPlace(position)` while `Beacon` in `SquadInventory.count >= 1`; server validates (alive; `RunSession.beaconPlaced == false`; client `position` **clamped to** `BEACON_PLACE_MAX_RANGE = 8 studs` of player's server-tracked position per B13; **LOS reachability raycast** player→clamped candidate unobstructed per C.5.5 / B4; raycast-to-floor from the clamped candidate succeeds; no overlap with existing geometry / other beacons) | BC2 | BC3 | Server: spawn Beacon BasePart at the validated (clamped) position; remove `Beacon` from `SquadInventory`; set `RunSession.beaconPlaced = true`; set `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition = position` (the clamped, validated position — not the raw client value); set `RunSession.beaconPlacerId = playerId`; fire `OnBeaconPlaced(position, placerPlayerId)` to squad |
| BCT3 | Any alive squad member completes a **tap-hold** (`TAP_HOLD_COMMIT_DURATION = 0.5 s`, B6) and the client fires `RequestBeaconActivate()` while within `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 4 studs` of `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition`; server validates (alive; `RunSession.beaconActivated == false`; range check) | BC3 | BC4 | Server (in this order, no yield between steps per H.80): (1) set `RunSession.beaconActivated = true`; (2) set `RunSession.beaconActivationTime = _clock()` (R4-4 — the injected clock seam, production default `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`; handler bodies do NOT read the global directly, per C.16); (3) call `DisturbanceService:Emit("Beacon", RunSession.beaconWorldPosition, 0.95, RunSession.beaconPlacerId)` — uses placer's ID, not activator's, per ED.C.3.6 attribution semantics; (4) fire `OnBeaconActivated(position, activatorPlayerId, timestamp, windowDurationSeconds)` to squad — **the full four-field payload (Conv-2 round-13): `timestamp = _clock()` and `windowDurationSeconds = BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` (the server-derived window length, build-gate O5); the client needs both to render the countdown + the surface-13 escalation locally**; (5) fire `OnEscapeBeaconActivated(activatorPlayerId, timestamp, windowDurationSeconds)` to squad — the window-**start** signal (HUD/audio), carrying the **same `timestamp` + `windowDurationSeconds`** (Conv-2 round-13 — previously truncated to `(activatorPlayerId, timestamp)`, which left the client unable to derive the window length), **NOT** the victory trigger; (6) initialise the window's per-tick state: `RunSession._windowPrevInRadius = {}` (round-9 — empty so the first BC4 tick fires one `inRadius=true` per member already in radius at activation), `RunSession._lineBreakSince = nil` (round-13 — no line-break in progress at activation), `RunSession._lineBreakRestoreTicks = 0` and `RunSession._lineBreakBroadcast = false` (round-15/16 — HUD presenting HOLDING at activation), **and `RunSession.requiredHoldersBaseline = math.ceil(RunSession.peakAliveCount / 2)`** (round-16 R16-1 FREEZE + round-19 R18-1 RATCHET — the holder threshold FROZEN at activation but computed from the run's **high-water alive count** `peakAliveCount` (the max-alive-so-far ratchet, C.10), **NOT** the activation-snapshot `#aliveMembers`; computed once here, never recomputed per tick; the per-tick `requiredHolders = min(requiredHoldersBaseline, #aliveMembers)`, C.10. **Reading `peakAliveCount` rather than the live activation count closes the round-18 "activate-late-for-low-baseline" lane** — delaying activation until teammates die cannot lower the baseline, because the run-scope drain already ratcheted `peakAliveCount` to the larger squad. `peakAliveCount` is current here (ratcheted every tick since RunStarted); entirely server-derived, no client input. The first `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot still reads the **pre-drain `aliveMembers` as it stands at activation** — `RequestBeaconActivate` is handled in Luau's single-threaded scheduler and the BC4 segment's step-(c) drain has not yet run this tick); then begin the per-`RunService.Heartbeat` survival-window evaluation (`BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW`, C.5.6a — Heartbeat-polled continuous-hold, not `task.delay`, per B5); (7) start the defensive decay-watch that polls ED for source expiry. **T8 does NOT fire here.** |
| BCT4 | Survival-window timer elapses (`now - RunSession.beaconActivationTime >= BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW`, evaluated per-Heartbeat per B5) **AND the per-tick `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot (C.10, round-7) meets the holder threshold (`#windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders`, round-15)** — i.e. at least `requiredHolders = ceil(#aliveMembers/2)` squad members are alive AND within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs` of `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition` at the top of this tick (B1 round-3; **round-13/15: the hold is maintained continuously, so reaching this tick at-or-above threshold means the squad held unbroken through the whole window — a mid-window drop below `requiredHolders` would have fired BCT-DEFEAT line-break first, or be inside its grace**) | BC4 | BC5 | Server: set `RunSession.beaconWindowSurvived = true`; fire `OnBeaconWindowSurvived(timestamp)` to squad → drives Player Controller's T8 (squad escape → victory); run ends in victory; Beacon BasePart removed via the run-end cleanup path; clear the survival-window + decay-watch timers. **This is the victory transition** (it replaces the pre-round-2 "T8 at BCT3" behavior). The proximity test reads the **single per-tick `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot** (C.10) — the one source of truth for both this check and the tie-break; it is NOT a second, independent live read (round-7 A-1/A-2 fix). |
| BCT-DEFEAT | Either: **(wipe)** on a BC4 Heartbeat `RunSession.aliveMembers` is empty after the step-(c) drains (`beaconActivated == true && beaconWindowSurvived == false` and no squad member remains alive — every member departed via `Humanoid.Died` **or `Players.PlayerRemoving`** clean disconnect, round-5 R4-5, all buffered + drained in the BC4 handler; with `aliveMembers` empty the snapshot is trivially below threshold) — fires **immediately, no grace** (no one is left to regroup), and **takes precedence over a same-tick scatter** (B-1 round-15); **or (scatter / line-break — B1 round-13 fail-fast; holder-threshold round-15)** the `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot is **below `requiredHolders`** while members remain alive (fewer than `requiredHolders = ceil(#aliveMembers/2)` within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs`) and **stays below threshold for a continuous `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE ≈ 3 s`** (G.6) without enough alive members re-entering the radius (tracked by the `_lineBreakSince` grace clock, C.10), **AND `aliveMembers` is still non-empty after the step-(c) drains** (else it is a wipe). A break that re-establishes (`>= requiredHolders`) within the grace does NOT fire BCT-DEFEAT — the grace clock resets and the run continues. *(Round-13: the scatter path is a CONTINUOUS line-break that can fire at any tick during the window, not only the window-end proximity check it was in round-3/9 — closing the KITING line.
