Round-15: "below threshold" generalises "empty", closing the SENTRY line.)* | BC4 | `RunEnded(defeat)` | Server (guarded by the `runOutcomeResolved` idempotent latch — round-9, so this body runs **at most once** even if another terminal path fires the same/adjacent tick): set `RunSession.runOutcomeResolved = true`; remove Beacon BasePart via the run-end cleanup path; clear the survival-window + decay-watch timers; **`OnBeaconWindowSurvived` does NOT fire; `OnBeaconDecayed` does NOT fire** (consistent with E.8 run-end cleanup vs. natural decay). **Single decision point (round-9, Blocker #5 — replaces the round-3 split-ownership):** during BC4 the one CraftingService Heartbeat handler owns **all three** window outcomes and fires **exactly one** at the deciding tick — `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` (victory, window elapsed with `#windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders`), `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="scatter")` (**round-13/15: the `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot stayed BELOW `requiredHolders` — members alive, fewer than `requiredHolders` in `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` — for the full `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE`; a continuous line-break, not the round-3 window-end check**), or `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")` (`RunSession.aliveMembers` is empty after the step-(c) drains — the buffered-departure drain emptied the alive-set; fires immediately, no grace, and wins a same-tick tie vs scatter — B-1 round-15). PC subscribes to `OnBeaconWindowFailed` and maps **both** reasons to `RunEnded(defeat)`. PC's generic all-dead `RunEnded(defeat)` path remains authoritative **only outside BC4** (the normal hunt); **during BC4, PC MUST suppress its all-dead path entirely** and defer the wipe to Crafting's `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")` (Crafting's buffered drain holds the deterministic in-window alive-set — the sole wipe authority during the window). **Mutual-exclusion protocol (round-11 Convergence #2 — the explicit guard the round-10 panel required, network B-NET-1 / gameplay BLOCK-2):** the `runOutcomeResolved` latch *alone* is INSUFFICIENT, because PC's all-dead `RunEnded(defeat)` can fire **synchronously from `Humanoid.Died`** BEFORE Crafting's Heartbeat drain sets the latch (the latch is written during the BC4 tick drain, *after* the synchronous death callback) — a same-frame double-cleanup. So the **primary** guard is PC's **beacon-state suppression**: PC gates its all-dead path on `RunSession.beaconActivated == true` **alone** (build-gate A4 fix — the whole post-activation lifecycle, **dropping the prior `&& !beaconWindowSurvived` clause**, which *released* suppression at the victory write and opened a post-victory defeat-race: a second member dying later in the victory frame could observe `beaconWindowSurvived == true` and let PC fire all-dead defeat *after* Crafting already fired victory). Once the beacon is activated, **every** terminal outcome flows through a Crafting signal — `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`→victory or `OnBeaconWindowFailed`→defeat — so PC never needs its all-dead path during the entire post-activation lifecycle; it suppresses from BCT3 until `RunEnded`. `beaconActivated` is set at BCT3, well before any BC4 death, so the read is race-free. The `runOutcomeResolved` latch is the **defense-in-depth second line** (it absorbs the adjacent-tick edge and any non-PC terminal path). Both together make in-window run-end cleanup fire **exactly once** (H.109). This gives the window a single owner — removing the round-8 "two firing paths, no mutual-exclusion → double-cleanup" defect (round-3 had assigned wipe to PC's path and scatter to Crafting, the split that produced the race). |
| BCT5 | Defensive decay-watch detects ED source expiry (`m(t, age) <= MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`); ~501 s after activation per ED.D.1 example | BC4 | BC6 | Server: **set `RunSession.runOutcomeResolved = true`** (round-9 gate-fix — BCT5 is a BC4 exit and must latch like the others, or the handler could re-enter); remove Beacon BasePart from workspace if still present; fire `OnBeaconDecayed()` to squad; clear the decay-watch timer **and `_windowPrevInRadius` + `_lineBreakSince` (both set nil — round-13)**. **Defensive fallback only** — fires ONLY from BC4 (the active window). BC5 is terminal-victory and BC6 is terminal-decay; neither is a valid `From` state (a transition out of BC5 would fire `OnBeaconDecayed` after victory, which E.26 forbids). Normally unreachable — the run ends at BCT4 victory or BCT-DEFEAT ~10× sooner. Was `BCT4` pre-round-2. |

**Concurrency / once-per-run guarantees**:
- BCT1 cannot fire twice in the same run because BT1's pre-validation checks `RunSession.beaconCrafted == false` for the Beacon recipe; the second `RequestCraft("Beacon")` is rejected before BT1 transitions. Same-Heartbeat double-fire is prevented by Roblox's single-threaded Luau scheduler — the second request is sequenced after the first sets the flag.
- BCT2 cannot fire twice because the `Beacon` item is removed from inventory at BCT2; subsequent `RequestBeaconPlace` calls find no item and reject.
- BCT3 cannot fire twice because of the `RunSession.beaconActivated == false` guard. Even a same-Heartbeat double-fire is sequenced by Luau's single-threaded execution; the second receives `BeaconRejected(reason="already-activated")`.
- BCT4 (window survived) fires at most once because it sets `RunSession.beaconWindowSurvived = true` and ends the run; the survival-window timer is cleared on the same tick, so a second elapse cannot fire.
- **BCT4 vs. BCT-DEFEAT are mutually exclusive and exhaustive over BC4's exit** (other than the defensive BCT5 fallback). Deaths (`Humanoid.Died`) and disconnects (`Players.PlayerRemoving`) during BC4 are **buffered** into `RunSession._pendingDepartures` via the same `_enqueueDeparture` fan-out seam as the bench tie-break (C.8, R4-5; the seam appends to both scope queues — C.10) — they do **not** mutate run/alive state from inside the event callback. On each `RunService.Heartbeat` while in BC4 the **single CraftingService Heartbeat handler** runs — and, per the `_step` ordering (C.8/C.10, **Conv-3 round-13**), the `if RunSession.runOutcomeResolved then return` guard sits at the **very top of `_step`** (above the bench progress tick), so a resolved run does no further work of any kind; the BC4 handler re-checks the same latch on entry. The BC4 handler then **builds the per-tick `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot** (C.10) — the set of members who are in `RunSession.aliveMembers` (the server-authoritative alive-set — round-9, Blocker #3) AND within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` of `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition`, computed ONCE at the top of the tick, BEFORE draining this tick's buffered departures — then evaluates, in this fixed straight-line **continuous-hold** order (round-13; a local `graceElapsed = false` is initialised **before** the (a)/(b) branch so step (c) can read it on either path — round-15 B-1):
  - **(a) hold maintained** — if `#windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders` (the holder threshold — `requiredHolders = min(requiredHoldersBaseline, #aliveMembers)`, round-16 R16-1: the baseline is the frozen activation value, this tick only clamps it down to the current alive count, C.10), the hold is intact: clear the grace clock (`RunSession._lineBreakSince = nil`), then check **window-survival** — has the window elapsed (`max(0, _clock() - beaconActivationTime) >= BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW`, round-9 monotonic-clock guard)? If so → BCT4 victory (set `runOutcomeResolved`, fire `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`). If the window has not yet elapsed, the run continues (the squad is holding).
  - **(b) hold broken — COMPUTE-ONLY (round-15 B-1)** — if `#windowAliveInRadius < requiredHolders`, the line is broken: start the grace clock if not already running (`if _lineBreakSince == nil then _lineBreakSince = _clock()`), then **compute** `graceElapsed = (_clock() - _lineBreakSince >= BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE)` into a local flag — **but do NOT fire the scatter terminal here.** The scatter terminal is fired in step (c), AFTER the drains, gated on `aliveMembers` still non-empty, so a same-tick wipe takes precedence (this fixes the round-13 inversion where step (b) latched scatter before the wipe drain — B-1). Otherwise the run continues **inside the grace** — the HUD shows BROKEN + a regroup countdown (step d), and a later tick returning to `>= requiredHolders` re-takes path (a), clearing the grace. (A window-elapse that lands while the line is broken is **not** a victory — path (a) requires `>= requiredHolders` — so a scattered squad must re-establish the hold within grace to win; the grace is ≤ ~3 s and cannot recover the KITING/SENTRY scatter line.)
  - **(c) drain `RunSession._pendingDepartures`** (removing each from `aliveMembers`), **then drain `RunSession._pendingReadds`** (re-adding each respawned member — round-13 build-gate fix) — together these maintain the alive-set (and thus `requiredHolders`) the **next** tick's snapshot reads. **Terminal resolution order (round-15 B-1 — wipe before scatter):** if `aliveMembers` is empty **after both drains** → **wipe defeat, immediately, no grace** → BCT-DEFEAT (set `runOutcomeResolved`, fire `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")`); **else, if step (b) computed `graceElapsed` AND `aliveMembers` is non-empty** → **scatter / line-break defeat** (set `runOutcomeResolved`, fire `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="scatter")`). **Wipe is checked first**, so a same-tick everyone-dies-mid-grace resolves as a **wipe**, not a scatter (B-1 — H.115). **Draining re-adds AFTER departures means a same-tick death+respawn nets to alive and does NOT trip a false wipe.**
  - **(d) hold-state crossing detection + squad cue** (presentation-only, round-9; hysteresis-banded round-11; debounced round-15) — **begins with its own in-step guard `if RunSession.runOutcomeResolved then return`, so it is skipped entirely when a terminal resolved earlier this tick** (N14-B1: no `OnBeaconLineBreak` or `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged` fires on a terminal/wipe/scatter tick). Otherwise: update the presented per-member in-radius set with the `BEACON_HOLD_HYSTERESIS` Schmitt-trigger band (enter at `<= R`, leave only at `> R + HYSTERESIS`), fire `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged` only for members whose presented state changed vs `RunSession._windowPrevInRadius`, overwrite `_windowPrevInRadius` (C.10); **and push the squad-level HOLDING / BROKEN binary to surface 13 via the strict-threshold `OnBeaconLineBreak`, edge-triggered off `RunSession._lineBreakBroadcast` (round-16 R16-2) with the round-15 restore debounce** — on a **below-threshold** tick (which has already reset `_lineBreakRestoreTicks` to 0): fire `broken=true` and set `_lineBreakBroadcast = true` **only if `_lineBreakBroadcast` was false** (the HOLDING→BROKEN edge — fire-once-per-episode; if already true, do NOT re-fire, the round-16 fix to the round-15 per-tick re-fire); on an **at-threshold** tick: increment `_lineBreakRestoreTicks` and, **only if `_lineBreakBroadcast` is true**, fire `broken=false` (**with `graceSeconds = 0` in the payload — round-19 R18-4**) + set `_lineBreakBroadcast = false` on the single tick `_lineBreakRestoreTicks` becomes **exactly equal to `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_RESTORE_DEBOUNCE`** (NOT `>=`, and the counter is NOT reset on fire — so it never re-fires while the hold persists; it returns to 0 only on the next below-threshold tick) — carrying a **colorblind shape/icon** redundancy (closed-ring vs broken-ring glyph + grace countdown), not color alone (ux F3). The win checks (a)/(b)/(c) use the strict threshold + the strict `_lineBreakSince` grace — both untouched by the hysteresis band and the restore debounce (presentation-only).
Steps (a)–(c) decide the outcome; (d) only feeds the HUD and never alters it. **Both the victory and the line-break checks read the same `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot — exactly one source of truth, taken before the drain** (round-7 A-1/A-2 fix: the round-6 panel found C.9 read a *live* `HumanoidRootPart.Position` while the tie-break read a *buffered* set — two sources of truth; this collapses them into the one per-tick snapshot). **Tie-break (a death/disconnect on the exact Heartbeat the window elapses): window-survival (a) is evaluated FIRST, against the pre-drain `windowAliveInRadius`** — a squad that held to the window boundary escapes even if a member dies or disconnects on that same tick (favors the squad, consistent with E.9). A member whose character is being destroyed in `Players.PlayerRemoving` is excluded from `windowAliveInRadius` only via their buffered departure (drained at step c), never via a racing live read that could observe a half-destroyed `HumanoidRootPart` — the snapshot reads `_memberPosition` (C.16) exactly once per member per tick, guarded by the alive check. **Why this is deterministic in production (round-5 R4-5):** the window comparison, the grace check, and the departure drain are ordered statements inside *one* Heartbeat handler — not independent Roblox callbacks racing on connection order — so the ordering is a property of the handler's own code, not scheduler luck. Roblox exposes no Heartbeat connection-priority API, so the round-3/round-4 "whichever the scheduler orders first wins" framing was not actually implementable; buffering removes the dependency. **Round-13 (continuous-hold) / round-15 (holder threshold):** the proximity clause is now evaluated on **every** BC4 Heartbeat against the holder threshold — a member leaving `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` such that the in-radius count **drops below `requiredHolders`** starts the line-break grace immediately, and only enough members re-entering (back to `>= requiredHolders`) within the grace keeps the run alive. The round-3/round-9 "members are free to leave and re-enter mid-window; only their status at window-end decides" rule is **retired** (it was the KITING exploit's enabling condition); round-15's `requiredHolders` additionally retires the **SENTRY** line (one member could satisfy the round-13 `>= 1` rule for the whole squad). **Outcome-first within `_step` (Conv-4 round-13):** because the BC4 handler runs **before** the bench progress tick in `_step`, a terminal outcome resolved on a given tick (victory or line-break) sets `runOutcomeResolved`, and the top-of-`_step` guard then **suppresses the same-tick bench BT3 completion** — a non-Beacon craft that would have completed the exact tick the run ends does not complete (no item granted, no Craft emission, no `OnCraftCompleted`-vs-terminal-signal race); the item is moot because the run is ending.
- The placing player may die between BCT2 and BCT3 — see C.5.4 cross-player rescue rule. The `RunSession.beaconPlacerId` field is preserved; activation by a different alive player still uses the placer's ID for `Emit` attribution.

**Beacon BasePart cleanup edge cases**:
- Run ends before BCT3 (squad wipes at BC3, beacon placed but never activated): the `RunEnded` event triggers cleanup of all RunSession-scoped state; the Beacon BasePart is removed without firing BCT4, BCT-DEFEAT, or BCT5 (see E.8 — `OnBeaconDecayed` is suppressed for run-end cleanup).
- **Full-squad wipe during the survival window (BC4)**: this is BCT-DEFEAT, a gameplay outcome (defeat), not a cleanup edge case — the beacon BasePart is removed via the run-end path; `OnBeaconDecayed` is suppressed.
- Server crash between BCT3 (activation) and BCT4 (window survived): ED's source list and the survival-window timer are purely runtime; both are lost on server restart. No persistence concern (Pillar 4) — there is no in-run save, so a crashed run does not resume mid-window.
- **Graceful server shutdown (`BindToClose`) during BC4 (round-3)**: a `game:BindToClose` (Studio stop, server migration, scheduled shutdown) firing while the beacon is active terminates the run as forced cleanup — identical to the run-end cleanup path of E.8: the Beacon BasePart is torn down, **`OnBeaconWindowSurvived` does NOT fire and `OnBeaconDecayed` does NOT fire**, and no victory/defeat result is awarded (the run simply ends — there is no in-run save to resume, per Pillar 4). The `BindToClose` handler must not block on the survival-window timer; it returns immediately after clearing RunSession-scoped state. This applies uniformly across BC2/BC3/BC4 — a `RunEnded` from shutdown in any beacon state uses the E.8 cleanup path, never a BCT4/BCT-DEFEAT/BCT5 transition.

#### C.10 — Server-Side Session Data Model

Three server-side data structures govern Crafting & Items state. All are purely runtime — never written to DataStore.

**RunSession** (one per active server, lifecycle = `RunStarted` to `RunEnded`):

```
RunSession = {
    runId:              string,    -- unique session identifier
    runStartTime:       number,    -- _clock() at session start (production default workspace:GetServerTimeNow() per C.16)
    beaconCrafted:      boolean,   -- true once BCT1 fires; once-per-run guard
    beaconPlaced:       boolean,   -- true once BCT2 fires
    beaconActivated:    boolean,   -- true once BCT3 fires; guards against duplicate Emit; also marks the start of the survival window
    beaconWindowSurvived: boolean, -- true once BCT4 fires (window elapsed with #windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders, round-15); the victory guard
    beaconActivationTime: number?, -- _clock() at BCT3 (production default workspace:GetServerTimeNow()); nil before; window elapses at beaconActivationTime + BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW. INVARIANT (round-3): beaconActivationTime != nil iff beaconActivated == true — they are set together at BCT3 step 1+2 and never independently; any code reading beaconActivationTime must guard on beaconActivated, never the reverse (verified by H.91)
    beaconWorldPosition: Vector3?, -- set at BCT2; nil before. The BCT4 proximity test (B1) and the tie-break both read the single per-tick windowAliveInRadius snapshot (below), which is derived from this + each alive member's HumanoidRootPart.Position; no per-player distance is stored on RunSession
    beaconCrafterId:    number?,   -- UserId; set at BCT1 from CraftSession.primaryCrafterId. ANALYTICS / AUDIT ONLY — written at BCT1, not read by any gameplay path (attribution for the Beacon's Emit uses beaconPlacerId, not this). Retained for the post-run disturbance/credit log; round-4 flagged it write-never-read-by-logic, which is intended
    beaconPlacerId:     number?,   -- UserId; set at BCT2; passed as sourcePlayerId in BCT3 Emit call
    benchMaxCraftersEffective: number, -- (round-7 A-4) int; the squad-scaled per-bench crafter cap = min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(squadSize/2)) (C.3.5). COMPUTED ONCE at RunStarted from the starting squad size and STORED here; read by BT2's entry guard (C.8) and by D.2's crafterSpeedMultiplier. NEVER recomputed on death/disconnect — the cap does not shrink mid-run (C.3.5). Round-6 flagged it referenced by BT2/D.2 with no storage field; this is its home
    aliveMembers:       { [number]: true }, -- (round-9, Blocker #3 / Q2) SERVER-AUTHORITATIVE ALIVE-SET: the set of squad-member UserIds currently alive, as a UserId→true lookup. INITIALISED at RunStarted to the full starting squad. The ONLY mutators are (a) the buffered _enqueueDeparture drain — a Humanoid.Died or Players.PlayerRemoving removes the member at the bench/RunSession drain step (C.8/C.9), **run from RunStarted on every Heartbeat** (round-11 Convergence #1), so a pre-activation death is reflected before BC4 begins; and (b) respawn re-add. **Round-11 (Convergence #1) — respawn re-add is now LOAD-BEARING, so PC's `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged(playerId, isAlive)` is upgraded to a BLOCKING forward obligation (F.4 PC row + C.12):** because the drain now removes a member on a *pre-BC4* death (not only an in-window one), a member who died before BC4 and **respawned** before window-end MUST be re-added to `aliveMembers` or they are wrongly excluded from the victory snapshot despite being alive-and-in-radius. Under the round-9 BC4-only drain this could not arise (a pre-BC4 death never left the set), so the signal was non-blocking; the whole-run drain makes it blocking. **Interim until PC publishes it:** Crafting re-adds a member to `aliveMembers` on that player's own `Player.CharacterAdded` + `Humanoid` ready (a self-sourced fallback Crafting can wire without PC), **superseded by PC's canonical `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged` when it lands** (PC owns the authoritative alive/respawn lifecycle, PC.S4); the self-sourced path is the interim so the system is correct in isolation while PC is in MAJOR REVISION (H.108). **The re-add is BUFFERED and drained in `_step`, not a direct callback write (round-13, build-gate fix):** `CharacterAdded` (or PC's `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged`) does NOT mutate `aliveMembers` directly from the event callback — it calls `_enqueueReadd(playerId)`, which appends the UserId to `RunSession._pendingReadds` (a re-add queue, schema below). That queue is **drained inside `_step` immediately AFTER the `_pendingDepartures` drain** (C.8 `_step` ordering), so adds and removes are ordered statements in **one** handler — eliminating the round-13-flagged asymmetry where a direct callback write raced the buffered departure drain (the same R4-5 hazard departures already avoid). **Drain-after-departures ordering is load-bearing:** within any single tick a same-tick death+respawn nets to **alive** (the departure removes, then the re-add re-adds), and because `_pendingDepartures` is emptied every tick no stale departure can ever undo a re-add from a prior tick. **The re-add is idempotent (round-11 network gate, Finding 2a):** `aliveMembers[playerId] = true` when already `true` is a no-op, so a doubled or disconnect/reconnect-spammed `CharacterAdded` cannot manipulate the set (mirroring the `_enqueueDeparture` de-dup); and `CharacterAdded` fires server-side, so the re-add is never client-asserted. **Supersession switch (build-gate O4):** the interim `CharacterAdded` path is active only while PC has not yet published `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged`; on the first receipt of PC's canonical signal Crafting **disables the self-sourced path (a one-way capability latch)** and thereafter re-adds only via PC's signal — and because both writes are idempotent, a brief overlap during the switch is harmless. **Position-validity of the re-add (build-gate A3; concrete predicate round-15 B-3):** the re-add fires only **after the respawned character's `HumanoidRootPart` exists AND has been positioned** (post-spawn placement), NOT merely on `Humanoid` ready — this preserves the "member ∈ `aliveMembers` ⇒ its character is valid *and positioned* to read this tick" guarantee, so the `windowAliveInRadius` read never observes an unplaced root part at the spawn point. **Concrete observable predicate (round-15 — the round-14 B-3 fix: "positioned" was undefined):** the server-side `CharacterAdded` handler does `character:WaitForChild("HumanoidRootPart")`, then on each subsequent `_step` evaluates the **positioned predicate** `hrp ~= nil AND hrp.Position.Magnitude > POSITION_SETTLED_EPSILON` (G.6, default `1.0` stud — a freshly-parented-but-unplaced root part reads the world origin `(0,0,0)`; Roblox places characters at a `SpawnLocation` well away from origin, so a non-origin position is the observable signal that placement has completed), and calls `_enqueueReadd(playerId)` **once** on the first `_step` the predicate holds (then unsubscribes the per-step check for that respawn). This makes "positioned" a single, testable boolean (the C.16 `_setMemberPosition` seam drives it directly: a test sets the member's position to origin → predicate false → no re-add; sets it non-origin → predicate true → re-add enqueued). Edge: a legitimately-near-origin spawn (a map whose spawn sits at world origin) is out of scope — Terranova safe-room spawns are not at `(0,0,0)`; if a future map needs it, the predicate switches to a `Humanoid.FloorMaterial ~= Air`-settled check (noted for the placement ADR). A respawning member is simply **absent** from `aliveMembers` (and thus the snapshot) until fully placed, then re-enters cleanly. The departure-drain removal is always safe (idempotent); the re-add is what the round-11 widening makes mandatory. This is the CANONICAL definition of "alive" for windowAliveInRadius (C.9) and is DECOUPLED from character-instance teardown timing: no code path ever asks a possibly-half-destroyed Character/Humanoid whether it is alive. The HumanoidRootPart.Position read in the windowAliveInRadius comprehension is GUARDED by membership here (member ∈ aliveMembers ⇒ its character is valid to read this tick). Resolves the round-8 "alive-predicate undefined / two implementers write two race-different predicates" blocker
    _pendingDepartures: { { playerId: number, reason: string } }, -- (round-5 R4-5 buffer; NAMED round-9 Blocker #2; enqueue/drain widened to whole-run round-11 Convergence #1) the RunSession-scope departure queue. LIFECYCLE: created empty at RunStarted; APPENDED by the _enqueueDeparture fan-out seam (C.8) for a **death/disconnect departure during an active run** (round-11 + build-gate C3 — `reason ∈ {"death", "disconnect"}` ONLY; a `player-initiated` cancel or out-of-bench-range departure leaves the player alive and is appended to the bench scope only, NEVER here; NOT gated on "window active"; the prior round-9 `beaconActivated == true && beaconWindowSurvived == false` gate meant a pre-activation death/disconnect never drained `aliveMembers`, so the first BC4 `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot could count an already-dead player — the round-10 TRIPLE-lens defect, network B-NET-3 / gameplay BLOCK-1 / systems BLOCK-4). The drain's removal from `aliveMembers` is **idempotent** (removing a player already absent from the alive-set is a harmless no-op), so enqueuing across the whole run is safe. DRAINED inside the CraftingService Heartbeat handler **every tick**: OUTSIDE BC4 it runs as a lightweight run-scope drain that ONLY maintains `aliveMembers` (so the set is always current entering BC4 — closing the stale-snapshot defect); DURING BC4 it runs at step (c) — AFTER the `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot + the survival/scatter checks (C.9) — and additionally drives wipe-defeat. Each drained entry removes its player from `aliveMembers` and is then DISCARDED (the queue is emptied every tick). **After each tick's departure + re-add drain pair (both inside and outside BC4), the handler ratchets `peakAliveCount = math.max(peakAliveCount, #aliveMembers)` (round-19 R18-1), so the run's high-water alive count is always current before BCT3 reads it for `requiredHoldersBaseline`.** DE-DUP (round-9, Blocker #6): _enqueueDeparture is a no-op if an entry for this {playerId, RunSession-scope} is already pending this tick — two same-tick departure signals for one player cannot double-drain. Round-8 flagged this queue referenced ~6× across the C.8/C.9 tie-break spine but absent from every schema; this is its home
    _pendingReadds: { number }, -- (round-13, build-gate fix) the respawn RE-ADD queue (UserIds). LIFECYCLE: created empty at RunStarted; APPENDED by _enqueueReadd(playerId) — called from the server-side Player.CharacterAdded + positioned-HumanoidRootPart path (interim) or PC's OnSquadMemberAliveChanged (canonical) ONLY after the respawned character's HumanoidRootPart exists AND is positioned (build-gate A3 — so the queue never holds an unplaced member); DRAINED inside _step IMMEDIATELY AFTER the _pendingDepartures run-scope drain — each entry does aliveMembers[playerId] = true (idempotent) and is then discarded (queue emptied each tick). Buffering the re-add (rather than mutating aliveMembers directly from the CharacterAdded callback) makes adds and removes ordered statements in ONE handler, closing the round-13-flagged direct-write-vs-buffered-drain race; draining re-adds AFTER departures means a same-tick death+respawn nets to alive. De-dup: appending a playerId already pending this tick is a no-op (mirrors _enqueueDeparture). aliveMembers is empty at the end of every drain pair only if a true wipe occurred — a pending re-add drained the same tick keeps the member in the set
    _windowPrevInRadius: { [number]: true }?, -- (round-9, Blocker #1 / Q1) PRESENTATION-ONLY edge-detection state for OnBeaconHoldStateChanged. The set of member UserIds that were alive-and-in-radius as of the PREVIOUS BC4 tick. nil before BC4; set to {} at BCT3 (so the first BC4 tick fires inRadius=true once for members already in radius at activation); after firing this tick's boundary-cross signals it is OVERWRITTEN with this tick's windowAliveInRadius membership. NEVER read by the win-condition evaluation — BCT4 victory + scatter read ONLY the per-tick windowAliveInRadius snapshot (below). It exists solely so the hold-state HUD signal can be EDGE-TRIGGERED (set-difference vs this set) rather than flooding per-Heartbeat. Resolves the round-8 "crossing-detection needs cross-tick state C.10 forbids storing" contradiction: the WIN snapshot stays un-stored and per-tick; this PRESENTATION set is explicitly stored, named, and walled off from the win path
    _lineBreakSince: number?, -- (round-13, B1 fail-fast) LINE-BREAK GRACE CLOCK. nil while the hold is intact (`#windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders`) and before BC4; set to _clock() on the first BC4 tick the snapshot drops BELOW the holder threshold (`#windowAliveInRadius < requiredHolders`) while >=1 member is still alive (a scatter/displacement line-break, C.9 step b — round-15: "below threshold", generalising the round-13 "empty"); RESET to nil the moment the snapshot returns to `>= requiredHolders` (enough alive members re-enter BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS, step a). When _clock() - _lineBreakSince >= BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE (G.6, ~3 s) with the snapshot still below threshold, BCT-DEFEAT(reason="scatter") fires. The wipe path (aliveMembers emptied) fires immediately at the drain (step c), no grace — it does NOT wait on this clock. (Build-gate note, round-13 / round-15 B-1: if a tick's snapshot is below threshold AND that same tick's drain empties aliveMembers, step (b) — now **compute-only** (round-15 B-1) — may TRANSIENTLY stamp this field, but does NOT fire the scatter terminal itself; the scatter terminal is fired AFTER step (c) and ONLY if `aliveMembers` is still non-empty after both drains, so a same-tick wipe takes precedence. The stamp is never READ on a wipe tick, because the wipe terminal resolves the tick and sets runOutcomeResolved, and the field is cleared by run-end cleanup; so a wipe is never delayed by the grace, and the scatter terminal only ever fires while members remain alive by construction of step (b)/(c).) Initialised nil at BCT3 (alongside _windowPrevInRadius); cleared to nil on any BC4 exit. This is the field that converts the round-3/9 window-end-only proximity check into the round-13 continuous-hold-with-grace rule; like _windowPrevInRadius it is window-scoped state, but unlike it this one DOES feed the win/lose evaluation (it is the grace timer for the line-break terminal). Verified by H.114
    _lineBreakRestoreTicks: number, -- (round-15 — HUD restore debounce, quadruple-lens fix) PRESENTATION-ONLY counter of CONSECUTIVE BC4 ticks the strict holder-threshold test (#windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders) has held at-or-above threshold since the last below-threshold tick. Incremented each at-threshold tick; reset to 0 ONLY on a below-threshold tick (NOT on fire). Gates the HUD OnBeaconLineBreak(broken=false) restore edge together with _lineBreakBroadcast (below): the restore fires on the SINGLE tick this counter becomes EXACTLY EQUAL to BEACON_LINE_BREAK_RESTORE_DEBOUNCE (G.6, 6 ticks) AND _lineBreakBroadcast is currently true — not >=, and the counter is not zeroed on fire, so subsequent at-threshold ticks (counter > knob) do NOT re-fire. NEVER read by the win/lose evaluation — the terminal grace clock is _lineBreakSince, which is independent. Initialised 0 at BCT3; reset 0 on any BC4 exit. Window-scoped presentation state like _windowPrevInRadius (walled off from the win path). Verified by H.95/H.119
    _lineBreakBroadcast: boolean, -- (round-16 R16-2 — HUD broken=true edge-trigger; the missing half of the quadruple-lens debounce) PRESENTATION-ONLY: the last `broken` value PUSHED to surface 13 (false = currently presenting HOLDING; true = presenting BROKEN). The squad-level OnBeaconLineBreak(broken=true) fires ONLY on the HOLDING→BROKEN edge — i.e. on a below-threshold tick WHEN _lineBreakBroadcast == false — and sets it true; OnBeaconLineBreak(broken=false) fires only on the BROKEN→HOLDING edge (when _lineBreakRestoreTicks hits the debounce AND _lineBreakBroadcast == true) and sets it false. This is what makes broken=true fire AT MOST ONCE per line-break episode: a boundary-oscillating sole holder whose count keeps dipping below threshold does NOT re-fire broken=true (it is already true), so the win-state banner cannot flap at Heartbeat rate. Round-15 debounced only broken=false; round-16 added this field so broken=true is edge-triggered too (the round-16 systems-B2/qa-B1 AC-vs-spec contradiction — H.119/H.95 assert "at most one broken=true," which the un-edge-triggered round-15 spec could not deliver). Initialised false at BCT3; reset false on any BC4 exit. NEVER read by the win/lose evaluation. Verified by H.119/H.95
    peakAliveCount:     number,    -- (round-19 R18-1 — max-alive-so-far ratchet) the HIGH-WATER mark of #aliveMembers over the whole run, ratcheted from RunStarted. INITIALISED to #squadRoster at RunStarted (all members start alive, so this is the run-start alive count); after each _step departure/re-add drain pair, peakAliveCount = math.max(peakAliveCount, #aliveMembers). Under the current full-alive-start invariant (no late join; aliveMembers ⊆ the starting roster) it stays equal to #squadRoster — the ratchet is the GENERAL mechanism so the frozen holder baseline tracks the largest squad the run ever fielded even if a future design admits late-join or downed-at-start members; it is NOT dead code, it is the activation-timing-independent source for the baseline. READ ONLY at BCT3 step 6 to set requiredHoldersBaseline (it is never read by the per-tick win check directly). Run-scoped (not cleared at BC4 exit; reset at the next RunStarted)
    requiredHoldersBaseline: number?, -- (round-16 R16-1 — FROZEN holder-count baseline; round-19 R18-1 — computed from the ratchet, NOT the activation snapshot) the holder threshold COMPUTED ONCE at BCT3 from the run's high-water alive count: ceil(peakAliveCount / 2) evaluated at window-open, then held frozen for the whole window. **Round-19 R18-1:** the source is `peakAliveCount` (the max-alive-so-far ratchet), NOT `#aliveMembers` at activation — so **delaying activation until teammates die cannot lower the baseline** (the round-18 "activate-late-for-low-baseline" degenerate lane: a 4-player squad that waited for 2 deaths would, under the old activation-snapshot rule, lock baseline `ceil(2/2)=1`; under the ratchet the baseline stays `ceil(4/2)=2`). nil before BCT3; set at BCT3 step 6; cleared nil on any BC4 exit. The per-tick threshold is requiredHolders = min(requiredHoldersBaseline, #aliveMembers) (C.10 holder-threshold note / C.9) — the min clamps DOWN to current alive (lone-survivor win + never-more-than-alive) but never UP, so a mid-window respawn can never raise the bar above this baseline (R16-1, closing the round-16 E.33 fantasy violation) and a death cannot relax it below the baseline either (the BASELINE is monotone-frozen; the live requiredHolders is non-monotone 2→1→2 by design — it clamps down on death and restores toward, never above, the baseline on respawn). This is the ONLY stored holder-count state; requiredHolders itself is derived per tick from this + the live count
    runOutcomeResolved: boolean, -- (round-9, Blocker #5) IDEMPOTENT TERMINAL LATCH. false until the first terminal run-outcome transition; set true (atomically — single-threaded Luau) by whichever fires first: BCT4 victory, BCT-DEFEAT scatter, BCT-DEFEAT wipe, **BCT5 defensive decay** (round-9 gate-fix — the gate found BCT5 was the one BC4-exit transition NOT in this list, so a normally-unreachable BCT5 could leave the latch unset and let the handler re-enter), the BindToClose forced-cleanup path, or — for a run that ends **outside** the beacon lifecycle (e.g. PC's all-dead path *before* activation, or any other run-end source) — **Crafting's own `RunEnded` subscription (build-gate C2 fix): `CraftingService` subscribes to the run-lifecycle `RunEnded` signal and, on receipt, sets `runOutcomeResolved = true` and ceases all `_step` run-scope work (bench + alive drains stop).** This is the mechanism behind "a RunEnded from any source": PC owns the `RunEnded` broadcast but does NOT write this Crafting-owned field — Crafting latches it on its own subscription, so a PC-owned outside-BC4 wipe cleanly halts Crafting's whole-run drain rather than leaving it running against an ended run. Equivalently: **every BC4-exit transition sets it, and any `RunEnded` (BC4 or not) sets it via the subscription.** `_step` checks `if runOutcomeResolved then return end` as its **very first statement** (Conv-3 round-13 — the `_step`-top guard, not merely the first statement of the BC4 segment; reconciles this field with C.8/C.9/H.113), it is re-checked after the BC4 segment to suppress the same-tick bench tick (Conv-4 outcome-first), and every terminal transition checks-and-sets it before running cleanup — so run-end cleanup executes EXACTLY ONCE no matter how many paths trigger on the same or adjacent tick. This is the mutual-exclusion guard that lets the BC4 handler own all three window outcomes (survived/scatter/wipe — C.9) without double-cleanup against PC's all-dead path. Resolves the round-8 "BCT-DEFEAT wipe has two firing paths, no mutual-exclusion → double-cleanup" blocker
}
```

**RunStarted initialisation contract (build-gate O1 fix — the run-init entry point the whole-run lifecycle needs):** `CraftingService` exposes `OnRunStarted(runId, squadRoster)` where `squadRoster: { number }` is the list of starting-squad UserIds **delivered by the upstream run-start / matchmaking gate** (F.1/F.7 — Crafting does not own roster assembly; it receives it). It is invoked once when the run begins, and populates the `RunSession` fields in this fixed order: (1) `runId`, `runStartTime = _clock()`; (2) `aliveMembers = { [uid]: true for uid in squadRoster }` (the full starting squad) and `peakAliveCount = #squadRoster` (round-19 R18-1 — the max-alive-so-far ratchet, seeded to the full starting squad); (3) `benchMaxCraftersEffective = min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(#squadRoster / 2))` (computed once, never recomputed — C.3.5); (4) `_pendingDepartures = {}`, `_pendingReadds = {}` (round-13), the lifecycle booleans `false`, `runOutcomeResolved = false`, position/time fields `nil`, `_windowPrevInRadius = nil` (set `{}` only at BCT3), `_lineBreakSince = nil` (round-13 — stays `nil` until a BC4 line-break starts it), `_lineBreakRestoreTicks = 0` (round-15 — HUD restore-debounce counter), `_lineBreakBroadcast = false` (round-16 — HUD presenting HOLDING), `requiredHoldersBaseline = nil` (round-16 — set at BCT3 from the `peakAliveCount` ratchet, R18-1). The single `_step` Heartbeat connection (C.8) is bound here. `#squadRoster >= MIN_SQUAD_SIZE` is asserted (C.17). The roster is the squad-size source for the cap and for the init assertion (H.84).

**Window-participant evaluation (round-7 A-1/A-2 — single source of truth; alive-predicate locked round-9):** the BC4 win/lose decision has exactly one data model. On each Heartbeat while in BC4, the single CraftingService Heartbeat handler derives a per-tick value `windowAliveInRadius` = `{ member | member ∈ RunSession.aliveMembers AND dist3D(_memberPosition(member), RunSession.beaconWorldPosition) <= BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS }`, computed ONCE at the top of the tick, BEFORE draining `_pendingDepartures` (C.8/C.9). **Holder threshold (round-15 SENTRY fix; round-16 R16-1 — FROZEN at window-open; round-19 R18-1 — from the max-alive-so-far ratchet):** the hold is evaluated against `requiredHolders`, defined as **`requiredHolders = min(RunSession.requiredHoldersBaseline, #RunSession.aliveMembers)`**, where `requiredHoldersBaseline = math.ceil(RunSession.peakAliveCount / 2)` is **computed ONCE at BCT3 (window-open) from the run's high-water alive count `peakAliveCount` (the max-alive-so-far ratchet, round-19 R18-1 — NOT the activation-snapshot alive count) and STORED frozen** for the whole window (C.9 BCT3 step 6; schema field below) — it is **NOT recomputed per tick from the live count** (the round-15 design, retired round-16) and **NOT taken from the activation snapshot** (the round-16 design, retired round-19 to close the activate-late lane). The per-tick value clamps the frozen baseline down to the **current** alive count via `min(...)` — never up — so a lone survivor always wins (`min(baseline, 1) = 1`) and the threshold can never demand more holders than are alive, while a mid-window respawn can **never raise the bar above the activation baseline** (R16-1, closing the round-16 E.33 fantasy violation). The predicate is **`held ⟺ #windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders`**. The snapshot numerator (`#windowAliveInRadius`) is built from the **same pre-drain `aliveMembers`** (top of tick, BEFORE step (c)'s departure/re-add drains); `requiredHolders` reads the frozen baseline + that same pre-drain alive count, so numerator and the clamp share one consistent alive-set. **Shorthand convention:** throughout this GDD the inline gloss `requiredHolders = ceil(#aliveMembers/2)` denotes this **run-start-frozen (peak-ratchet), clamped** value — the baseline is `ceil(peakAliveCount/2)` computed once at BCT3 (round-19 R18-1), then clamped down per tick by `min(baseline, #aliveMembers)`. Under the full-alive-start invariant `peakAliveCount == #squadRoster`, so the gloss equals `ceil(#startingSquad/2)`; it is NOT a live per-tick recompute (a round-16 reconciliation error) and NOT the activation-snapshot count (a round-19 reconciliation error, the activate-late lane). The round-13 binary "`held ⟺ windowAliveInRadius non-empty`" was the `requiredHolders == 1` special case; round-15 generalised it to **at least half the living squad** (closing the SENTRY line where one of four held for the rest); round-16 froze the denominator (so deaths cannot relax the bar below baseline and respawns cannot raise it above baseline); round-19 (R18-1) changed the frozen denominator's *source* from the activation snapshot to the run's **high-water alive count** (`peakAliveCount`), so delaying activation to thin the squad can no longer lower the bar (the activate-late lane). The BASELINE is thus monotone-frozen; the live `requiredHolders` is non-monotone (2→1→2) by design — it clamps down to the current alive count and restores toward, never above, the baseline. `requiredHolders` is **derived per tick** from the frozen baseline + live count (only `requiredHoldersBaseline` is stored); `aliveMembers`-empty is the separate **wipe** path (it does not go through `requiredHolders`). `ceil(1/2)=1` (a 1-alive activation) and the `min(baseline, currentAlive)` clamp both keep the lone-survivor win. **Position source (round-9 gate-fix):** the member's position is read through the injected `_memberPosition(playerId) → Vector3` seam (C.16) — production default reads the member's server-tracked `HumanoidRootPart.Position`; the test override returns an injected value (`_setMemberPosition`). Handlers never read `HumanoidRootPart.Position` directly, for the same hermeticity reason as `_clock()`. **`dist3D` is full 3D Euclidean distance** (`(a − b).Magnitude`), NOT horizontal/XZ-plane — a member on a floor above/below the beacon is out-of-radius if their 3D distance exceeds `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`, so the hold is a true sphere around the plinth (level design must keep the plinth's reachable hold-space within a **12-stud** sphere and ensure `requiredHolders` members can physically stand within it — F.2 LD note: a 12-stud radius is a tight last-stand on the plinth, so the plinth's standable footprint must fit the max `requiredHolders` for a 4-player squad, `ceil(4/2) = 2` bodies). **"Alive" is defined as membership in `RunSession.aliveMembers`** (the server-authoritative alive-set, above) — round-9 (Blocker #3 / Q2) replaces the previously-undefined "member alive" phrasing, which left two implementers free to write two race-different predicates (live `Humanoid.Health` vs a `PlayerRemoving` connection vs an authoritative set). Because membership is decoupled from character teardown, the `HumanoidRootPart.Position` read is *guarded* by it: a member still in `aliveMembers` is guaranteed a valid character to read this tick, and a member being torn down in `Players.PlayerRemoving` has already been removed from `aliveMembers` at the departure drain — it is **never** observed via a racing half-destroyed `HumanoidRootPart`; its removal flows only through the buffered-departure drain. `windowAliveInRadius` is itself a **derived per-tick value, not stored across ticks** (no `RunSession` field persists it — recomputing each tick is cheap and avoids a stale-cache class). **Both** the BCT4 victory check and the BCT-DEFEAT line-break check read this same snapshot — resolving the round-6 "two sources of truth" finding (C.9 read a *live* `HumanoidRootPart.Position`; the H.77 tie-break read a *buffered pre-drain* set). **Round-13 (continuous-hold), round-15 (holder threshold):** the snapshot is consulted on **every** BC4 tick, not only at window-end — a **below-threshold** tick (`#windowAliveInRadius < requiredHolders`) while members are alive starts/continues the `_lineBreakSince` grace (C.9 step b), and an **at-or-above-threshold** tick (`>= requiredHolders`) clears it; the single per-tick snapshot (with the per-tick `requiredHolders`) remains the one source of truth for victory, line-break, and the grace decision alike. Verified by H.77 (tie-break reads the snapshot) + H.99 (single source of truth) + H.100 (`benchMaxCraftersEffective` storage) + H.103 (alive-set is the only alive-predicate).

**Hold-state crossing detection (round-9, Blocker #1 / Q1 — presentation-only, walled off from the win path; hysteresis added round-11 Convergence #4):** the `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged` HUD signal (C.14/C.15) is **edge-triggered with hysteresis** off the stored `RunSession._windowPrevInRadius` set (the set the HUD currently shows as in-radius), NOT off the win snapshot. After the handler builds this tick's `windowAliveInRadius` and runs the win checks (above), it updates the presented set with a **Schmitt-trigger band**: for each alive member, a member NOT currently shown in-radius **enters** (→ shown in-radius) only when `dist3D <= BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`; a member currently shown in-radius **leaves** (→ shown out-of-radius) only when `dist3D > BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS + BEACON_HOLD_HYSTERESIS` (G.6, ~3 studs); a member inside the band `(R, R + HYSTERESIS]` **keeps their prior presented state**. The handler fires `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged(playerId, inRadius=true|false)` only for members whose presented state **changed** this tick (an `inRadius=false` fires only for a member who is **still alive** and genuinely crossed the outer band outward), then overwrites `_windowPrevInRadius` with the new presented set. **Members removed from `aliveMembers` (death/disconnect) are dropped from the presented set SILENTLY — no `inRadius=false` is fired for them (build-gate A2):** a dead/disconnected member should not read as merely "out of radius"; their state is conveyed by PC's death/disconnect HUD path, and the HUD clears that member's hold-row on that event. Firing `inRadius=false` only for live radius-exits keeps the hold indicator's two states ("in-radius" / "out-of-radius, still alive") from being conflated with "dead." `_windowPrevInRadius` starts `{}` at BCT3, so members already inside `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` at activation each fire one `inRadius=true` on the first BC4 tick. **Why hysteresis (round-11 Convergence #4 — network B-NET-2 / ux UX-R2):** without it, a player standing at the exact 12-stud boundary (`BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`) with normal network/animation position jitter flip-flops across the single threshold every Heartbeat — the round-10 panel measured ~120 events/s × squad against the bandwidth budget. The band means a member must travel a full `BEACON_HOLD_HYSTERESIS` past the radius to register a *leave*, so boundary jitter emits nothing. **The win snapshot `windowAliveInRadius` is UNAFFECTED — it stays the strict `dist3D <= BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` test with NO hysteresis (C.9 / H.92), so victory/scatter is decided on the exact radius; only the HUD presentation is debounced.** This set is **only** read/written by the crossing-detection step — the win decision never touches it. This resolves the round-8 contradiction (crossing detection appeared to need cross-tick state C.10 forbade storing): the WIN snapshot stays un-stored and per-tick; the PRESENTATION prev-set is explicitly stored, named, and isolated. **Round-13 — squad-level cue; round-15 — debounced + threshold:** the same crossing-detection step (d) also emits the strict squad-level `OnBeaconLineBreak(broken, timestamp, graceSeconds)` (C.14) on the HOLDING↔BROKEN edge of the **strict holder-threshold test `#windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders`** (NO hysteresis — it must agree with the win check), driven by the `_lineBreakSince` grace clock — this is the win-honest HUD cue (it dissolves ux-B12-2 because it never disagrees with the strict win check), distinct from the per-member hysteresis-banded `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged`. **Restore debounce + broken=true edge-trigger (round-15 quadruple-lens N14-B2 / WCAG fix; round-16 R16-2 completes the symmetry):** without a debounce, a sole holder oscillating at the 12-stud boundary flips the count across the threshold every Heartbeat and flaps the squad-level win-state banner at up to 60 Hz. The banner is therefore driven as a **two-state edge machine** off the stored presented state `RunSession._lineBreakBroadcast` (false = presenting HOLDING, true = presenting BROKEN), so **each edge fires at most once per line-break episode**:
> - **`broken=true` (HOLDING→BROKEN) — edge-triggered (round-16 R16-2):** on a below-threshold tick, fire `OnBeaconLineBreak(broken=true)` and set `_lineBreakBroadcast = true` **only if `_lineBreakBroadcast` was `false`** (the HOLDING→BROKEN edge). If `_lineBreakBroadcast` is already `true` (the banner is already BROKEN), do **NOT** re-fire — this is the round-16 fix: the round-15 spec fired `broken=true` on *every* below-threshold tick, so a boundary-oscillating holder re-fired it at Heartbeat rate (the systems-B2/qa-B1 AC-vs-spec contradiction — H.119/H.95 assert "at most one broken=true," which the un-edge-triggered rule violated). The alarm is still **immediate** (it fires the first below-threshold tick of the episode), just not repeated.
> - **`broken=false` (BROKEN→HOLDING) — debounced (round-15):** the `_lineBreakRestoreTicks` counter on `RunSession` is incremented each at-threshold tick and **reset to 0 only on a below-threshold tick** (not on fire). The restore edge fires `OnBeaconLineBreak(broken=false)` and sets `_lineBreakBroadcast = false` on the **single tick `_lineBreakRestoreTicks` becomes exactly equal to `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_RESTORE_DEBOUNCE`** AND `_lineBreakBroadcast` is currently `true`. Because it is not reset on fire, further at-threshold ticks leave the counter `> knob` and never re-fire; the counter only returns to 0 when the hold next drops below threshold.
>
> With both edges gated by `_lineBreakBroadcast`, a boundary-oscillating holder that never sustains the threshold for the full debounce fires **exactly one `broken=true` and zero `broken=false`** (not a per-tick flap); a genuine recovery (≥ `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_RESTORE_DEBOUNCE` consecutive at-threshold ticks) fires exactly one `broken=false`. The debounce + edge-trigger are **HUD-only** — the `_lineBreakSince` terminal grace clock uses the strict per-tick test and is unaffected, so a debounced banner can never delay or deny the actual scatter terminal. **Wipe-tick guard (round-15 N14-B1):** `OnBeaconLineBreak` is NOT fired on a tick that resolves a terminal — **step (d) begins with its own in-step guard `if RunSession.runOutcomeResolved then return`** (NOT the `_step` post-segment guard, which sits *after* the whole window/alive segment that contains (d) — so it could not pre-empt (d)); a terminal resolved earlier in the BC4 segment (victory in (a), wipe/scatter in (c)) therefore skips (d) entirely, and a wipe (aliveMembers empty) fires `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")` with no preceding `OnBeaconLineBreak(broken=true)` on that tick. **Colorblind (round-15 ux F3):** the HOLDING/BROKEN binary is the most safety-critical HUD element, so it carries a **shape/icon redundancy** (a closed-ring "HOLDING" glyph vs a broken-ring "BROKEN" glyph + the grace countdown), never color alone — mirroring the locked surface-4 Coil colorblind fix (UI.1 surface 13). **Cleanup (round-9 gate-fix; window-state fields extended round-13/15/16):** on **any** BC4 exit — BCT4 victory, BCT-DEFEAT (scatter/wipe), BCT5 decay, or the BindToClose forced path — the centralised run-end cleanup resets **all** window-scoped state: `_windowPrevInRadius = nil`, `_lineBreakSince = nil` (round-13), `_lineBreakRestoreTicks = 0` and `_lineBreakBroadcast = false` (round-15/16), and `requiredHoldersBaseline = nil` (round-16) — alongside clearing the survival-window + decay-watch timers (the build-from-artifact gate found the individual transition side-effect tables didn't all enumerate this; it is centralised in the run-end cleanup path — a named `_runEndCleanup()` — so no single transition can forget it). **`_craftSessions` sweep (round-16 recommended — round-12 REC-5 closure):** `_runEndCleanup()` ALSO clears `_craftSessions` (any in-progress bench sessions are abandoned without firing BT3/BT5 — there is no bench crafting to preserve once the run has ended) and the `PersonalInventory`/`SquadInventory`/`EquippedEffects`/`ActiveDeployables` runtime state (per C.10 close), so no bench-progress tick can run against an ended run. Verified by H.94 (window-state) + H.122 (`_runEndCleanup` clears `_craftSessions`).

**RunSession flag invariants (round-3, declared so any rollback bug is detectable; round-9 fields added):** `beaconActivationTime != nil ⟺ beaconActivated == true` (set together at BCT3). `beaconWorldPosition != nil ⟺ beaconPlaced == true` (set together at BCT2). The three lifecycle booleans are monotonic within a RunSession (never reset to `false` before `RunEnded`, per H.54). `beaconWindowSurvived == true` is terminal (victory) and is mutually exclusive with the BCT-DEFEAT path. **Round-9 / round-11:** `aliveMembers` is monotonically non-increasing **between respawns** (the drain only removes; respawn is the sole re-add path). **Precise non-empty invariant (build-gate C1 fix):** it is never empty *during BC4* outside a terminal transition (the win evaluation requires a non-empty alive-set, and a BC4 emptying IS the wipe terminal). **Outside BC4 the whole-run drain CAN transiently empty it** — a pre-activation full-squad-down before anyone respawns — and that transient is NOT a Crafting invariant violation: an all-dead state outside the beacon lifecycle is **PC's all-dead `RunEnded(defeat)` path's** terminal to own, after which Crafting latches `runOutcomeResolved` via its `RunEnded` subscription (above). The earlier "never empty while the run is live outside a terminal transition" phrasing was too broad now that the drain runs whole-run; the BC4-scoped statement is the correct invariant. `runOutcomeResolved` is monotonic `false → true` and, once `true`, is terminal for the whole run (every cleanup path is a no-op after it is set). `_windowPrevInRadius != nil` only while in BC4 (set `{}` at BCT3, cleared at any BC4 exit); it has no meaning outside the window and is never consulted by the win evaluation. **Round-13 / round-15:** `_lineBreakSince` is `nil` whenever the hold is intact (`#windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders`) and is `nil` outside BC4; it is non-`nil` **only** during an in-progress BC4 scatter line-break (members alive, **fewer than `requiredHolders`** in radius, grace not yet elapsed), and is reset to `nil` the instant the hold is re-established (`>= requiredHolders`) or the run exits BC4. **Round-15:** `_lineBreakRestoreTicks` is `0` outside BC4 and on any below-threshold tick; it counts up only across consecutive at-threshold BC4 ticks and is presentation-only (never feeds a terminal). **Round-16:** `_lineBreakBroadcast` is `false` outside BC4 and is presentation-only (it gates the `OnBeaconLineBreak` edges, never the terminal). `requiredHoldersBaseline` is `nil` outside BC4 and **set exactly once at BCT3** from the `peakAliveCount` ratchet (round-19 R18-1 — NOT the activation alive-set), then frozen until the next BC4 exit clears it (R16-1); the per-tick `requiredHolders = min(requiredHoldersBaseline, #aliveMembers)` is derived, never stored, and consulted by the win/lose evaluation (it is the holder threshold the line-break test reads, C.9). **Round-19:** `peakAliveCount` is monotonically non-decreasing over the run (the `max` ratchet only raises it), run-scoped (set at RunStarted, not cleared at BC4 exit); under the full-alive-start invariant it equals `#squadRoster` throughout, and it is read only at BCT3 to derive the baseline. Unlike `_windowPrevInRadius`, the `_lineBreakSince` grace clock **is** consulted by the win/lose evaluation (it is the scatter-defeat grace timer, C.9 step b) — it is the one piece of *timing* window-scoped state that feeds a terminal decision. `_pendingDepartures` (both scopes) **and `_pendingReadds` (round-13)** are empty at the end of every drain — a non-empty queue surviving a tick is a drain bug.

**CraftSession** (one per active bench, lifecycle = BT1 to BT3 or BT5/BT6). `CraftingService` holds the active-session map `_craftSessions: { [benchId]: CraftSession }` (round-9 gate-fix — the gate flagged the service-level bench→session map was unnamed): `_craftSessions[benchId] == nil ⟺ that bench is in BS1`; a `CraftSession` is inserted at BT1 and removed at BT3/BT5/BT6; the per-`_step` progress tick iterates `_craftSessions`, and `_enqueueDeparture`'s bench scope targets the session the departing player is a crafter in:



```
CraftSession = {
    benchId:           string,    -- level-design-assigned bench identifier
    benchPosition:     Vector3,   -- the bench's immutable server-tracked world position; snapshotted at BT1 from the level-design bench record. The `position` argument for every D.3 / D.7 Emit call (round-2 B8: referenced by D.3/D.7 but previously absent from the schema)
    recipeId:          string,    -- recipe being crafted
    crafters:          { number },-- ordered array of UserIds; length 1..BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS
    primaryCrafterId:  number,    -- UserId of the BT1 initiator; set once at BT1 and NEVER reassigned (round-2 B7: a stable attribution field, distinct from crafters[1] which can shift if the initiator departs via BT4). Drives D.7 midpulse attribution and BCT1 beaconCrafterId
    craftProgress:     number,    -- 0.0..1.0; DERIVED each Heartbeat by the D.1 clock-elapsed closed form, NOT a running accumulator (round-5 R4-3). craftProgress = clamp(progressAtRateAnchor + ((_clock() - rateAnchorClock) × crafterSpeedMultiplier(N)) / CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME, 0, 1)
    progressAtRateAnchor: number, -- (round-5 R4-3) progress value captured at the last rate change (0.0 at BT1; reset to the current craftProgress on each BT2/BT4). The piecewise base for the from-start closed form
    rateAnchorClock:   number,    -- (round-5 R4-3) _clock() captured at the last rate change (BT1; re-captured on each BT2/BT4). The segment's elapsed time is _clock() - rateAnchorClock. Clock-elapsed (not step×dt) so it is correct under variable Heartbeat dt and exactly testable via the C.16 _clock seam (H.27/H.28/H.30/H.31/H.41)
    startTime:         number,    -- _clock() at BT1 (production default workspace:GetServerTimeNow() per C.16). For analytics/estimatedCompletionTime; rateAnchorClock is the value the D.1 closed form reads
    lastCrafterCount:  number,    -- N; updated on BT2/BT4 to drive D.2 rate calculation; the input to crafterSpeedMultiplier(N) in the D.1 closed form
    midpulseFired:     boolean,   -- Beacon-only: tracks whether the 5s mid-craft pulse has fired (per C.2.7)
    _pendingDepartures: { { playerId: number, reason: string } }, -- (round-5 R4-5 buffer; NAMED in the schema round-9, Blocker #2) the BENCH-scope departure queue for THIS CraftSession. LIFECYCLE: created empty at BT1; APPENDED by the _enqueueDeparture fan-out seam (C.8) when a departing player is in this session's `crafters`; DRAINED inside the bench progress-tick AFTER the D.1 completion check (C.8 tie-break) — each drained entry removes its player from `crafters`, fires BT4 (others remain) or BT5 (sole crafter), then is DISCARDED (queue emptied each tick). DE-DUP (round-9, Blocker #6): _enqueueDeparture is a no-op if an entry for this {playerId, this-CraftSession-scope} is already pending — covers the RequestCraftCancel double-fire (two cancels in one Heartbeat both pass the in-handler validation because the caller is still in `crafters`, but only the first enqueues, so the drain cannot double-remove a possibly-cleared session). The RunSession/window scope has its own independent queue (RunSession._pendingDepartures); the same departure event fans out to both (H.101)
}
```

**Server-crash mid-craft (round-1 B11)**: `CraftSession` is purely runtime (per C.4 / Pillar 4). A server crash between BT1 and BT3 loses the entire session with no persistence and no resume — on restart there is no in-run save to recover (consistent with the BCT3→BCT4 crash note in C.9). No materials were deducted (deduction is atomic at BT3 only, C.3.6), so nothing is lost from player inventories; the player simply re-approaches the bench and restarts. The bench returns to `BS1` by default because no `CraftSession` survives the restart. Verified by H.74.

**ActiveDeployables** (squad-scoped; tracks live placed items):

```
ActiveDeployables = {
    signalAnchor: { anchorId: string, position: Vector3, lifetimeStart: number, tripped: boolean }?,
    -- (round-5 R4-2: squadRelay entry removed with the Squad Relay cut — the Signal Anchor is now the only tracked deployable)
    -- Beacon BasePart tracked via RunSession.beaconWorldPosition + beaconActivated flag, not here
}
```

Constraint: `signalAnchor != nil` is mutually exclusive with itself across time (placing a second Anchor cancels the first per C.6 Signal Anchor rule). Server enforces by clearing the existing entry on the new `RequestPlaceItem` validation pass before spawning the replacement.

**EquippedEffects** (per-player, lifecycle = equip event to consumption / expiry / death):

```
EquippedEffects[playerId] = {
    dampenerCoil:   { equippedAt: number, expiresAt: number }?,    -- expiresAt = equippedAt + DAMPENER_DURATION OR next bench visit OR death; nil if not equipped
    -- (round-2: compositePatch + quietStepWrap fields removed with the recipe cut — DampenerCoil is the only equipped effect)
}
```

**Death-cleanup invariant**: when a player enters `Dead-Respawning` (per PC.S4), the server clears all entries in `EquippedEffects[playerId]`. Effects terminate; consumable items return to the squad shared pool only if they were not already consumed. This is the implementation of "On equipped player's death, effect terminates immediately" from C.4.

**Inventory schemas** (already defined in C.4): `PersonalInventory[playerId]` (per-player; raw materials only) and `SquadInventory` (squad-wide; crafted items only). Both are part of the Crafting Service's runtime state; both are cleared at `RunEnded`.

### Interactions with Other Systems

#### C.11 — Disturbance System Contract (publisher)

Crafting & Items publishes two emission types per ED.C.3.6: `Beacon` (already locked) and `Craft` (new — consumes 1 of 4 reserved enum slots; registry update in Phase 5). Per ED.F.3a, this section is normative — drift between this section and ED is a defect against ED.

1. **Craft emission** — fired by the server at recipe-completion (BT3) for each crafter:
   - `DisturbanceService:Emit(emissionType="Craft", position=benchPosition, initialMagnitude=recipeBurstMagnitude, sourcePlayerId=crafterId_i)` where `recipeBurstMagnitude` is the recipe's `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_*` constant per C.2 (range: 0.10 – 0.35).
   - **N concurrent independent emissions** at BT3 — one Emit call per crafter (preserves attribution + ED.C.1.2 reserved-band compliance per D.3).
   - **Position semantics**: `benchPosition` is the bench's server-tracked world position (immutable per level-design). NOT the crafting player's `HumanoidRootPart.Position`.
   - **In-window Oxygen Canister use (round-7 — Group-B bunker counter; emission position → beacon round-11 EC-3; post-terminal guard round-16):** a 4th Craft-emission trigger. When `RequestUseItem("OxygenCanister")` is handled **while the survival window is genuinely live** (`RunSession.beaconActivated == true && not RunSession.runOutcomeResolved` — round-16 network BLOCKING-1: `not runOutcomeResolved`, NOT `beaconWindowSurvived == false`, so a use after a *defeat* resolves cannot fire an orphaned Emit into an ended run; see C.6), the server fires `DisturbanceService:Emit(emissionType="Craft", position=RunSession.beaconWorldPosition, initialMagnitude=MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE=0.18, sourcePlayerId=usingPlayerId)` after the RM restore — a SINGLE emission attributed to the using player but fired at the **beacon's world position** (round-11 EC-3 ruling — NOT the player position, NOT `benchPosition`: the in-window oxygen spend concentrates at the beacon, foreclosing the round-10 scatter-to-breathe bait, C.6). Outside BC4 this trigger does not fire (C.6). Reserved-band compliant (0.18 ≤ 0.35 ≤ 0.85). The `position` is a server-tracked `RunSession` field (the `RequestUseItem` payload has no position field), so it opens no client-spoof path; `sourcePlayerId` is the using player, so attribution is correct; it does not touch the beacon completion/attribution chain (the beacon's own activation Emit uses `beaconPlacerId`).
   - **Server clock**: every time read routes through the C.16 `_clock()` seam (production default `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`), same underlying rule as ED.D.1; handler bodies MUST NOT call `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` directly (R4-4).
   - **No client-callable Emit path**: ED.C.1.8 / ED.F.4 forbid client-driven Emit. `RequestCraft` is a *request* to start a craft; the Emit fires server-internally only on legitimate craft completion (BT3 with all guards passed).
2. **Beacon emission** — fired by the server at BCT3:
   - `DisturbanceService:Emit(emissionType="Beacon", position=RunSession.beaconWorldPosition, initialMagnitude=MAGNITUDE_BEACON=0.95, sourcePlayerId=RunSession.beaconPlacerId)`.
   - **Single emission per run** — guarded by `RunSession.beaconActivated`.
   - Position per ED.C.3.6: beacon's world position, NOT the activator's. Already locked.
   - `attributionChain` includes placer's UserId + action label `"BeaconPlaced"` per ED.C.3.6.
3. **Beacon mid-craft pulse** — fired once when `craftProgress >= CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_PROGRESS = 0.5` for the Beacon recipe only:
   - `DisturbanceService:Emit(emissionType="Craft", position=CraftSession.benchPosition, initialMagnitude=MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE=0.18, sourcePlayerId=CraftSession.primaryCrafterId)` (single emission; uses the stable primary-crafter field — round-2 B7, NOT `crafters[1]` which can shift on BT4 departure; not duplicated for N=2).
   - At default tunings (Beacon craft time 10 s, N=1), this fires at wall-clock 5 s (the `CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_TIME = 5 s` worked example in C.2.7 is the derived value at default; the *registered tunable* is the progress fraction, not the seconds).
   - Tracked by `CraftSession.midpulseFired` boolean. BT2 multi-crafter join does NOT re-fire the midpulse if it has already fired.
4. **No client-bound Service.Client surface**: Crafting & Items' Knit `Service:Client` exposes ZERO RemoteFunctions (matches ED.F.4 "RemoteFunctions intentionally absent from the client surface" rule). All client→server communication is via Knit RemoteSignal (one-way fire-and-forget). Adding any client-callable RemoteFunction is a regression that reviewers MUST reject.
5. **Reserved-band compliance**: all `Craft` emissions are below 0.85 (max 0.35 per C.2). All `Beacon` emissions are exactly 0.95 (locked). No emission this GDD publishes can violate ED.C.1.2.

#### C.12 — Player Controller Contract

Per PC.F.2 (provisional contracts when authored), Crafting & Items resolves both rows:

1. **Crafting → PC: victory + window signals** *(round-2 change — the victory trigger moved from activation to window-survival)*:
   - **`OnBeaconWindowSurvived()`** — fired at **BCT4** (survival window elapsed with **at least `requiredHolders = min(requiredHoldersBaseline, #aliveMembers)` squad members alive AND within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs`** — where `requiredHoldersBaseline = ceil(peakAliveCount/2)` is **frozen at activation** from the max-alive-so-far ratchet, **not** recomputed per tick — B1 round-3, holder-threshold round-15, freeze round-16 / ratchet round-19; the bare `ceil(#aliveMembers/2)` gloss elsewhere is the C.10 shorthand convention for exactly this frozen-baseline value, spelled out here because PC reads this row to wire T8 — round-21 systems R3). **This is the T8 victory trigger.** PC subscribes for its T8 (squad escape → victory) state transition. Payload: `{timestamp: number}`. Fires at most once per run (guarded by `RunSession.beaconWindowSurvived`). PC's behaviour on receipt: broadcast `RunEnded(victory)`; lock all inputs (per PC.C.6 T8 transition).
   - **`OnEscapeBeaconActivated()`** — fired at **BCT3** (activation). **No longer the victory trigger** — it now signals the *start* of the survival window, for HUD (window countdown) + audio (Hunt-stem rise per Section B). Payload: `{activatorPlayerId: UserId, timestamp: number, windowDurationSeconds: number}` (Conv-2 round-13 — carries `timestamp` + the server-derived `windowDurationSeconds = BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW`, consistent with `OnBeaconActivated` and C.9 BCT3; the prior `{activatorPlayerId, timestamp}` form was truncated and left the client unable to derive the window length). Fires once per run (guarded by `RunSession.beaconActivated`). PC may use it to enter a "beacon active / surviving the window" presentation state, but PC MUST NOT fire T8 on this signal.
   - **Defeat paths (round-9 single decision point — BOTH BC4 defeats route through one Crafting signal; round-11 adds the suppression protocol)**: during BC4 there are two defeat sub-cases and **both fire `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason)`** which PC subscribes to and maps to `RunEnded(defeat)`: (a) a **full-squad wipe** — Crafting's buffered departure drain empties `RunSession.aliveMembers` before window-end → `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")`; (b) a **scatter / line-break** (B1; continuous-hold round-13, holder-threshold round-15) — the `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot stays **below `requiredHolders = ceil(#aliveMembers/2)`** (members alive, fewer than `requiredHolders` within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs`) for the full `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE`, resolvable at **any** BC4 tick (NOT only window-end — the round-3/9 window-end-only check is retired) → `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="scatter")`. Payload: `{reason: "scatter" | "wipe", timestamp: number}`. Fires at most once per run (mutually exclusive with `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` — Crafting fires exactly one of the three window outcomes at window-end, C.9). *(Round-9 change, Blocker #5: pre-round-9 the wipe was routed through PC's all-dead path with no Crafting signal — the split that produced the round-8 double-fire race; round-9 made the BC4 handler the single owner of all three outcomes. This bullet's prior "(a) wipe via PC's all-dead path, Crafting fires no signal" wording was a round-10 reconciliation miss, corrected here alongside E.24 / H.76 / H.89.)* PC's behaviour on receipt: identical to its all-dead defeat handling (broadcast `RunEnded(defeat)`, lock inputs), just triggered by signal instead of by the all-dead condition. **Suppression protocol (round-11 Convergence #2; gate widened per build-gate A4):** PC MUST suppress its own generic all-dead `RunEnded(defeat)` path **for the whole post-activation lifecycle — gate on `RunSession.beaconActivated == true` alone** (from BCT3 until `RunEnded`), NOT the narrower `beaconActivated && !beaconWindowSurvived` (which would *release* suppression at the victory write and let a second member's later same-frame death fire a post-victory defeat — build-gate A4). PC lets Crafting's `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")` own the in-window wipe; otherwise PC's synchronous `Humanoid.Died`→`RunEnded(defeat)` can fire before Crafting's drain sets `runOutcomeResolved`, a same-frame double-cleanup. Once activated, every terminal outcome reaches PC through a Crafting signal (`OnBeaconWindowSurvived`/`OnBeaconWindowFailed`), so PC never needs its all-dead path post-activation. Outside the whole beacon-active lifecycle (i.e. before BCT3), PC's all-dead path owns defeat as before. The Crafting→PC couplings are therefore: PC must **not** treat beacon activation as victory (wait for `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`); PC must subscribe to `OnBeaconWindowFailed` and map **both** `{scatter, wipe}` to defeat; and PC must **suppress its all-dead path during BC4**.
2. **PC → Crafting: `RequestInteract(targetId)`** — PC fires this for crafting bench and beacon interactions (round-5: the squad-relay interaction is removed with the Relay cut). Crafting & Items owns the **receive-side validation**. The `targetId` routes to the appropriate handler:
   - If `targetId` resolves to a bench: server treats as `RequestCraft`'s prerequisite (open the bench UI). Validates per BT1 prelim.
   - If `targetId` resolves to the placed Beacon (BC3 only): server treats as `RequestBeaconActivate`. Validates per BCT3 prelim.
   - If `targetId` resolves to nothing this system owns: server returns `InteractRejected(reason="not-crafting-target")` and PC may try other systems.

   This is a routing convenience. The bench-open / beacon-activate flows have their own dedicated RemoteEvents (`RequestCraft`, `RequestBeaconActivate`); the `RequestInteract` from PC is the user-input-level abstraction that maps to those system-specific calls. Architecture phase will reconcile via an ADR.
3. **PC's `Dead-Respawning` (S4) state**: per PC.C.5, gather/interact/craft inputs are denied on the client. Crafting Service additionally rejects ALL Crafting-system RemoteEvents from dead players server-side (defense-in-depth). The `EquippedEffects[playerId]` cleanup at S4 entry (per C.10) ensures equipped effects do not persist through death.
4. **Reuse of PC-locked constants**: Crafting reuses `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 4 studs` for bench-open / beacon-activate ranges *(round-5: relay-activate range removed with the Squad Relay cut R4-2; round-21: scrubbed the two orphaned "relay-activate" tokens that survived here)*; reuses `TAP_HOLD_COMMIT_DURATION = 0.5 s` for the **beacon placement and beacon activation** commits (B6 round-3 ruling — both are tap-hold, per art-bible's high-consequence-action lock; see C.5.2, UI.2). Bench-open remains **single-tap-with-prompt** (low-consequence, reversible / repeatable). Recipe-commit at the bench is the bench's own **two-tap** pattern (C.3, UI.2). **Crafting does NOT redefine these constants** — PC owns them. *(Round-3 correction: the pre-round-3 text claimed "none in MVP — beacon uses single-tap"; that contradicted the art-bible tap-hold lock and the two-tap recipe-commit, and is the B6 fix.)*

#### C.13 — Resource Management & Resource Node Contracts

Both downstream systems are **Not Started** at this GDD's authoring. Provisional contracts for the interfaces this GDD requires:

**Resource Management** (downstream — RM consumes Crafting's item definitions):
- Crafting GDD owns the **Oxygen Canister item definition** (recipe + cost + craft-burst). RM owns the oxygen-pulse transfer math (how much oxygen a Canister adds; whether it adds to a player's segment or the squad pool; rate limits).
- Crafting GDD provides the signal: `OnOxygenPulseRequest(amount, requestingPlayerId)` fires from Crafting Service when a player uses an Oxygen Canister (round-5: the Squad Relay's `OnOxygenTransferRequest` path is removed with the Relay cut). Amount is recipe-defined (TBD in RM GDD); requestingPlayerId is the using player.
- RM is responsible for the squad oxygen pool's data model, decay tick, and per-player grace timer (per PC.C.5.1).
- **Bidirectional consistency obligation**: RM GDD MUST list Crafting & Items as a dependency (Crafting → RM: oxygen item definition + transfer requests).

**Resource Node** (downstream — RN consumes Crafting's material definitions):
- Crafting GDD owns the **3 material definitions** (BIOMASS, MINERAL, RESONANT). RN owns the per-node yield rates (how many materials per gather; node respawn cadence; node placement).
- Crafting GDD provides the signal: `OnGatherCompleted(playerId, nodeId, materialType, count)` fires from Resource Node Service when a gather succeeds. Crafting Service handles this by adding `count` units of `materialType` to the gathering player's `PersonalInventory`, capped at `MATERIAL_STACK_MAX = 20` per material.
- RN owns the gather emission per ED.C.3.1 (`MAGNITUDE_GATHER_LIGHT/MEDIUM/HEAVY`). Crafting & Items does NOT publish gather emissions — RN does.
- **(round-2: removed)** The Composite Patch ↔ Resource Node gather-downgrade interaction is **gone** with the Patch cut. Crafting no longer exposes a `GetEquippedEffect` accessor to RN, and RN no longer reads any equipped-effect state before computing gather emission magnitude — **a net simplification of the RN contract**. RN's gather emission is now computed solely from node tier (`MAGNITUDE_GATHER_LIGHT/MEDIUM/HEAVY`) with no Crafting-side modifier. *(The H.59 AC that tested this accessor is removed in Phase B.)*
- **Bidirectional consistency obligation**: RN GDD MUST list Crafting & Items as a dependency (Crafting → RN: material definitions; RN → Crafting: gather completions).

#### C.14 — HUD Contract

The HUD GDD is **Not Started**. Provisional contract for the data Crafting & Items publishes for HUD consumption:

| Server-pushed signal | Payload | HUD render expectation |
|---|---|---|
| `OnInventoryChanged` | `{playerId, scope: "personal"\|"squad", itemId, newCount, delta}` | HUD updates inventory readout (personal: this client's bag; squad: shared pool). |
| `OnCraftStarted` | `{benchId, crafterId, recipeId, estimatedCompletionTime}` | HUD shows progress ring on the crafting player's screen (and a smaller indicator on watching squadmates' HUDs). |
| `OnCraftProgressUpdate` | `{benchId, progress: 0.0..1.0}` | ≤ 5 Hz push to the owning client; HUD lerps the progress ring toward the latest server value. |
| `OnCraftCompleted` | `{benchId, recipeId, crafterIds}` | HUD plays a brief completion animation; updates inventory readout via the simultaneous `OnInventoryChanged`. |
| `OnCraftCancelled` | `{benchId, recipeId, reason}` | HUD plays a "fail" animation on the progress ring; dismisses. |
| `OnBenchOccupancyChanged` | `{benchId, crafterCount}` | HUD shows N=2 vs N=1 indicator (squad coordination read). |
| `OnBeaconPlaced` | `{position, placerPlayerId}` | HUD shows a Beacon waypoint marker for all squad members. |
