| `OnBeaconActivated` | `{position, activatorPlayerId, timestamp, windowDurationSeconds}` | HUD plays the activation cue (white column + flora-snap-to-off) AND **opens the survival-window countdown** — activation is the *window-start* beat, not victory (round-2 change). **`windowDurationSeconds` carries the server-derived `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` (build-gate O5 fix — the client cannot otherwise know the window length, which is a server value, G.6):** the client computes window-end `= timestamp + windowDurationSeconds` against `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` and renders the countdown for all squad members; this same client-side countdown drives the Conv #5 final-`BEACON_HOLD_ESCALATION_LEAD` escalation (UI.1 surface 13), combined with the `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged` in/out state — no separate escalation server signal is needed. |
| `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` | `{timestamp}` | HUD plays the **victory** sequence (per art-bible §4.4 "Victory" beat). Fires at window-end (BCT4). This is the beat that was previously tied to activation. |
| `OnBeaconWindowFailed` | `{reason, timestamp}` | HUD clears the survival-window countdown + hold indicator and lets PC's defeat screen take over. Fires on a BC4 defeat with `reason ∈ {"scatter", "wipe"}` (round-9 — the BC4 handler is now the single decision point for both; round-3 fired it only for scatter and left wipe to PC's all-dead path, the split that produced the round-8 double-fire race). **Round-13 (continuous-hold) / round-15 (holder threshold) — `scatter` redefined:** `"scatter"` = the hold was broken (members alive, **fewer than `requiredHolders` within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`**) for the full `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE` — **can resolve at ANY BC4 tick, not only window-end** (the round-3 window-end-only definition is retired); `"wipe"` = the buffered-departure drain emptied `aliveMembers` (immediate, no grace). Mutually exclusive with `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`; Crafting fires exactly one of the three window outcomes (C.9). |
| `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged` (round-7 C-2) | `{playerId, inRadius: bool}` | HUD updates that member's **per-member positional** hold-state chevron (surface 13 — in-radius vs out-of-radius; demoted to a positional helper round-13, NOT the win indicator). Fired during BC4 **when a member crosses the `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` boundary** (both directions), throttled to boundary-cross with a **`BEACON_HOLD_HYSTERESIS` Schmitt band** (round-11 Convergence #4 — enter at `dist <= R`, leave only at `dist > R + HYSTERESIS`; debounces boundary jitter so a player standing at the radius does NOT flood ~120 events/s). This carries the **live per-member distance state** the round-6 panel found missing. **Presentation only — it does NOT alter the win-condition evaluation, which is now CONTINUOUS (round-13): the strict per-tick `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot + the `_lineBreakSince` grace decide victory/line-break every BC4 tick (C.9/C.10), not a single window-end check.** The authoritative win-state cue is the squad-level `OnBeaconLineBreak` (below), not this per-member signal. A scattered off-screen player combines this in/out state with the beacon waypoint chevron (surface 8, merged into 13 during BC4) for a directional "get back to the beacon" cue (see UI.1 surface 13). |
| `OnBeaconLineBreak` (round-13, B1 fail-fast; debounce concretised round-15) | `{broken: boolean, timestamp: number, graceSeconds: number}` | **The authoritative squad-level HOLDING / BROKEN cue.** Edge-triggered on the strict squad-level holder-threshold transition (NO hysteresis — uses the same strict `#windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders` the win check uses, so the HUD can never read HOLDING while the squad is actually losing; this is what dissolves ux-B12-2). `broken=true` fires the instant the count goes **below `requiredHolders`** (line-break starts), carrying the break `timestamp` + `graceSeconds = BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE` so the client renders **BROKEN + a regroup countdown** locally (grace-end `= timestamp + graceSeconds`); `broken=false` fires when the hold is re-established (the restore debounce completes). **On the `broken=false` payload the server sends `graceSeconds = 0` (round-19 R18-4 / network N18-B2):** the grace is already resolved at re-establish time, so `0` is the unambiguous server-authoritative value — this **replaces the round-16 "the field is present for symmetry and the client IGNORES it" convention**, which relied on an unwritten HUD GDD honoring a parenthetical; sending `0` server-side removes that dependency. The `broken=true` payload carries `graceSeconds = BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE`. **Restore debounce + broken=true edge-trigger — CONCRETE rule (round-15 concretised the round-13 circular "debounce to the `_lineBreakSince` reset"; round-16 R16-2 added the broken=true edge-trigger the round-15 rule was missing):** both edges are gated by the stored presented state `_lineBreakBroadcast` (C.10), so **each fires at most once per line-break episode** — `broken=true` is immediate (fail-fast alarm) but **edge-triggered**, firing only on the HOLDING→BROKEN transition (`_lineBreakBroadcast` false→true), NOT on every below-threshold tick (the round-15 spec re-fired it per tick — the round-16 systems-B2/qa-B1 AC-vs-spec contradiction); `broken=false` fires **only after `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_RESTORE_DEBOUNCE` (G.6, 6) consecutive at-threshold ticks** (counter `_lineBreakRestoreTicks`, C.10) on the BROKEN→HOLDING transition. So a sole boundary-holder oscillating across the strict radius produces **at most one `broken=true` and zero `broken=false`**, never a Heartbeat-rate flap (bandwidth + WCAG 2.3.1 — H.95/H.119; presentation-only, the terminal `_lineBreakSince` grace clock is unaffected). Carries a colorblind shape/icon redundancy (ux F3). NOT fired on a wipe/terminal tick (round-15 N14-B1 — step d is skipped when `runOutcomeResolved` is set; PC's defeat screen takes over via `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")`). |
| `OnSignalAnchorTripped` | `{anchorId, anchorPosition}` | HUD shows a directional indicator pointing to the Anchor for `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_INDICATOR_DURATION` (TBD in HUD GDD). |
| `OnEquippedEffectStarted` | `{playerId, effectName, equippedAt, expiresAt?}` | HUD shows an active-effect badge for the equipping player (only the owner sees their own badge by default). |
| `OnEquippedEffectExpired` | `{playerId, effectName, reason}` | HUD removes the active-effect badge. |

**HUD inventory readout obligations** (passed as design intent; HUD GDD owns the visual spec):
- Personal inventory: 3 material counts (BIOMASS, MINERAL, RESONANT) per player; this client sees only their own.
- Squad inventory: 4 item types' counts (Oxygen Canister, Dampener Coil, Signal Anchor, Beacon — round-5: Squad Relay cut); visible to all alive squad members; updates in real time as items are crafted / consumed.
- Bench-armed prompt: when player enters bench `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS`, HUD shows interaction prompt ("Press E / tap to open bench"). Same pattern as PC's gather proximity prompt.
- **Beacon-cap visual cue**: per ED.F.2a row 7, HUD MAY render a beacon-cap-active indicator when ED's round-5 stationary-squad cap engages. **This is an ED↔HUD obligation, not a Crafting↔HUD obligation** — flagged here for cross-GDD visibility; this GDD does not publish the cap-state signal (ED owns it).

#### C.15 — RemoteEvent Surface

Following PC GDD C.9 format. All client → server requests are validated server-side; rate limits are enforced per-player. All server-pushed events are scoped per the "Direction" column.

| Event | Direction | Payload | Rate Limit | Server Validation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RequestCraft` | C → S | `{recipeId: string}` | 1/s/player sustained; burst up to 3 per 3 s | Per BT1 prelim: alive (not S4/S5); within `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` of a bench; recipe in catalog; combined personal-pool has materials; if Beacon, `RunSession.beaconCrafted == false`; player not already a crafter on this bench session |
| `RequestCraftCancel` | C → S | `{}` (bench inferred from active session) | 1/s/player | Player is currently registered as a crafter in an active `CraftSession` |
| `RequestBeaconPlace` | C → S | `{position: Vector3}` — **direction hint only** (round-2 B13); client fires after a tap-hold commit (B6) | 1/5 s/player | Alive; has Beacon item in `SquadInventory` (`count >= 1`); `RunSession.beaconPlaced == false`. **Position is NOT trusted as an absolute coordinate**: the server validates in order (per C.5.5): (1) **clamp** the requested `position` to within `BEACON_PLACE_MAX_RANGE = 8 studs` of the player's **server-tracked** `HumanoidRootPart.Position`; (2) **LOS reachability raycast** player→clamped candidate — if obstructed, reject `BeaconRejected(reason="unreachable-placement")` (round-3 B4, closes the cross-wall exploit); (3) raycast-to-floor from the clamped candidate; (4) overlap check vs. existing geometry / other beacons. A client value outside max range is clamped to the range boundary, never honored verbatim. See E.22 + E.28 + H.75 + H.83. |
| `RequestBeaconActivate` | C → S | `{}` (position is server-tracked via `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition`); client fires after a tap-hold commit (B6) | 1/s/player (per-player flood guard); AND 1/run/squad effective (enforced by `RunSession.beaconActivated`) | Alive; within `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` of `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition`; Beacon in BC3 (`beaconPlaced == true && beaconActivated == false`). Atomic handler order per C.9 BCT3 (no yield, H.80) |
| `RequestUseItem` | C → S | `{itemId: string}` (Oxygen Canister; instant-use) | 1/s/player sustained; burst up to 3 per 3 s | Alive; item in `SquadInventory` (`count >= 1`); itemId is an instant-use type (currently only Oxygen Canister). **Round-7 (position → beacon round-11 EC-3, reconciled round-13 Conv-1; post-defeat guard round-16):** **while the survival window is genuinely live (`beaconActivated == true && not runOutcomeResolved`** — round-16 network BLOCKING-1: the same guard as C.6/C.11/E.32/H.93, NOT the loose "in BC4" / `beaconWindowSurvived == false` form, so a use after a *defeat* resolves cannot fire an orphaned Emit), the handler additionally fires the in-window Craft emission (C.6 / C.11) **at `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition`** (a server-tracked field — *not* the using player's position, the round-11 EC-3 scatter-to-breathe foreclosure; the `RequestUseItem` payload carries no position, so there is no client coordinate to trust) after the RM restore. `sourcePlayerId` stays the using player (attribution); only the emission *position* is the beacon's. **This cell previously read "at the using player's server-tracked position," contradicting C.6/C.11/E.32/H.93 — the round-12 Conv-1 reconciliation miss.** |
| `RequestEquipItem` | C → S | `{itemId: string}` (Dampener Coil — the only equippable item post-round-2) | 1/s/player sustained; burst up to 3 per 3 s (round-7 — added for parity, round-1 B12 class: a flat 1/s with no burst throttles a legitimate retry after an `already-equipped`/`item-unavailable` rejection) | Alive; item in `SquadInventory`; itemId is an equippable type; player does not have an active equipped instance of the same type |
| `RequestPlaceItem` | C → S | `{itemId: string, position: Vector3}` — **direction hint only** (round-2 B13) | 1/5 s/player | Alive; item in `SquadInventory`; itemId is a placeable type (Signal Anchor — the only placeable post-round-5). Same client-position handling as `RequestBeaconPlace`: clamp → **LOS reachability raycast (round-3 B4)** → raycast-to-floor → overlap check; the raw client value is never trusted as an absolute coordinate, and an LOS-obstructed candidate is rejected (`reason="unreachable-placement"`). See E.28 + H.75 + H.83. |
| ~~`RequestActivateRelay`~~ | — | — | — | **REMOVED (round-5, R4-2)** — the Squad Relay was cut; this event and its per-player rate limit, atomic handler order, and `OnOxygenTransferRequest` side effect are retired. |
| `OnInventoryChanged` | S → owning C (personal scope) or squad (shared scope) | `{playerId, scope, itemId, newCount, delta}` | On mutation only | n/a (server-pushed) |
| `OnCraftStarted` | S → C (squad) | `{benchId, crafterId, recipeId, estimatedCompletionTime}` | On BT1/BT2 only | n/a |
| `OnCraftProgressUpdate` | S → owning C only | `{benchId, progress: 0.0..1.0}` | ≤ 5 Hz | n/a |
| `OnCraftCompleted` | S → C (squad) | `{benchId, recipeId, crafterIds: { number }}` | On BT3 only | n/a |
| `OnCraftCancelled` | S → C (squad) | `{benchId, recipeId, reason: string}` | On BT5/BT6 only | n/a |
| `OnBenchArmed` / `OnBenchDisarmed` | S → owning C only | `{benchId}` | On range-cross only | n/a |
| `OnBenchOccupancyChanged` | S → C (squad) | `{benchId, crafterCount}` | On occupancy change only | n/a |
| `OnBeaconPlaced` | S → C (squad) | `{position: Vector3, placerPlayerId: number}` | On BCT2 only | n/a |
| `OnBeaconActivated` | S → C (squad) | `{position: Vector3, activatorPlayerId: number, timestamp: number, windowDurationSeconds: number}` | On BCT3 only | n/a (`windowDurationSeconds` = server-derived `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW`, build-gate O5 — lets the client render the countdown + the Conv #5 final-lead escalation locally) |
| `OnEscapeBeaconActivated` | S → C (squad) | `{activatorPlayerId: number, timestamp: number, windowDurationSeconds: number}` | On BCT3 only — window-**start** cue (HUD countdown + audio Hunt-stem rise). **NOT** the victory trigger (round-2 change) | n/a (`windowDurationSeconds` per `OnBeaconActivated`) |
| `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` | S → C (squad) | `{timestamp: number}` | On BCT4 only — **PC's T8 victory subscription** (round-2). Guarded by `RunSession.beaconWindowSurvived` | n/a |
| `OnBeaconWindowFailed` | S → C (squad) | `{reason: "scatter" \| "wipe", timestamp: number}` (round-16: `reason` typed as the closed union, not bare `string` — network round-14 carryover) | On **either** BC4 defeat sub-case (round-9 single decision point, Blocker #5) — **PC's BC4-defeat subscription**; `reason ∈ {"scatter", "wipe"}` (**round-13 continuous-hold / round-15 holder threshold:** scatter = the hold was broken — members alive, **fewer than `requiredHolders` within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs`** (round-16 fix — this prose previously read the round-13 "none in radius," which a HUD/PC programmer would have implemented as "zero holders," wrong for 3–4-player squads where the threshold is 2) — for the full `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE`, resolvable at any BC4 tick, NOT only window-end; wipe = `aliveMembers` emptied by the departure drain, immediate, no grace). Mutually exclusive with `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`; Crafting fires exactly one window outcome. PC maps both reasons to `RunEnded(defeat)`; PC's all-dead path stays authoritative only outside BC4 | n/a |
| `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged` (round-7 C-2) | S → C (squad) | `{playerId: number, inRadius: boolean}` | On a member crossing the `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` boundary during BC4 only, **with a `BEACON_HOLD_HYSTERESIS` band** (round-11 Conv #4 — enter `<= R`, leave `> R + HYSTERESIS`; not per-Heartbeat, no boundary-jitter flood) | n/a (server-pushed; **per-member positional helper, presentation-only** — does not alter the win-condition evaluation, which uses the strict `<= R` snapshot; round-13: demoted below `OnBeaconLineBreak` as the win cue) |
| `OnBeaconLineBreak` (round-13, B1 fail-fast) | S → C (squad) | `{broken: boolean, timestamp: number, graceSeconds: number}` | Edge-triggered on the strict squad-level HOLDING↔BROKEN transition during BC4 (NO hysteresis — strict `<= R` snapshot, the same the win check uses); `broken=true` carries the break `timestamp` + `graceSeconds = BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE`; `broken=false` on re-establish **carries `graceSeconds = 0`** (round-19 R18-4 — the grace is resolved at re-establish; the round-16 "client IGNORES it" convention is retired). Inherits the grace's jitter-tolerance; not a per-Heartbeat flood | n/a (server-pushed; **authoritative squad-level win-state cue** — drives the HOLDING/BROKEN binary + regroup countdown, surface 13; dissolves ux-B12-2 because it is strict, never hysteresis-banded) |
| `OnBeaconDecayed` | S → C (squad) | `{}` | On BCT5 only (defensive decay fallback; normally unreached — run ends at window-end). NOT fired on run-end cleanup per E.8 | n/a |
| `OnSignalAnchorTripped` | S → C (squad) | `{anchorId, anchorPosition: Vector3}` | On single-shot trip only | n/a |
| `OnSignalAnchorExpired` | S → C (squad) | `{anchorId}` | On lifetime expiry only | n/a |
| `OnEquippedEffectStarted` | S → C (squad — owner sees their own badge; squadmates may see a small indicator) | `{playerId, effectName, equippedAt, expiresAt?}` | On equip event only | n/a |
| `OnEquippedEffectExpired` | S → C (squad) | `{playerId, effectName, reason}` | On consumption / expiry / death only | n/a |
| `OnCraftRejected` | S → owning C only | `{reason: string}` | On rejection only | n/a — **the catch-all rejection channel for ALL non-beacon Crafting requests** (build-gate O6): `RequestCraft`, `RequestCraftCancel`, `RequestUseItem`, `RequestEquipItem` (e.g. `reason="already-equipped"`, H.88), `RequestPlaceItem`. The `reason` enum disambiguates the source. `RequestEquipItem`/`RequestUseItem`/`RequestPlaceItem` do not each carry a dedicated `On*Rejected` event — they all route rejections through this one channel. |
| `OnBeaconRejected` | S → owning C only | `{reason: string}` | On rejection only | n/a — beacon place/activate rejections only (`RequestBeaconPlace`/`RequestBeaconActivate`); separate from `OnCraftRejected` |

**Trust-boundary enumeration vs. game-concept registry**:
- The game-concept's RemoteEvent enumeration (#2 Craft request, #3 Beacon activation) corresponds to this GDD's `RequestCraft` (#2) and `RequestBeaconActivate` (#3). This GDD's surface adds: `RequestCraftCancel`, `RequestBeaconPlace`, `RequestUseItem`, `RequestEquipItem`, `RequestPlaceItem` — operational sub-events of the same crafting trust boundary (round-5: `RequestActivateRelay` removed with the Relay cut). Architecture phase will reconcile via an ADR enumerating the full Crafting trust boundary.
- **Per-player global RemoteEvent budget** (game-concept tech risk row): this GDD adds **7** client-initiated events (round-5: down from 8 with the `RequestActivateRelay` removal). Combined with PC's 4 events + ED's 0 client-initiated + RN's expected 1 (RequestGather) = **12** client-initiated total at MVP. Budget headroom remains for HUD's pings/emotes (already 2 in PC), Predator AI, and future content.

**Per-event rate limits + per-player global rate limit** (game-concept tech risk): each event has a per-player rate limit above. The architecture phase ADR additionally enforces a per-player **global** rate limit across ALL Crafting events combined to defend against the spam-multiple-low-rate-limited-events bypass pattern surfaced in game-concept. Recommended ceiling: `CRAFTING_GLOBAL_RATE_LIMIT = 8 events/s/player` with a **3 s burst window of 16 events** (round-7: aligned the global burst window from 2 s to 3 s to match every per-event burst window — `RequestCraft`/`RequestUseItem`/`RequestEquipItem` are all "burst N per 3 s"; a 2 s global window misaligned with the 3 s per-event windows could trip the global cap on a legitimate retry burst the per-event limits explicitly permit). Verified by H.98.

**Global-limit reachability (round-2 B15, re-summed round-3, re-summed round-5)**: round-1 flagged that a single legitimately-motivated player might reach `8 events/s`. Re-assessed: the global limit is an **abuse backstop, not an expected operating point**. Summing the per-event sustained ceilings for one player — `RequestCraft` (1/s) + `RequestUseItem` (1/s) + `RequestCraftCancel` (1/s) + `RequestEquipItem` (1/s) + `RequestBeaconActivate` (1/s — round-3 per-player flood guard, though it can only *succeed* once/run) + the 1/5-s-gated place/beacon-place events (~0.2/s each ≈ 0.4/s) — yields a worst-case legitimate sustained ceiling of **~5.4 events/s** even for a player frantically cycling every flow at once, still under 8. *(Round-5: down from ~6.4 — the cut `RequestActivateRelay` (1/s) is removed from the sum; the headroom to the 8-event cap widens accordingly.)* The burst window (16 events / 2 s) absorbs legitimate retry-after-rejection bursts (the B12 fix) without tripping. The limit therefore gates only genuine cycle-between-events abuse; no legitimate single-player pattern reaches it. The constant is left at `8` (range 4–16 per G.7) — lowering toward 4 would risk false positives on a player cycling all flows; raising it weakens the backstop. Verified by H.64 (legitimate multi-bench burst stays under) + H.26 (abuse pattern blocked).

#### C.16 — Test-Harness Injection Seam (testability contract — round-3 B8/B9)

Several edge cases and ACs assert **time-dependent or restart-dependent** outcomes that the project determinism standard (`coding-standards.md`: "no time-dependent assertions", "no random seeds", "unit tests do not call external APIs … use dependency injection") forbids testing against the live clock or wall-clock `task.wait`. This subsection declares the **single seam** through which the test harness drives those outcomes deterministically. It is a normative architecture obligation (an ADR in F.4), not optional test scaffolding — the timing ACs are written against it.

`CraftingService` takes its time, tick, position, and departure sources by **dependency injection** rather than calling the globals directly. The injected collaborators (production defaults in parentheses) — the full set; any production handler that bypasses one is a reviewable regression (R4-4):

| Seam | Production default | Test override | Drives |
|---|---|---|---|
| `_clock()` → number | `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` | a controllable clock the test advances explicitly | craft `startTime`, `beaconActivationTime`, all duration/expiry comparisons (Coil, Anchor lifetime, survival window) |
| `_step(dt)` (the Heartbeat tick) | bound to `RunService.Heartbeat` | the test calls `_step(dt)` manually with a fixed `dt` | the D.1 closed-form progress (R4-3); the per-Heartbeat survival-window comparison (B5); the **buffered death/disconnect drain** + the BT3/BCT4-vs-departure tie-break ordering (C.8 / C.9, R4-5) |
| `_simulateRestart()` | no-op (real crashes are external) | discards all in-memory `CraftingService` state and re-initialises, modelling a server process restart | H.74 server-crash (no persistence, no resume, no material loss) |
| `_simulateBindToClose()` (round-7 D-1) | bound to `game:BindToClose` | invokes the `BindToClose` forced-cleanup path deterministically (the test calls it instead of triggering a real server shutdown) | H.90 BindToClose-during-BC4 (forced cleanup, no victory/defeat/decay signal) |
| `_enqueueDeparture(playerId, reason)` (round-5 R4-5 seam; **tabled round-9, Blocker #2** — flagged absent from this table) | bound to the `Humanoid.Died` / `Players.PlayerRemoving` connections + the 5 Hz proximity watcher + the `RequestCraftCancel` effect path; the single fan-out entry point (C.8) | the test calls `_enqueueDeparture(playerId, reason)` directly to model a departure into BOTH the bench `CraftSession._pendingDepartures` and the `RunSession._pendingDepartures` window scope on a chosen tick — no real death/disconnect needed | the C.8/C.9 buffered-departure tie-break + the dual-scope fan-out + the {playerId, scope} de-dup (H.99 / H.101 / H.104); same-tick departure-vs-completion (C.8) and departure-vs-window-survival (C.9) ordering |
| `_enqueueReadd(playerId)` (**re-add seam — round-13 build-gate fix**) | bound to the server-side `Player.CharacterAdded` + positioned-`HumanoidRootPart` path (interim) / PC's `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged` (canonical) | the test calls `_enqueueReadd(playerId)` directly to model a respawn re-add into `RunSession._pendingReadds`, drained in `_step` after the departure drain — no real respawn needed; exercises the same-tick death+respawn-nets-to-alive ordering and the re-add de-dup | the round-13 buffered re-add (H.108 respawn re-add; the death+respawn net-alive ordering) |
| `_memberPosition(playerId) → Vector3` (**position seam — round-9, Blocker #1** — flagged needed for H.94; the gate flagged the *read* side was unnamed) | reads the member's server-tracked `HumanoidRootPart.Position` | the test override (`_setMemberPosition(playerId, position)` sets it) returns an injected value, so `windowAliveInRadius` membership and `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` boundary-crossings are driven deterministically without moving a real character. The BC4 handler reads positions ONLY through this seam — never `HumanoidRootPart.Position` directly | H.94 hold-state boundary-cross; H.99 window-end in/out-of-radius mix; the exact-12.0-stud (`BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`) `<=` boundary (H.92) |

**Rules this seam makes testable deterministically (no tolerance, no playtest):**

- **Clock-elapsed progress (closed form — R4-3, round-5 qa-gate refinement)**: `craftProgress` is computed each tick by the D.1 **clock-elapsed closed form** `clamp(progressAtRateAnchor + ((_clock() − rateAnchorClock) × crafterSpeedMultiplier(N)) / CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME, 0, 1)` — driven by the injected `_clock()`, **not a running sum and not `stepCount × dt`**. A test sets `_clock()` to an exact dwell boundary (e.g. `rateAnchorClock + 10.0` for a 10 s Beacon at N=1) and asserts `craftProgress == 1.0` **exactly** — `10.0/10.0`, `5.0×2/10.0`, `2.0/2.0`, `2.5×2/10.0` are all exactly representable, so the assertion is bit-exact on any IEEE-754 runtime (no dependence on whether `600 × (1/60)` rounds to 10.0). This is the round-5 fix to the round-4 IEEE-754 finding **and** to the variable-`dt` incorrectness of the literal `stepCount × dt` form (Heartbeat `dt` is not constant, so step-count×dt ≠ true elapsed time). H.27/H.28/H.30/H.31/H.39/H.41 assert exact values against the injected `_clock` boundary instead of a `± 0.0002` / `± 0.1 s` tolerance.
- **Heartbeat-aligned window** (B5): because the survival window is a per-`_step` comparison `_clock() - beaconActivationTime >= BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` (never `task.delay`), a test sets `_clock` to exactly `beaconActivationTime + BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` and (optionally) fires a death on the **same** `_step`, then asserts the C.9 tie-break deterministically (H.73/H.76/H.77).
- **Push-rate decoupling** (H.61): the ≤5 Hz `OnCraftProgressUpdate` throttle is driven by `_clock()`, so a test advancing `_clock` over a simulated 1 s observes **exactly** the server-side push-scheduler's emit count (a server-side assertion on the throttle, not a client-side receive count).
- **Restart** (H.74): the test calls `_simulateRestart()` mid-`CraftSession` and asserts the bench is `BS1`, no session survived, no material was deducted, and no `DataStoreService` call was made.
- **Graceful shutdown** (H.90, round-7 D-1): the test calls `_simulateBindToClose()` while the beacon is in BC4 and asserts the forced-cleanup path runs (Beacon BasePart torn down; no `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` / `OnBeaconWindowFailed` / `OnBeaconDecayed`; no victory/defeat awarded; handler returns without blocking on the window timer).
- **Buffered departures + injected positions** (round-9): `_enqueueDeparture(playerId, reason)` injects a same-tick departure into both the bench and window scopes (H.99 / H.101) and exercises the `{playerId, scope}` de-dup so a double-fire cannot double-drain (H.104); `_setMemberPosition(playerId, position)` drives `windowAliveInRadius` membership, the `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged` boundary-crossings (H.94), and the exact-`12.0`-stud (`BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`) `<=` boundary (H.92) with no live character. Because `RunSession.aliveMembers` is mutated only by the drain + respawn (never by a live `Humanoid` read), "alive" is asserted deterministically (H.103).
- **Monotonic clock guard** (round-9, H.102): `_clock()` is contractually **monotonic non-decreasing** (the production default wraps `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` in a `_lastClock = max(_lastClock, GetServerTimeNow())` clamp so an NTP/VM-migration backward slew never surfaces). A test sets `_clock()` to a value **below** a prior reading and asserts craft progress does not reverse and the survival window still completes once the clock recovers (the `max(0, …)` defense-in-depth in D.1 / C.9 means a backward delta is treated as zero elapsed, never negative).

**Forbidden (narrowed to handler bodies — round-5 R4-4)**: a **production handler body** (any executable server code path that runs on a Heartbeat or in a RemoteEvent handler) MUST NOT read `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`, `os.time()`, `tick()`, or `os.clock()` directly (per ED.D.1) — it MUST route every time read through `_clock()` so tests are hermetic. A handler that reads the global clock directly instead of `_clock()` is a regression reviewers MUST reject (it re-introduces a non-injectable time dependency).

**Explicitly NOT a violation (the round-4 self-contradiction fix):** *documentation and schema-comment references* to `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` as the production default that `_clock()` resolves to are **fine** — specifically the "Production default" column of the seam table above, the F.5 engine-dependency table row, and the C.4/C.10 schema comments (which now read `_clock()` with the production default named in parentheses). Those describe what `_clock()` *is* in production; they are not executable handler code reading the global. Round-4 flagged that C.16's blanket prohibition contradicted C.9 BCT3 step 2 (which instructed `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` directly) and the C.10/C.4 schema comments; round-5 resolves it by (a) rewriting the BCT3 step-2 **handler instruction** to `_clock()`, (b) rewriting the schema comments to `_clock()` (default named), and (c) narrowing this prohibition to handler bodies. The seam is the only sanctioned source of time and tick *in executable code*.

#### C.17 — Squad-Size Assumption & Solo-Play (round-3)

**Solo-play (a run that *starts* with squad size 1) is NOT a supported MVP configuration.** `MIN_SQUAD_SIZE = 2` is enforced **at run start by the upstream run-start / matchmaking gate** (out of this system's scope per F.7) — `CraftingService` may assume `squadSize >= 2` at `RunStarted`, and `benchMaxCraftersEffective = min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(squadSize/2))` (C.3.5) is therefore always ≥ 1 with at least one watcher in a 2-player squad. This matches the 2–4-player game-concept and removes the round-3 "solo-play unaddressed" blocker (trivial BCT-DEFEAT, inventory-lock soft-lock, fantasy collapse) by declaring the configuration unsupported rather than authoring degenerate rules for it.

**A run MAY still drop to one alive member mid-run** (death / disconnect) — that is the normal late-run survival state, not solo-play, and it is already fully handled:

1. The lone survivor crafts from **their own `PersonalInventory`**, which is never locked (the dead-player inventory lock, C.3.6, applies only to *dead* players' materials — it cannot soft-lock a living player out of their own materials). Dead teammates' locked materials are simply unavailable; the survivor is not blocked from crafting what their own pool affords.
2. The **squad shared item pool** (C.4) persists across teammate deaths, so any already-crafted Beacon / aid items remain usable by the lone survivor.
3. A lone survivor can still satisfy the victory clause: with one member alive, `requiredHolders = ceil(1/2) = 1`, so a single *held* member (alive AND within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs` at window-end) wins (round-15 — the holder threshold scales with the living count, so it never makes a lone survivor's win harder than the round-13 `>= 1` rule). The win condition requires at least half the *living* squad held, not a fixed count. A lone survivor's death during BC4 is BCT-DEFEAT (wipe), as intended.

`MIN_SQUAD_SIZE` is registered (G, registry) as a value Crafting *reads* (for the cap formula and for an assertion that the assumption holds) but does **not** own — the enforcement lives upstream. See E.30 + H.84.

## Formulas

### D.1 — Craft Progress (per-Heartbeat update)

Server-authoritative **from-start closed-form** progress evaluator running while a `CraftSession` is active (bench states BS2/BS3). Client receives `OnCraftProgressUpdate` at ≤ 5 Hz for visual rendering; the server's value is canonical. Drives BT3 (`craftProgress >= 1.0`).

**Closed form (round-5 R4-3, refined per the round-5 qa-lead gate — clock-elapsed, replaces the round-3 per-tick accumulator):**

```
craftProgress = clamp(
    progressAtRateAnchor
    + (max(0.0, _clock() − rateAnchorClock) × crafterSpeedMultiplier(N)) / CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME,
    0.0, 1.0)
```

**Backward-time-slew guard (round-9, Blocker #4):** the elapsed term is `max(0.0, _clock() − rateAnchorClock)`, never a raw subtraction. This — together with the C.16 contract that **`_clock()` is monotonic non-decreasing** (the production default wraps `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` in a `_lastClock = max(_lastClock, GetServerTimeNow())` clamp, so an NTP correction or VM live-migration that steps the wall clock backward never surfaces to a handler) — guarantees a backward slew can only ever *freeze* progress for that interval, never **reverse** it or make `craftProgress` go negative/`NaN`. The same `max(0, …)` form guards the survival-window comparison in C.9 (`max(0, _clock() − beaconActivationTime) >= BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW`), so a slew during BC4 cannot deny a legitimately-earned victory — the window simply resumes counting from where it froze once the clock recovers. Round-8 flagged that the prior raw `_clock() − rateAnchorClock` could go negative under slew (freezing craft and denying BCT4) and that C.16 only tested a monotonically-advanced injected clock; the monotonic-clock contract + this clamp close both, and H.102 tests a backward-step injection asserting no reversal and eventual completion.

Each Heartbeat (`_step(dt)`, C.16) while in BS2/BS3, the handler does: (1) **recompute** `craftProgress` fresh from the closed form above using the *current* server time `_clock()` (it is **not** accumulated, and it does **not** multiply a stored step-count by `dt`); (2) if `craftProgress >= 1.0` → BT3. On a rate change (BT2 join / BT4 departure), the handler sets `progressAtRateAnchor = craftProgress` (the current closed-form value) and `rateAnchorClock = _clock()`, then recomputes `crafterSpeedMultiplier(N)` at the new `N` — so the formula is **piecewise** across rate segments but within each segment it is a single from-start elapsed-time computation, never a running sum.

**Why clock-elapsed, not `stepCount × dt` (round-5 qa-lead gate fix):** CD ruling R4-3 specified the from-start form `progress = craftRate × stepCount × dt` to kill the round-4 IEEE-754 accumulation drift (the round-3 accumulator `progress += craftRate×dt` did one float addition per Heartbeat — 300–600 of them — drifting ±1–2 ULP so BT3 could fire on the wrong step). The round-5 qa-lead gate found the *literal* `stepCount × dt` form has **two** problems R4-3's intent did not want: (a) **it is incorrect under variable `dt`** — Heartbeat `dt` is not constant (typical 1/60 s, up to 1/30 s on slow servers, and jittery frame-to-frame), so `storedStepCount × currentDt` is not the true elapsed time (300 ticks at 1/60 then 300 at 1/30 is 15 s of real time, but `600 × (1/30) = 20`); and (b) **`600 × (1/60) / 10 == 1.0` is not guaranteed exact** in IEEE-754 double on the production runtime. Both are fixed by driving progress from **elapsed server time** `(_clock() − rateAnchorClock)` instead of step×dt: it uses the *real* wall-clock interval (correct for any `dt`), it is a single subtract-multiply-divide each tick (no accumulation, drift bounded to one rounding regardless of tick count), and it is **provably exact under test** because the C.16 seam lets the test set `_clock()` to an exact dwell boundary (e.g. `rateAnchorClock + 10.0`), and `10.0 / 10.0`, `5.0 × 2 / 10.0`, `2.0 / 2.0`, `2.5 × 2 / 10.0` are all exactly representable. This is also **consistent with the survival-window timer** (C.5.6, which already uses `_clock() − beaconActivationTime`) — both progress gates are now clock-elapsed comparisons through the same `_clock()` seam. The division is by `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME` (not a multiply by a precomputed reciprocal `1/dwell`, which would add a second rounding).

`craftRate = crafterSpeedMultiplier(N) / CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME` is retained only as the human-readable "fractional progress per second" reported in `OnCraftStarted.estimatedCompletionTime`; it is **not** a canonical state variable (there is no accumulation, no stored step count).

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Progress at last rate anchor | `progressAtRateAnchor` | float | 0.0–1.0 | `CraftSession.progressAtRateAnchor`; 0.0 at BT1; reset to the current `craftProgress` on each BT2/BT4 |
| Clock at last rate anchor | `rateAnchorClock` | float | server time | `CraftSession.rateAnchorClock`; `_clock()` captured at BT1 and re-captured on each BT2/BT4. The segment's elapsed time is `_clock() − rateAnchorClock` |
| Server clock | `_clock()` | float | server time | The C.16 injected clock (production `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`); the test sets it explicitly |
| Per-recipe dwell time | `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME` | float | 2 s – 10 s (locked per recipe in C.2; floor 0.5 s per the D.1 guard) | Seconds to complete the recipe at N=1. Not a global constant — varies per recipe |
| Crafter speed multiplier | `crafterSpeedMultiplier(N)` | float | 1.0–2.0 | Output of D.2; scales effective rate by crafter count. **This is the single canonical name** — the registry's `effective_N` and any `mult(N)` shorthand refer to this same D.2 output (round-5 name-unification) |
| Result | `craftProgress` | float | 0.0–1.0 | Progress after this tick (the canonical server value; also `CraftSession.craftProgress`) |

**Output Range**: `[0.0, 1.0]`, hard clamped. Upper clamp means the formula is idempotent once completion is reached — the tick that crosses 1.0 transitions BT3 atomically and the `CraftSession` is cleared before the next Heartbeat.

**Domain guard — `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME > 0` (round-3 D.1):** the closed form divides by `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME`, so the constant **must be strictly positive**. A value of `0` (e.g. a config typo, or a hypothetical "instant craft" cosmetic that the cosmetic-boundary rule already forbids) would yield division by zero → `craftProgress = inf` → clamps to 1.0 on the first tick → **instant craft, no dwell, no exposure window** (a Pillar-1/Pillar-3 break and a balance exploit). The mitigation is a **startup config-validation invariant**: at `CraftingService` init, every recipe's `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME` is asserted `>= CRAFT_DWELL_TIME_FLOOR = 0.5 s` (the G.3 coupled-tuning floor; below this the multi-crafter dwell differentiation also collapses). A recipe failing the assertion is a hard startup error — the service refuses to start rather than run with a degenerate rate (fail-fast, not silently). `crafterSpeedMultiplier(N)` is `min(N, cap) >= 1` by construction (D.2), so it can never be the zero factor. Verified by H.82.

**Worked example** (Escape Beacon, N=1): the test sets `_clock() = rateAnchorClock + 10.0` (the 10 s dwell) → `craftProgress = (10.0 × 1) / 10.0 == 1.0` **exactly** (10.0 / 10.0 is exact) → BT3 fires. Mid-craft check: `_clock() = rateAnchorClock + 5.0` → `(5.0)/10.0 == 0.5` exactly. N=2: `_clock() = rateAnchorClock + 5.0` → `(5.0 × 2)/10.0 == 1.0` exactly (completes in half the wall-clock). Oxygen Canister (2 s dwell, N=1): `_clock() = rateAnchorClock + 2.0` → `2.0/2.0 == 1.0`. All boundaries (1.0, 0.5, 2.0, 5.0, 2.5) are exactly representable, so the assertions are bit-exact on any IEEE-754 runtime — no dependence on whether `600 × (1/60)` happens to round to 10.0. In production with jittery `dt`, the elapsed `_clock() − rateAnchorClock` is the *real* time regardless of per-frame `dt`, so the wall-clock dwell is honored exactly.

### D.2 — Multi-Crafter Dwell-Time Reduction

Derives the effective craft rate when N players are at the same bench. Applied by D.1 as `crafterSpeedMultiplier(N)`.

`crafterSpeedMultiplier(N) = min(N, benchMaxCraftersEffective)`

Equivalently: `dwell_effective = CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME / min(N, benchMaxCraftersEffective)`

where `benchMaxCraftersEffective = min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(squadSize / 2))` (C.3.5) — the squad-scaled cap (round-2). In a 2-player squad this is 1, so `crafterSpeedMultiplier ≡ 1.0` (no multi-crafter speedup is possible — BS3 is unreachable); in 3–4-player squads it is 2.

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Current crafter count | `N` | int | 1–`benchMaxCraftersEffective` | `|CraftSession.crafters|` at the current Heartbeat; sourced from `CraftSession.lastCrafterCount` |
| Effective per-bench cap | `benchMaxCraftersEffective` | int | 1–2 | `min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(squadSize/2))`; computed at run start; enforced by BT2 entry guard |
| Max crafters constant (ceiling) | `BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS` | int | 2 (locked) | Hard ceiling on the effective cap |
| Result (multiplier) | `crafterSpeedMultiplier` | float | 1.0–2.0 | Multiplier applied to `craftRate` in D.1 |
| Result (effective dwell) | `dwell_effective` | float | `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME / 2` – `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME` s | Actual wall-clock completion time |

**Output Range**: discrete set `{1.0, 2.0}` under MVP rules (and `{1.0}` only, in a 2-player squad). The `min()` clamp guards against any race condition that could yield N > cap — BT2's pre-transition guard is the primary defence; this clamp is the secondary.

**Rate-change on BT2/BT4 (closed-form anchor reset — round-5 R4-3)**: when `N` changes mid-craft, the handler captures the *current* `craftProgress` into `CraftSession.progressAtRateAnchor`, sets `CraftSession.rateAnchorClock = _clock()`, and updates `lastCrafterCount = N` so the D.1 clock-elapsed closed form continues from the preserved progress at the new multiplier. Progress is preserved; only the going-forward rate changes; no retroactive credit. The reset is what makes the closed form piecewise-exact across rate changes (one from-start elapsed-time segment per rate, so H.31's 5.0 s-then-2.5 s split lands on `1.0` exactly).

**Worked example** (Escape Beacon, 10 s base dwell): N=1 → `craftRate = 0.100/s`, dwell 10 s. N=2 → `craftRate = 0.200/s`, dwell 5 s. If the second crafter joins at `progress = 0.5` (5 s elapsed at N=1), remaining = `0.5 / 0.200 = 2.5 s`; total wall-clock = 7.5 s — faster than solo 10 s, slower than full N=2 5 s. Demonstrates "progress preserved, rate changed" semantics.

### D.3 — Multi-Crafter Disturbance Emission at Completion

A dispatch rule, not a scalar formula. At BT3 (craft completion) with `N` crafters in `CraftSession.crafters`, the server fires `N` independent `DisturbanceService:Emit` calls — one per crafter — each carrying the recipe's full base burst magnitude.

```
for each crafterId_i in CraftSession.crafters:
    DisturbanceService:Emit(
        emissionType     = "Craft",
        position         = benchPosition,        -- immutable world position; NOT player HumanoidRootPart
        initialMagnitude = recipeBurstMagnitude, -- from C.2 per-recipe constant
        sourcePlayerId   = crafterId_i
    )
```

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Crafter count | `N` | int | 1–2 | `|CraftSession.crafters|` at BT3 moment |
| Per-recipe burst magnitude | `recipeBurstMagnitude` | float | 0.10–0.35 | `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_*` constant from C.2 for the completing recipe; same value used for all N calls |
| Bench world position | `benchPosition` | Vector3 | world-space (XZ significant) | Immutable; set at level-design time |
| Total field contribution at bench (instantaneous) | `totalContribution` | float | `N × recipeBurstMagnitude × falloff(0)` ≈ `N × recipeBurstMagnitude` | At `dist2D = 0`, falloff = 1.0; bench position receives the full sum immediately post-emission |

**Output Range**: each individual emission is bounded `[0.10, 0.35]` — well below ED.C.1.2's reserved-band ceiling of 0.85. N=2 worst-case raw sum: `2 × 0.35 = 0.70` at the bench — still below 0.85, reserved-band compliant.

**Attribution rationale**: individual `sourcePlayerId` per emission preserves the ED attribution chain (C.11.1) and per-player analytics. It does not create an exploit because per-emission magnitudes are locked recipe constants, not user-supplied.

**Worked example** (Escape Beacon, N=2, `recipeBurstMagnitude = 0.35`): two `Emit` calls fire atomically at BT3. At the bench position post-emission: `fieldValue ≥ min(1.0, 0.35 + 0.35) = 0.70` (Hunt-tier spike, `t > HUNT_THRESHOLD = 0.65`). A solo crafter on the same recipe produces `t = 0.35` (Tense tier) — the coordination cost to the squad for going 2× faster is a Hunt-floor lure window instead of a Tense spike.

### D.4 — Composite Patch Gather-Magnitude Downgrade — *REMOVED (round-2, 2026-05-30)*

> The Composite Patch recipe was cut (C.2 round-2 catalog revision). This formula and its `PATCH_DOWNGRADE_TABLE` lookup are removed. The section header is retained (numbering preserved so `D.6` / `D.7` and their citations are unaffected). Resource Node no longer applies any gather-magnitude downgrade — it emits the nominal node-tier magnitude (`MAGNITUDE_GATHER_LIGHT/MEDIUM/HEAVY`) directly. The H.34 / H.35 ACs that tested this formula are removed in Phase B.

### D.5 — Quiet Step Wrap Sprint-Magnitude Reduction — *REMOVED (round-2, 2026-05-30)*

> The Quiet Step Wrap recipe was cut (C.2 round-2 catalog revision). This formula and `QUIET_STEP_WRAP_REDUCTION` are removed; the constant is deleted from G.5 in Phase B. The section header is retained (numbering preserved). With the Wrap gone, the only remaining emission modifier is the Dampener Coil (D.6), and the canonical order-of-operations collapses to the single 2-stage chain in D.6a. The H.36 AC and the E.15 edge case that tested the Wrap × grace-window interaction are removed in Phase B.

### D.6 — Dampener Coil Light-Magnitude Reduction

Applied to ALL Light pulse emissions fired by the equipped player while the Coil is active (duration `DAMPENER_DURATION = 60 s`, or until next bench visit or death). Each covered pulse is reduced; the Coil is not consumed per pulse — it expires on time/condition.

`effective_light_magnitude = MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE × (1 − DAMPENER_REDUCTION)`

where `DAMPENER_REDUCTION = 0.50` (50 % reduction).

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Base light magnitude | `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE` | float | 0.08 (locked, ED registry) | Nominal light pulse magnitude before any modification |
| Coil reduction factor | `DAMPENER_REDUCTION` | float | 0.50 (tunable; range 0.30–0.70) | Fractional reduction applied to every Light pulse while active |
| Coil active duration | `DAMPENER_DURATION` | float | 60 s (locked, C.2) | Real-time remaining for the Coil effect; tracked via `EquippedEffects[playerId].dampenerCoil.expiresAt` |
| Result | `effective_light_magnitude` | float | 0.024–0.056 over tuning range | Magnitude forwarded to `DisturbanceService:Emit` for each covered Light pulse |

**Output Range**: at default tunings, `0.08 × 0.50 = 0.04`. Over the **interim** tuning range `[0.30, 0.56]` (round-11) output spans `[0.0352, 0.056]`; over the post-PC range `[0.30, 0.70]` it spans `[0.024, 0.056]`. The lower bound at 0.70 (0.024) approximates a stationary-attenuated Light pulse (PC D.4) — *if Stage-2 stacks* — so at `DAMPENER_REDUCTION = 0.70` the Coil's effect would match maximum stationary attenuation even while moving, making the moving lantern nearly silent. **That silencing only materializes once PC applies the D.6a Stage-2 factor (PC in MAJOR REVISION — unverified-until-PC, D.6a); until then a moving Coil player at 0.70 emits 0.024, above `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02` — NOT silent.** This is why the ceiling is held at an **interim 0.56** (round-11, systems BLOCK-3), with 0.70 the post-PC ceiling, not an interim recommendation (G.5).

**Non-linear threshold effect**: a stationary Coil-equipped player's effective Light pulse is `0.04 × STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR = 0.04 × 0.30 = 0.012` — below `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02`. The pulse expires immediately. The Coil's true value at default tunings is *making stationary lantern-bearers effectively silent* in the disturbance field, not the linear 50 % reduction.

**Worked example**: player equips Dampener Coil at run-second 60; raises lantern. Light pulses at seconds 64, 68, 72, …, 116 are each: `effective_light_magnitude = 0.08 × 0.50 = 0.04`. At second 120 (`expiresAt = 60 + 60 = 120 s`), Coil expires. Pulse at second 120: nominal 0.08. The 15 covered pulses each cost the squad 0.04 instead of 0.08 — a half-price lantern window for 60 real seconds.

### D.6a — Canonical Emission-Modifier Order of Operations

**This is the single, normative pipeline for computing any modified emission magnitude.** Round-1 flagged the modifier order-of-operations as non-deterministic across four review angles (Wrap × grace-window contradiction, Coil × stationary-attenuation order, midpulse attribution, yield-contingent race). The round-2 catalog cut removed the Composite Patch and Quiet Step Wrap, leaving the **Dampener Coil** as the *only* emission-magnitude modifier this GDD owns. The four-angle ambiguity therefore collapses to one declared 2-stage chain.

**Canonical chain** (applied to any pulse emission whose owner has gameplay modifiers active):

```
magnitude_final = floor_discard(
    base_magnitude                       -- nominal pulse magnitude (ED registry; e.g. MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE = 0.08)
    × gameplay_modifier                  -- Stage 1: Dampener Coil ×(1 − DAMPENER_REDUCTION) if active on a Light pulse; else ×1.0
    × pc_stationary_attenuation,         -- Stage 2: PC.D.4 stationary factor (×STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR = 0.30 when stationary; else ×1.0)
    MAGNITUDE_FLOOR                      -- discard: if result < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02, the emission is NOT fired (ED zero-magnitude rejection)
)
```

> **`floor_discard()` is NOT `math.clamp` (round-5 rename, was `clamp_floor`):** a below-floor result is **dropped (no emission)**, not clamped *up* to `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`. The round-3 name `clamp_floor` read like `math.clamp(x, MAGNITUDE_FLOOR, ∞)` (which would *emit* a floor-valued pulse) — the opposite of the intent. The operation is: `if (product < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR) then no-emit else emit(product)`.

**Stage order is fixed and total:**

| Stage | Operator | Owner | Applies to | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 — Base | `base_magnitude` | ED registry | all pulses | The nominal per-action magnitude. |
| 1 — Gameplay modifier | `× (1 − DAMPENER_REDUCTION)` | Crafting (D.6) | Light pulses only, Coil active | The only Crafting-owned modifier post-round-2. If no Coil (or non-Light pulse), factor is 1.0. |
| 2 — PC stationary attenuation | `× STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` | Player Controller (PC.D.4) | any pulse, when the emitter is stationary | Movement-state attenuation. Independent of Stage 1. |
| 3 — Floor discard | `< MAGNITUDE_FLOOR → no emit` | ED (zero-magnitude rejection) | all | The pulse is dropped, not clamped to the floor. |

**Why Stage 1 before Stage 2**: the two factors are independent multipliers, so the *product* is order-independent arithmetically — but the **discard decision** (Stage 3) is evaluated once, on the final product, after both factors apply. Declaring Stage 1 → Stage 2 → Stage 3 as the canonical sequence removes the round-1 ambiguity about whether a gameplay modifier could push a pulse below floor "before" or "after" stationary attenuation: it is always the single final product that is floor-tested. This is the resolution of round-1 blockers B5/B6/B7 and the missing E.15 AC (E.15 itself is removed with the Wrap, but the Coil × stationary case it half-covered is now the canonical worked example below).

**Worked example (Coil + stationary)**: a stationary Coil-equipped player's Light pulse: `0.08 × 0.50 (Coil) × 0.30 (stationary) = 0.012 < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02` → **no emission fired** (Stage 3 discard). This matches the "Non-linear threshold effect" in D.6 and is the single authoritative computation; H.38 in Section H tests exactly this chain. The Coil is **not** consumed by a discarded pulse (the Coil is duration-based, not per-pulse — D.6).

**Stage ownership — who applies which factor (round-7 A-5):** the chain is applied **exactly once, by the single service that publishes that pulse's `DisturbanceService:Emit`** (the pulse owner). The round-6 panel flagged Stage-2 ownership as undefined — double-apply (both Crafting and PC multiply the stationary factor) or omit (neither does) would break the Coil floor-discard. Ruling: **the pulse's publishing service applies the whole chain in one place.** For a **Light pulse** (the only pulse a Coil modifies), the pulse owner is **Player Controller** (PC.D.4 fires the lantern Light pulse); PC applies **both** Stage 1 (reading whether the emitter has an active `EquippedEffects[playerId].dampenerCoil` — Crafting-owned *data* PC reads, not a second multiply) **and** Stage 2 (its own `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR`), in the single canonical chain, then Stage 3 floor-discards the final product. Crafting **does not** separately multiply the stationary factor onto any Light pulse — Crafting owns the Coil's Stage-1 *factor value* (D.6) and exposes the active-Coil state; it does not own the Light pulse's emission, so it never applies Stage 2. For a **Craft burst** (bench completion, midpulse, or the round-7 in-window Canister use) the pulse owner is Crafting, and Craft bursts are **not** subject to Stage 1 (no Coil modifier on a Craft burst) and are emitted at full tier magnitude regardless of emitter movement (a bench/Canister burst is a discrete event, not a movement-attenuated continuous pulse) — so Stage 2 does not apply to Craft bursts either. Net: the stationary factor is applied once, by PC, on Light pulses only; never by Crafting; never twice. Cross-ref PC.D.4. The Stage-1+Stage-2 chained product (H.38) is therefore **unverified-until-PC** for its Stage-2 half (PC must apply the factor in this canonical chain when PC's revision lands); the Stage-1 Coil half is testable now.

**Determinism guarantee**: because there is exactly one modifier and one declared chain, the project-standard determinism rule (`coding-standards.md` testing determinism) is satisfiable — the same inputs always produce the same `magnitude_final`, testable without playtest.

### D.7 — Beacon Mid-Craft Pulse Trigger

A conditional single-emission rule applied during the Escape Beacon's craft session only. The trigger is idempotent — fires exactly once per craft session regardless of N or mid-craft rate changes.

```
if CraftSession.recipeId == "Beacon"
   AND craftProgress >= CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_PROGRESS
   AND CraftSession.midpulseFired == false:

    DisturbanceService:Emit(
        emissionType     = "Craft",
        position         = benchPosition,
        initialMagnitude = MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE = 0.18,
        sourcePlayerId   = CraftSession.primaryCrafterId   -- stable BT1 initiator; NOT crafters[1] (which can shift on BT4 departure — round-2 B7); not duplicated for N=2
    )
    CraftSession.midpulseFired = true
```

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Midpulse progress threshold | `CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_PROGRESS` | float | 0.5 (tunable; range 0.4–0.6) | Fractional progress at which the pulse fires |
| Midpulse magnitude | `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE` | float | 0.18 (locked, C.2) | Emission magnitude of the mid-craft pulse; equals `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_MID` |
| Idempotency flag | `CraftSession.midpulseFired` | bool | {false, true} | Set true after firing; prevents re-fire in same session |
| Attribution | `CraftSession.primaryCrafterId` | int (UserId) | — | Stable BT1 initiator (round-2 B7 — NOT `crafters[1]`, which can shift if the initiator departs via BT4); midpulse uses single attribution, not N |

**Output Range**: zero or one emission per craft session. The `midpulseFired` flag makes the trigger boolean. Does not fire if Beacon craft is cancelled before threshold.

**Wall-clock timing** (proportional, not time-absolute):

| N | `dwell_effective` | Wall-clock at midpulse (progress = 0.5) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 10.0 s | ~5.0 s |
| 2 | 5.0 s | ~2.5 s |

At N=2, the squad hears the bench twice in short succession (midpulse at 2.5 s, completion burst at 5.0 s) — concentrating the crafting noise into 2.5 seconds rather than spreading over 10 s. Multi-crafter Beacon crafting is faster but acoustically denser.

**Tuning note**: `CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_PROGRESS = 0.5` creates a halftime beat — the squad hears the bench at the midpoint and again at completion, two equal warning beats. Below 0.4 fires the midpulse too early; above 0.6 clusters midpulse and completion too tightly.

## Edge Cases

This section enumerates 32 live edge cases (numbered through E.34; E.14 + E.15 retired in the round-2 catalog cut) organized into 14 categories. Each case specifies the triggering condition, the exact server-authoritative outcome, the rule from C/D it protects, and any cross-GDD interaction. **[P0]** marks exploit / race / data-corruption risks; unmarked entries are degenerate-strategy or attribution-correctness cases. Design-gap resolutions applied per the systems-designer review (2026-04-30) are noted inline. The round-2 patch (2026-06-01) added E.24–E.26 for the Beacon survival window; the round-3 authoring pass (2026-06-01) added E.27–E.31 (scatter-defeat, cross-wall placement, BT3 tie-break, lone-survivor, server-shutdown); round-7 added E.32 (in-window Canister); round-16 added E.33 (respawn holder-threshold edge); round-19 added E.34 (position-replication trust gap / FENCE — R18-3).

### Inventory & Equipped Effects

**E.1 — Equipped Dampener Coil owner dies mid-effect**
- **If**: Player has an active `DampenerCoil` (within `DAMPENER_DURATION = 60 s` window) and dies (PC.T5 fires).
- **Then**: Atomically on the same Heartbeat as `Humanoid.Died`, before any pending input from that tick is processed: clear `EquippedEffects[playerId].dampenerCoil`; do **NOT** return the Coil to `SquadInventory`; fire `OnEquippedEffectExpired(playerId, "DampenerCoil", reason="death")`.
- **Why**: Per C.6, the Dampener Coil is *consumed on equip* — it is the only equippable item in the round-2 catalog. Death after equip therefore does not return the Coil to the squad pool: it was already spent at equip-time, and the active effect simply terminates. Documents the consume-on-equip semantic so implementers do not mistakenly add a death-return path for it.
- **Cross-system**: PC.S4 entry. Atomicity guarantee per gap-resolution G2.

### Bench Occupancy

**E.2 — Third player attempts to join an in-progress craft**
- **If**: Bench is in BS3 (two crafters active) and a third player fires `RequestCraft(recipeId)` for the same recipe.
- **Then**: Reject with `OnCraftRejected(reason="bench-full")`. Bench remains armed for the third player (no `OnBenchDisarmed` fires) — they may retry once a crafter departs.
- **Why**: Enforces the effective cap `benchMaxCraftersEffective = min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(squadSize/2))` (C.3.5) — the hard ceiling `BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS = 2` is reached only in 3–4-player squads; in a 2-player squad the effective cap is 1 and even a *second* joiner is rejected here. Without explicit rejection an over-cap `sourcePlayerId` would enter D.3's emission loop, producing an extra independent emission and breaking the dwell-rate model.

**E.3 — Join attempt at exactly `craftProgress = 0.85`**
- **If**: A second player fires `RequestCraft(recipeId)` and server-side `craftProgress` is exactly `0.85` at validation moment.
- **Then**: Reject with `OnCraftRejected(reason="too-late-to-join")`. The cutoff uses **strict less-than** (`craftProgress < CRAFT_JOIN_CUTOFF`); `0.849999...` admits, `0.85` does not.
- **Why**: Floating-point boundary contract. A `<=` mistake would let a player join in the final 15% and receive D.3 attribution for trivial contribution.

**E.4 — Simultaneous join attempts from two players**
- **If**: Two players fire `RequestCraft(recipeId)` for the same recipe on the same Heartbeat while bench is BS2 with `craftProgress < 0.85`.
- **Then**: Roblox Luau's single-threaded server execution sequences them. First admitted (BS2 → BS3, N=2). Second now sees `|crafters| = BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS` and is rejected with `bench-full`.
- **Why**: The single-threaded scheduler is the architectural race-guard. No mutex required. Implementations MUST NOT introduce async patterns (coroutines yielding inside the handler) that break the guarantee.

**E.5 — Original crafter cancels late while joiner has passed cutoff**
- **If**: Bench is in BS3 with `craftProgress = 0.90`. Crafter[1] fires `RequestCraftCancel`.
- **Then**: BT4 fires: crafter[1] removed from `CraftSession.crafters`; bench → BS2 with crafter[2] at progress 0.90. When crafter[2] reaches BT3, **only crafter[2]'s emission fires** — D.3 iterates `CraftSession.crafters` at the moment of BT3, not historical members.
- **Why**: Closes the "join, leave at 0.84, let the other complete" deserter exploit — the leaver pays nothing, the completer pays the full per-crafter emission. Confirms attribution is snapshotted at BT3.

### Material Deduction Races

**E.6 [P0] — Squad pool drops below recipe cost between BT1 validation and BT3 completion**
- **If**: At BT1 the combined personal pool is sufficient. During the craft window, a concurrent craft on a different bench fires its BT3 first and deducts a material that puts the running craft's pool below cost.
- **Then**: At the running craft's BT3, the atomic deduction fails. BT6 fires: `OnCraftCancelled(benchId, recipeId, reason="insufficient-materials")`. No materials deducted, no item added, no emission. The other (already-completed) craft retains its emission — only the failing craft is rolled back.
- **Why**: Two independent CraftSessions racing for the same shared pool. The HUD must distinguish this mid-craft cancellation from player-initiated cancel and from pre-craft `OnCraftRejected`.
- **Cross-system**: HUD GDD reason taxonomy.

**E.7 [P0] — Solo crafter dies with all Beacon materials in personal inventory**
- **If**: Solo player at bench (BS2), all 8 Beacon materials in their personal inventory, `craftProgress = 0.70`. Player dies.
- **Then**: BT5 fires: `CraftSession` cleared, progress discarded, no emission, no materials deducted. The dead player's `PersonalInventory` is **LOCKED** (per C.3.6) — not cleared. On respawn the inventory unlocks with the full material count intact; the player can re-approach the bench and start over.
- **Why**: Two failure modes prevented: (a) materials permanently lost on death (they aren't — death locks, not wipes); (b) the locked inventory being mistakenly drawn from by a concurrent BT3 deduction on a different bench. The LOCK is the critical guarantee.
- **Cross-system**: PC.S4, C.3.6 inventory-lock, C.10 death-cleanup.

### Beacon Lifecycle

**E.8 — Beacon placed but not activated when squad wipes**
- **If**: Beacon is in BC3 (placed, inert). All squad members enter PC.S4 simultaneously with no alive player within `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 4 studs` of the beacon.
- **Then**: `RunEnded` fires. Beacon BasePart removed from workspace via the **run-end cleanup path**, which is distinct from the defensive decay transition BCT5: `OnBeaconDecayed` MUST NOT fire (BCT5 requires natural source-expiry, not a forced cleanup). `RunSession.beaconPlaced` cleared with the session.
- **Why**: HUD must suppress `OnBeaconDecayed` for run-end removal — otherwise the squad's last frame shows a "beacon decayed" notification on top of the defeat screen, which is misleading. Distinct code path is contractual. *(Round-2 renumber: the decay transition was BCT4 pre-round-2; it is now BCT5 because the new victory transition took the BCT4 slot — see C.9.)*

**E.9 [P0] — Beacon activator dies on the same Heartbeat as activation**
- **If**: Player fires `RequestBeaconActivate` and `Humanoid.Died` fires for them on the same Heartbeat.
- **Then**: Server validates `alive` inside the `RequestBeaconActivate` handler. If validation passes (the Died event has not yet been processed by the server's event order this tick), BCT3 fires atomically: `RunSession.beaconActivated = true`; `DisturbanceService:Emit(magnitude=0.95, sourcePlayerId=RunSession.beaconPlacerId, ...)`; `OnEscapeBeaconActivated` fires (the window-**start** cue). The activating player's death does **NOT** affect attribution — the emission is attributed to `beaconPlacerId`, not the activator. **Activation succeeds and the survival window opens; it does NOT win the run.** `T8` does NOT fire here — victory is awarded only at window-end (BCT4) if at least `requiredHolders = ceil(#aliveMembers/2)` squad members are alive AND within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs` then (C.5.6a, B1, holder-threshold round-15, H.44). The activator's death simply removes one survivor from the window; the run continues. If the activator was the *last* living member, their same-Heartbeat death routes to BCT-DEFEAT (wipe) on the next BC4 evaluation.
- **Why**: Activation must be valid if the Beacon was legitimately activated before or concurrent with death (the `alive` guard is defense-in-depth against a dead player's buffered request completing after their PC state transitions to S4). *(Round-2: pre-patch this edge case fired T8 and ended the run in victory at activation — the root of the round-1 Beacon-dominance finding. Victory now requires surviving the window; this body was reconciled with H.44 in the round-2 follow-up.)*

**E.10 — Attempted second Beacon craft when `RunSession.beaconCrafted = true`**
- **If**: Beacon already crafted (`beaconCrafted = true`) — whether currently in inventory, placed, or activated — and a player fires `RequestCraft(recipeId="EscapeBeacon")`.
- **Then**: BT1 pre-validation rejects with `OnCraftRejected(reason="beacon-already-crafted")`. No `CraftSession` created; no materials deducted.
- **Why**: Once-per-run gate. `RunSession.beaconCrafted` is set true at BC2 and **never reset within a RunSession** — only cleared at `RunEnded`. Documents the invariant any future BC2-rollback bug would violate.

### Multi-Crafter Completion

**E.11 — One crafter cancels at exactly `craftProgress = 0.84`**
- **If**: Bench in BS3 with `craftProgress = 0.84`. Crafter[1] fires `RequestCraftCancel`.
- **Then**: BT4: crafter[1] removed; bench → BS2; crafter[2] continues at 0.84 with N=1 rate. At any later moment while `craftProgress < 0.85`, a *third* player MAY join (BT2 fires again, bench → BS3 with new crafters[]). The cutoff applies to **join decisions**, not to cancel decisions.
- **Why**: BT4 reduces N but does not close the join window. The `< 0.85` rule is the only gate on joining.

**E.12 [P0] — Both crafters die simultaneously at `craftProgress = 0.50`**
- **If**: Both crafters of a BS3 session die on the same Heartbeat.
- **Then**: Death handlers process sequentially per Luau scheduler. First death: BT4 (BS3 → BS2 with crafter[2] still in `crafters`, but they are also dead and will be processed next). Second death: handler MUST check `if removing this crafter empties the array, fire BT5; else fire BT4`. With this check, second death fires BT5: `CraftSession` cleared, no emission, no deduction, both inventories LOCKED.
- **Why**: A naive implementation that always fires BT4 leaves a zombie session in BS2 with no crafters — never resolves. The post-removal emptiness check is the implementation invariant.

**E.13 [P0] — Crafter exits bench radius without firing `RequestCraftCancel`**
- **If**: Player in BS2 walks > `GATHER_PROXIMITY_RADIUS + 2 studs = 6 studs` from the bench while `craftProgress = 0.40`, without sending a cancel RemoteEvent.
- **Then**: A **server-side** proximity watcher running at `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 5` cadence (gap-resolution G5) detects the violation and fires BT5: session cleared, progress discarded, no emission, no deduction, `OnCraftCancelled(reason="out-of-range")`. The check is server-authoritative — never client-reported — to prevent a client from suppressing position updates to maintain a phantom session.
- **Why**: Without server-driven detection, a client that stops sending input while walking away holds an indefinite craft slot. The 5 Hz cadence inherits ED's field-update cadence; an architecture ADR will pin the exact watcher mechanism per gap-resolution G5.

### Aid Item Effects

**E.14, E.15 — *REMOVED (round-2, 2026-06-01)*** — both covered cut recipes: E.14 was the Composite Patch Light-node floor case (D.4), E.15 was the Quiet Step Wrap × `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` interaction (D.5). With the Patch and Wrap cut from the catalog (C.2 round-2 revision), neither edge case exists. The numbers are retired (not reused) so downstream citations resolve unambiguously. The Dampener Coil — the only remaining equippable — is covered by E.1 (death) and the canonical order-of-operations D.6a (its single interaction with PC stationary attenuation, formerly the half-covered concern behind E.15).

### Signal Anchor

**E.16 — Multiple Anchors: second placement while first is live**
- **If**: Squad has two Signal Anchors in inventory. First placed and live (within `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_LIFETIME = 45 s`). A player places the second.
- **Then**: Server cancels the first: (1) remove first Anchor BasePart; (2) fire `OnSignalAnchorExpired(anchorId, reason="replaced")` — the reason enum is `replaced | lifetime | tripped` per gap-resolution G8; (3) spawn second Anchor BasePart; (4) update `ActiveDeployables.signalAnchor`. Second Anchor's item is consumed only on successful placement.
- **Why**: Single-Anchor-at-a-time prevents a perimeter of Anchors around a safe room — that would constitute base-building, violating the game-concept anti-pillar. The cancel-on-place rule is the mechanical enforcement.
- **Cross-system**: HUD GDD branches on `OnSignalAnchorExpired.reason` for distinct UI feedback.

### Disturbance & Attribution

**E.17 — In-window craft emission fires at the BEACON position (round-15 Ruling 3; Hunt-floor cap interaction)**
- **If**: Beacon is in BC4 (active). A squad member (necessarily a roamer outside the hold, since crafting requires standing at a bench) completes a craft at a bench during the survival window.
- **Then**: **Round-15 (Ruling 3 — game F5):** the craft completion burst emits at **`RunSession.beaconWorldPosition`, NOT the bench position** (`DisturbanceService:Emit("Craft", RunSession.beaconWorldPosition, recipeBurstMagnitude, crafterId)` — C.8 BT3 BC4-conditional), exactly parallel to in-window Canister **use** (E.32). It is therefore subject to ED.C.3.6's Hunt-floor cap at the beacon position like any other beacon-position emission (the cap is position-specific — it clamps `fieldValue(beaconPosition)`; an in-window craft burst now lands there and is capped with it). **Outside BC4, craft bursts emit at `benchPosition` as normal (D.3) — the BC4-conditional only redirects in-window crafts.**
- **Why**: Round-15 F5 — closes the in-window scatter-to-craft-quietly line. Under continuous-hold a roamer who left the hold to craft would otherwise make noise "somewhere else" (a bench hotspot away from the beacon, cheaper relative to the committed predator than crafting at the beacon) — an inconsistency, since in-window **use** already emits at the beacon (E.32). Routing in-window **craft** noise to the beacon too means *any* noise the squad makes during the finale draws to the beacon the predator is already committed to. *(The round-7 "deliberate distraction vector at the bench" framing applied to the pre-round-15 bench-position emission and is retired for in-window crafts — a predator committed to the beacon lure does not chase a secondary bench hotspot during BC4 anyway.)*
- **Cross-system**: C.8 BT3 (BC4-conditional emission position); E.32 (parallel in-window use rule); ED.C.3.6 (cap rule), ED.E.25 (cap engagement); verified by H.52.

**E.18 — N=2 crafters complete simultaneously: two emissions at same position**
- **If**: BS3 reaches BT3. D.3 fires two `Emit` calls — both at `benchPosition`, same `initialMagnitude`, different `sourcePlayerId`.
- **Then**: Per ED.C.1.5 + ED.E.3, the two sources do **NOT** merge in the live-source list — they are independent attributable sources. Bench position `fieldValue` immediately post-emission: `min(1.0, 2 × initialMagnitude)`. The hotspot dedup grid (`HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE = 4 studs`) collapses them to a single hotspot **candidate** for predator targeting, but both sources decay independently in the live-source list. Attribution preserved per crafter.
- **Why**: Confirms D.3 dispatch produces two-emission output and ED handles it correctly. Surface so future implementers don't add a "same position, same tick → merge" optimization that would corrupt attribution.

### Player Controller Interactions

**E.19 — Player dies while registered as crafter: emission attribution**
- **If**: Player is in `CraftSession.crafters` and dies mid-craft. (The session continues if N was 2 before the death; cancels if N was 1 — covered by E.7 / E.12.)
- **Then**: When N=2 becomes 1, BT4 removes the dead player from `CraftSession.crafters`. At BT3 of the surviving crafter, D.3's emission loop iterates only the surviving member — the dead player receives **no completion emission attribution**. Their personal inventory is LOCKED until respawn; the BT3 deduction draws from alive squad members per E.7's order.
- **Why**: Attribution is determined by the *array contents at the moment of BT3*, not by historical membership. A dead crafter who left at BT4 is not attributed. The disturbance log correctly records the completion as the surviving crafter's action.

### Server-Authority & Exploit Attempts

**E.20 [P0] — Client claims craft completion early (timing attack on RequestUseItem)**
- **If**: A malicious client fires `RequestUseItem` for an item before the server's BT3 has fired (item is not yet in `SquadInventory`).
- **Then**: Validation: `item in SquadInventory AND count >= 1` fails. Reject with `OnCraftRejected(reason="item-unavailable")`. Server BT3 timeline is unaffected; no item effect fires; no emission.
- **Why**: The server is the sole authority for `SquadInventory`. Items do not exist until BT3's atomic creation step. Defense is the inventory-presence check; rate limit is the secondary throttle.

**E.21 [P0] — Client claims material count mismatch (inventory inflation attack)**
- **If**: A malicious client fires `RequestCraft(recipeId="EscapeBeacon")` while their server-side personal inventory has insufficient materials.
- **Then**: BT1 reads `PersonalInventory[playerId]` directly from server-authoritative state and computes the alive-squad combined pool. If insufficient, reject with `OnCraftRejected(reason="insufficient-materials")`. **The `RequestCraft` payload contains only `recipeId` (per C.15) — no material-count field exists to spoof.**
- **Why**: Schema-level defense. The server never reads material counts from the request payload. Documents the schema design as the primary exploit closure, not just a runtime check.

**E.22 [P0] — Client fires RequestBeaconActivate before Beacon is placed (false-victory attempt)**
- **If**: A malicious client fires `RequestBeaconActivate` while `RunSession.beaconPlaced = false`.
- **Then**: BCT3 pre-validation requires `beaconPlaced == true AND beaconActivated == false`. Both flags are server-authoritative and never exposed as writable client inputs. Reject with `OnBeaconRejected(reason="beacon-not-placed")`. No emission, no T8, no win.
- **Why**: Two-layer flag validation. An attacker must bypass two server-only flags to trigger a false win — neither is reachable from the client side. The most-exploitable RemoteEvent in the system is closed by schema + flag invariants.

**E.23 — `RequestCraft` and `RequestBeaconActivate` flood (rate limit coverage)**
- **If**: A client floods the server with 50 `RequestCraft` calls in 1 s.
- **Then**: Per-event rate limit (`RequestCraft: 1/s/player sustained; burst 3 per 3 s` — round-2 B12) gates the first wave. After burst exhaustion, excess requests are **silently dropped** (no `OnCraftRejected` per excess to avoid log-flooding the client). The global limit `CRAFTING_GLOBAL_RATE_LIMIT = 8 events/s/player` caps the combined per-player Crafting RemoteEvent traffic, preventing cycle-between-events bypass. Server logs the first excess request per player per second for the ops trail.
- **Why**: ED.E.12's `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES = 500` is the downstream defense against emission spam; this case covers the RemoteEvent layer before any Emit is reached. Two-layer rate-limit pairing per C.15.

### Beacon Survival Window (round-2)

**E.24 [P0] — Full-squad wipe during the survival window (defeat)**
- **If**: Beacon is in BC4 (activated; survival window counting down) and the **last living** squad member departs — `Humanoid.Died` **or `Players.PlayerRemoving`** (clean disconnect — round-5 R4-5) — before `now - beaconActivationTime >= BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW`.
- **Then**: BCT-DEFEAT fires on the **wipe** path (C.9 single decision point): the BC4 Heartbeat handler's buffered departure drain empties `RunSession.aliveMembers`, sets the `runOutcomeResolved` latch, and **fires `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")`** → PC maps it to `RunEnded(defeat)`. The Beacon BasePart is removed via the run-end cleanup path; the survival-window + decay-watch timers are cleared. **`OnBeaconWindowSurvived` does NOT fire** (no victory) and **`OnBeaconDecayed` does NOT fire** (run-end cleanup, not natural decay — consistent with E.8). *(Round-9 change, Blocker #5: pre-round-9 the wipe routed through PC's all-dead `RunEnded(defeat)` path with **no** Crafting signal — the split that produced the round-8 double-fire race; round-9 made Crafting's BC4 handler the single owner of all three outcomes and routes the wipe through `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")`. This "Then" wording was a round-10 reconciliation miss, corrected round-11 with H.89/H.76/C.12.1.)* During BC4 PC suppresses its own all-dead path (round-11 Convergence #2, C.9/C.12.1), so there is no second defeat trigger to race.
- **Why**: Activation is no longer an instant win (round-2 keystone). A squad that rushed the Beacon with no aid preparation can be wiped during its own loud finale — this is the design-intended failure mode that makes the aid suite load-bearing. The "no `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`/`OnBeaconDecayed`" rule prevents a victory cue or a "beacon decayed" notification from painting over the defeat screen.
- **Note (round-3; round-13 update)**: this is the **wipe** defeat path — `aliveMembers` emptied — and it fires **immediately, with NO grace** (no one is left to regroup). The second BCT-DEFEAT path — **scatter / line-break** (the `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot below `requiredHolders` while members remain alive), covered by E.27 — is the one with the `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE` (round-13/15). Both end the run in defeat through the same cleanup and both fire `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason)` (round-9 single decision point). The wipe path takes precedence over an in-progress scatter grace: if everyone dies mid-grace, the run resolves as a wipe (C.9 step c).
- **Cross-system**: C.9 BCT-DEFEAT (wipe) → `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")`; C.12.1 PC subscription + BC4 all-dead suppression; H.76/H.89.

**E.25 [P0] — Last held player dies OR disconnects on the exact window-end Heartbeat (tie-break)**
