- **If**: On a single Heartbeat, for the beacon in BC4: the survival-window timer has elapsed (`_clock() - beaconActivationTime >= BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW`) **AND** the last squad member who is both alive AND within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` **departs on that same tick** — either their `Humanoid.Died` **or their `Players.PlayerRemoving`** (clean disconnect — round-5 R4-5) fires, both buffered into `_pendingDepartures`.
- **Then**: In the single BC4 Heartbeat handler, **window-survival is evaluated FIRST** (C.9 tie-break rule), against the alive-and-in-radius membership **as it stands at the top of the tick, before the buffered departure is drained**. Because the member was alive-and-in-radius at the window-end evaluation point, BCT4 fires → `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` → T8 victory; the same-tick buffered departure is then drained but finds the run already terminal (`beaconWindowSurvived = true`) and is a no-op. The squad that *held* the beacon to the window boundary escapes even though their last holder dies or disconnects on that same tick. BCT-DEFEAT (both wipe and scatter) is suppressed.
- **Why**: The window comparison and the departure drain are two ordered statements in **one** Heartbeat handler (round-5 R4-5) — not two racing Roblox callbacks — so "window-survival first" is deterministic in production, not just in the unit test. Roblox has no Heartbeat connection-priority API, so the round-3 "declared connection order" could not actually guarantee this; buffering does. Favors the squad, consistent with E.9 ("activator dies on activation Heartbeat still wins"). A QA tester must get the same victory result every run for this input. Tested deterministically via the C.16 seam (set `_clock` to exactly window-end and inject the death **or** the disconnect on the same `_step`).
- **Cross-system**: C.9 BCT4 vs BCT-DEFEAT mutual-exclusion + tie-break; C.8 `_pendingDepartures` buffer; B1 proximity clause; C.16 injection.

**E.26 — Beacon natural decay vs window-survival ordering**
- **If**: A run somehow leaves a beacon source live in ED past the survival window without the run having ended (a defensive-only path — e.g. a bug that suppressed both BCT4 and BCT-DEFEAT).
- **Then**: The defensive decay-watch (BCT5) eventually detects ED source-expiry (`m(t, age) <= MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`, ~501 s after activation) and removes the BasePart, firing `OnBeaconDecayed`. Under all *normal* paths this is unreachable: the run ends in victory (BCT4, ~49 s) or defeat (BCT-DEFEAT) roughly 10× before natural decay. BCT5 is retained only so a stuck beacon source cannot persist indefinitely in ED's live-source list.
- **Why**: Documents that decay is strictly ordered *after* window resolution and is a safety net, not a gameplay outcome. Prevents an implementer from wiring victory/cleanup to the decay event (the pre-round-2 behavior) instead of to the window timer.
- **Cross-system**: ED.D.1 source-expiry; C.9 BCT5.

### Beacon Hold-Radius, Placement & Run-Config (round-3)

**E.27 [P0] — Hold broken: fewer than `requiredHolders` alive members within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` for the full line-break grace (scatter / line-break defeat — B1 round-13 fail-fast, holder-threshold round-15)**
- **If**: Beacon is in BC4 and on some Heartbeat the per-tick `windowAliveInRadius` snapshot (C.10) is **below the holder threshold** (`#windowAliveInRadius < requiredHolders`, where `requiredHolders = ceil(#aliveMembers/2)` — the C.10 shorthand for the frozen `ceil(peakAliveCount/2)` baseline clamped to current alive, round-19 R18-1) — ≥1 squad member is alive but fewer than `requiredHolders` are within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs` of `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition` (the squad scattered off the beacon, or a sole sentry tried to hold for the whole squad) — and the snapshot **stays below threshold for a continuous `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE ≈ 3 s`** (G.6) without enough alive members re-entering the radius.
- **Then**: the `_lineBreakSince` grace clock (C.10), stamped on the first below-threshold tick, reaches `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE`; the scatter terminal is fired in C.9 step (c) (after the drains, gated on `aliveMembers` still non-empty — B-1): because the members are **alive**, PC's all-dead path cannot end the run, so **Crafting fires `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="scatter")`** → PC maps it to `RunEnded(defeat)`. The Beacon BasePart is removed via run-end cleanup; the timers are cleared. **`OnBeaconWindowSurvived` does NOT fire; `OnBeaconDecayed` does NOT fire** (same suppression as the wipe path E.24). **If enough alive members re-enter the radius (back to `>= requiredHolders`) before the grace elapses, the line is re-established (the grace clock resets to `nil`) and the run continues — no defeat fires.**
- **Why**: This is the B1 round-13 fail-fast ruling + the round-15 holder threshold that give the window its teeth and close BOTH the round-12 KITING line and the round-14 SENTRY line — "activate, scatter for ~38 s, regroup the final ~10 s" no longer wins (the continuous grace forfeits it), and "appoint ONE member to camp a corner while the other three play freely" no longer wins either (one of four no longer meets `requiredHolders = 2`). At least half the living squad must hold the beacon through its **entire** finale, which is exactly the Section B "holding the loudest thing" fantasy and Pillar 2 ("the squad is the experience"); the round-3 rule (proximity checked only at window-end, members free to leave/re-enter) collapsed into press-and-scatter-and-regroup, and the round-13 `>= 1` rule still allowed a lone sentry. The ~3 s grace exists so a single predator knockback or position jitter at the boundary does not instantly lose — and with the radius tightened to 12 it is traversable on mobile, but far too short to permit a real scatter. The HUD HOLDING/BROKEN binary (colorblind shape/icon + grace countdown, UI surface 13) makes a line-break legible so it never feels like an unsignalled gotcha.
- **Cross-system**: C.9 BCT-DEFEAT line-break (step b); C.10 `_lineBreakSince`; UI.1 surface 13; G.6 `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` + `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE`.

**E.28 [P0] — Cross-wall placement attempt (LOS reachability rejection)**
- **If**: A player fires `RequestBeaconPlace(position)` (or `RequestPlaceItem`) aiming a position that is within `BEACON_PLACE_MAX_RANGE = 8 studs` and has a valid floor beneath it, but lies on the **far side of a wall** from the player (an adjacent room the squad cannot physically reach without going around).
- **Then**: After the clamp, the server runs the **LOS reachability raycast** (C.5.5 / B4) from the player's `HumanoidRootPart` to the clamped candidate; the wall obstructs the segment, so the placement is rejected with `BeaconRejected(reason="unreachable-placement")` (beacon) or the equivalent place-item rejection. No BasePart is spawned; the item is not consumed; the player may re-aim.
- **Why**: Clamp-distance + downward floor-raycast alone (the round-2 validation) would let a player place an *unreachable* beacon/anchor through a thin wall — for a beacon this is fatal, because the B1 hold-radius then requires `requiredHolders` members standing within `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs` of a point the squad cannot get to, making victory impossible while the beacon is irreversibly spent (BC3 is terminal-pre-activation, C.5.3). The LOS ray makes "can a squadmate actually stand by what I placed?" a placement precondition.
- **Cross-system**: C.5.5; C.15 `RequestBeaconPlace`/`RequestPlaceItem`; F.4 Signal-Anchor/Beacon placement-validation ADR.

**E.29 [P0] — Craft completes on the same Heartbeat the sole crafter cancels/dies/disconnects/leaves (BT3 vs BT5 tie-break)**
- **If**: A sole crafter's `craftProgress` crosses `1.0` on the exact Heartbeat that the crafter also fires `RequestCraftCancel`, dies (`Humanoid.Died`), **disconnects (`Players.PlayerRemoving`)**, or the 5 Hz proximity watcher (E.13) flags them out-of-range.
- **Then**: Per the C.8 tie-break (round-5 R4-5), the single CraftingService Heartbeat handler evaluates the **D.1 closed-form progress + completion check FIRST**, then drains `_pendingDepartures` SECOND: BT3 fires, the craft completes (materials deducted, item granted, emission fired, session cleared), and the same-tick buffered cancel/death/disconnect/range-violation drains to find no session and is a no-op. The squad keeps the item they earned. The one exception is BT6 (insufficient materials *at* the atomic deduction) — that rolls the craft back as a completion failure, not a tie-break.
- **Why**: Buffering departures and draining them after the completion check inside one handler makes completion-first a property of the handler's straight-line code, not of Roblox callback-ordering luck (which has no priority API) — closing the round-4 production-determinism gap and covering disconnect, not only death. A QA tester gets the same completion every run. Consistent with the C.9 window tie-break (E.25) and E.9. Tested deterministically via the C.16 seam (drive `_step` so the closed form crosses 1.0 on the same tick a death/disconnect is injected into the buffer).
- **Cross-system**: C.8 tie-break + `_pendingDepartures` buffer; C.16 injection; D.1.

**E.30 — Run drops to a single alive member mid-run (lone survivor, not solo-play)**
- **If**: During a run that started with `squadSize >= 2` (`MIN_SQUAD_SIZE`, C.17), all but one member die / disconnect, leaving one alive player.
- **Then**: The lone survivor continues normally: they craft from their **own** `PersonalInventory` (never locked — the C.3.6 lock applies only to *dead* players' materials, so a living player is never locked out of their own pool); already-crafted items in the **squad shared pool** (C.4) remain usable; they can place + tap-hold-activate the Beacon and satisfy the victory clause alone (`requiredHolders = ceil(1/2) = 1` when only they are alive, so one held member suffices — round-15). If they then die during BC4, that is BCT-DEFEAT (wipe). No special-case rules fire — the existing inventory-lock, shared-pool, and beacon-lifecycle contracts already cover the lone-survivor state.
- **Why**: Round-3 flagged "solo-play unaddressed." The resolution (C.17) is that solo *start* is unsupported (`MIN_SQUAD_SIZE = 2` upstream), but a mid-run drop to one alive member is normal late-game and must not soft-lock. This case documents that it doesn't: the dead-player lock cannot strand a living player, and the shared item pool survives teammate deaths.
- **Cross-system**: C.17; C.3.6 inventory lock; C.4 shared pool; C.9 BCT-DEFEAT.

**E.31 — Graceful server shutdown (`BindToClose`) during the survival window**
- **If**: `game:BindToClose` fires (Studio stop, server migration, scheduled shutdown) while the beacon is in BC4 (or BC2/BC3).
- **Then**: The run terminates as **forced cleanup** via the E.8 run-end path: the Beacon BasePart is torn down; **`OnBeaconWindowSurvived` does NOT fire and `OnBeaconDecayed` does NOT fire**; no victory/defeat result is awarded (there is no in-run save to resume, Pillar 4). The `BindToClose` handler clears RunSession-scoped state and returns promptly — it must NOT block on the survival-window timer.
- **Why**: Round-3 recommended item — `BindToClose` during BC4 was undefined. Treating it as the same forced-cleanup path as E.8 keeps the "no victory/decay cue painted over a forced teardown" contract uniform across all beacon states. There is no fairness obligation to award a result on a server shutdown (no persistence, Pillar 4).
- **Cross-system**: C.9 BindToClose-during-BC4 cleanup; E.8 run-end suppression.

### In-Window Oxygen Canister Disturbance (round-7)

**E.32 [P0] — Oxygen Canister use is loud during BC4, silent outside it (Group-B bunker counter)**
- **If**: An alive player fires `RequestUseItem("OxygenCanister")` — (a) while the survival window is genuinely live (`beaconActivated == true && not runOutcomeResolved` — round-16 network BLOCKING-1, the guard is `not runOutcomeResolved` not `beaconWindowSurvived == false`); or (b) at any other time (pre-activation, BC2/BC3, **or after window resolution — including after a *defeat*, where `beaconWindowSurvived` is still `false` but `runOutcomeResolved` is `true`**).
- **Then**: Validation (alive; item in `SquadInventory`) runs identically in both cases, the Canister is consumed, and `OnOxygenPulseRequest` fires to RM. **Only in case (a)** the server additionally fires one `DisturbanceService:Emit("Craft", RunSession.beaconWorldPosition, MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE = 0.18, usingPlayerId)` after the RM restore (C.6 / C.11). In case (b) no disturbance is emitted (the disturbance was paid at craft completion). The emission position is the **beacon's world position** (round-11 EC-3 ruling — *not* the using player's position): a player who has scattered far from the beacon and breathes still adds to the *beacon's* noise, never baiting the predator toward their own location (closing the round-10 scatter-to-breathe concern). A Canister used on the exact window-end Heartbeat still emits (the `RequestUseItem` handler is independent of the BC4 window-survival check; the use is processed in its own handler, not the `_pendingDepartures` drain). The emission's `sourcePlayerId` is the using player (correct attribution); it touches no beacon completion/attribution flag.
- **Why**: Round-7 Group-B (user-approved Hybrid A+B). The round-4 Squad-Relay cut left the Canister as the sole oxygen line, and the round-6 panel found "stack ~10 Canisters and bunker quietly" was the dominant *quiet* solution to the loud finale — ludonarrative dissonance. Making in-window use loud converts the bunker line into a noisy expenditure the committing predator hears, restoring "every breath costs noise" (Pillar 3) at the climax. The remaining necessity (that the squad *must* spend that oxygen, and hiding is punished) is the RM/PA forward obligations (F.4). Documented as an edge case so the BC4-conditional emission is unambiguous: same item, two disturbance outcomes by beacon state.
- **Cross-system**: C.6, C.11 (publisher), C.15 `RequestUseItem`, G.4 `MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE`; RM oxygen restore; verified by H.93.

**E.33 — Respawn during BC4 never raises the hold bar; activating late never lowers it (holder-threshold baseline edges — round-16 R16-1 FREEZE + round-19 R18-1 RATCHET)**
- **If**: (sub-case A — respawn) Beacon is in BC4 and a member who was dead/disconnected **respawns** mid-window (their re-add drains from `_pendingReadds` at C.9 step c), growing `RunSession.aliveMembers`; **OR** (sub-case B — activate-late) a squad that started with N members lets some die **before** activating the Beacon, so `#aliveMembers` at activation is below N.
- **Then**:
  - **Sub-case A (respawn — closed by the R16-1 freeze):** `requiredHolders = min(requiredHoldersBaseline, #aliveMembers)` is evaluated against the **frozen** `requiredHoldersBaseline` (set once at BCT3, C.10). A respawn raises `#aliveMembers` but **can never raise `requiredHolders` above the baseline** — the `min` is capped. The respawned member is purely additive — a body that *can* help hold (re-enter the radius to count toward the threshold), never a body that *raises* the requirement. The only case a respawn changes the live `requiredHolders` is restoring it **toward (never above)** the baseline after earlier deaths had clamped it lower (e.g. baseline 2, dropped to 1 alive [required `min(2,1)=1`], then a teammate respawns to 2 alive → required `min(2,2)=2`) — recovering the requirement that was already true, which is legible ("we needed 2 holders; we're back to 2 alive, so 2 hold"). If a respawn-driven restore momentarily leaves the held count short, the `_lineBreakSince` grace (≈3 s, traversable in the 12-stud radius) gives the squad time to meet it; a respawn never *directly* fires a terminal.
  - **Sub-case B (activate-late — closed by the R18-1 ratchet):** `requiredHoldersBaseline = ceil(peakAliveCount / 2)`, and `peakAliveCount` is the run's **high-water alive count** (ratcheted from RunStarted), so it records the largest squad the run ever fielded — **not** the (smaller) alive count at a deliberately-delayed activation. A 4-player squad that waits for 2 teammates to die before lighting the Beacon still gets `peakAliveCount = 4 → baseline = ceil(4/2) = 2` — it does **not** drop to `ceil(2/2) = 1`. So delaying activation buys **no** reduction in the holder bar (the round-18 "activate-late-for-low-baseline" degenerate lane); the only consequence of activating depleted is the usual `min`-clamp (if only 2 are alive at activation, `requiredHolders = min(2, 2) = 2` — both survivors hold; if it later drops to 1 alive, `min(2,1)=1`, the lone survivor holds alone).
- **Why**: The two rulings together bound the holder bar from both directions so **neither respawn timing nor activation timing is a degenerate lever**. R16-1 (freeze) removed the round-15 respawn-punishes-holders feel (a respawn making the hold *harder than it was at activation* contradicted Section B); R18-1 (ratchet) removed the symmetric activate-late exploit it had opened (dying-down to lock a baseline of 1 contradicted the "activate together at peak tension" fantasy and is the degenerate-optimal class Patch/Wrap/Relay were cut for). **Monotonicity, precisely (round-18 systems wording fix):** the **baseline** is monotone — frozen at BCT3 and never changed — but the **live `requiredHolders`** is *non-monotone by design* (it tracks `2→1→2` as members die and respawn, via the `min` clamp). "Monotonic difficulty" means the *bar you must clear* never exceeds the baseline and never drops below the current alive count — not that the live number is constant. The `min(baseline, #aliveMembers)` clamp preserves the lone-survivor win (C.17/E.30: `min(baseline, 1) = 1`) so a frozen high baseline can never strand a sole survivor.
- **Cross-system**: C.9 step (b)/(c) + BCT3 step 6; C.10 `aliveMembers` / `_pendingReadds` / `peakAliveCount` / `requiredHoldersBaseline` / `requiredHolders`; G.6 `BEACON_HOLD_MIN_HOLDERS`; verified by H.118 (freeze) + H.129 (ratchet / activate-late).

### Position-Replication Trust (round-19)

**E.34 [P0] — Position-spoof during BC4: a client manipulates its replicated `HumanoidRootPart.Position` to forge or grief the hold (FENCE — documented MVP risk acceptance, R18-3)**
- **If**: A malicious client manipulates the `HumanoidRootPart.Position` the server replicates for its **own** character during BC4 — either **(a) spoof INTO** `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` to be counted toward `requiredHolders` while physically elsewhere (forge a hold the squad is not maintaining → a false `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` → T8 victory), or **(b) spoof OUT of** the radius to drop `#windowAliveInRadius` below `requiredHolders` and grief the squad into a line-break / scatter defeat.
- **Then (MVP — FENCE / explicit risk acceptance, NOT anti-cheat)**: the win check reads each member's position through `_memberPosition` (C.16), whose production default reads the server-tracked `HumanoidRootPart.Position`. In Roblox a player's own character physics is **client-replicated by default**, so a determined exploiter *can* influence the value the server reads. For the MVP this is an **explicitly accepted, bounded risk**: (i) a client can only spoof **its own** position (one member's inclusion/exclusion), so in the worst case — a 4-squad with frozen `requiredHolders = 2` — it flips the threshold by **one**, not a mass forge; (ii) a forge-win still cannot skip the survival window — `beaconActivated` / `beaconWindowSurvived` are server-only flags an attacker cannot set (E.22), so the run must genuinely reach window-end; (iii) the win-condition **authority chain** (server-only flags, `beaconPlacerId` attribution, the single-decision-point handler) is **untouched** — only the position *input* is spoofable, and only the spoofer's own. Robust position anti-cheat (server-authoritative / server-reconciled character movement) is a project-wide concern **out of MVP scope** and would destabilize the 18-round-stabilized authority chain; it is deliberately **not** built now.
- **Post-MVP hardening (forward obligation, H.126)**: the designated hardening is a server-side **position-delta velocity sanity check** — ignore/reject a `_memberPosition` update whose implied speed between ticks exceeds a plausible `MAX_PLAYER_SPEED` ceiling (a teleport-into/out-of-radius detector), so a client cannot snap across the `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` boundary faster than a real character could move. This is named here and carried as the forward-obligation AC **H.126** (Integration, unverified-until the anti-cheat/movement pass). **Document-and-fence: do NOT build it in the MVP.**
- **Why**: round-18 network N18-B1 escalated this from a round-16 nice-to-have to BLOCKING because R16-1's frozen `requiredHolders` means a single spoofed position can flip the threshold for a 4-squad. The honest MVP stance is to **name** the vectors (forge-win + grief-line-break), **bound** their impact, and **schedule** the velocity check — not to leave the gap undocumented (round-18 found no edge case, no AC, no F.5 caveat) and not to over-build anti-cheat the MVP scope excludes.
- **Cross-system**: C.9 BCT4 proximity clause; C.10 `_memberPosition`; C.16 position seam; F.5 (`HumanoidRootPart.Position` caveat); H.126 (post-MVP velocity check); R18-3.

>

>

ED is the source of truth. The Beacon-specific constants `MAGNITUDE_BEACON = 0.95`, `BEACON_HALF_LIFE = 90 s`, and the `BEACON_STATIONARY_*` family are also ED-owned and referenced (not redefined) here.
> - **Publishing path**: Crafting publishes via `DisturbanceService:Emit(...)` per ED.C.3.6 (Beacon) and the new `Craft` emission type. Crafting does NOT bypass and write to the spatial grid directly. Magnitude per emission type: `MAGNITUDE_BEACON = 0.95` is owned by ED; the `Craft`-type magnitudes (`MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_XS`, `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_MID`, `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_HEAVY`, `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE` — `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_RELAY` retired round-3, and the **Squad Relay recipe itself cut round-5** per R4-2) are owned by THIS GDD's Section G.
> - **Subscribing path**: Crafting & Items does NOT subscribe to any disturbance signal. It is a pure publisher. The Signal Anchor sensor reads predator position directly via Roblox `workspace` queries — it does NOT consume `OnMeterUpdate`, `OnDisturbanceAlert`, or any other ED-owned RemoteSignal. Crafting honours ED's zero-RemoteFunction-on-Client rule (ED.F.4) and the no-client-Emit rule (ED.C.1.8) — see E.20 + E.22 for the server-authority enforcement.
> - **Predator AI F.2a obligations resolved**: NOT APPLICABLE — Crafting & Items is not the Predator AI GDD.

So PC's BC4 handling has exactly two inbound outcomes from Crafting: `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`→victory, `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason)`→defeat (scatter or wipe); outside BC4, PC's all-dead path owns defeat as before. **Round-9 (2026-06-03) — respawn/alive-state signal; round-11 (2026-06-04) UPGRADED TO BLOCKING + BC4 all-dead suppression:** PC owes a `OnSquadMemberAliveChanged(playerId, isAlive)` signal that Crafting subscribes to, to maintain its server-authoritative `RunSession.aliveMembers` set (C.10) — death/disconnect removal already flows through Crafting's own `Humanoid.Died`/`Players.PlayerRemoving` connections, but the **re-add on respawn** must come from PC (PC owns the canonical alive/respawn lifecycle, PC.S4). **Round-11 (Convergence #1) upgrades this from non-blocking to BLOCKING:** the round-11 fix widened the `aliveMembers` drain to the *whole run* (not only BC4), so a member who dies pre-BC4 and **respawns** before window-end is now *removed* from `aliveMembers` and MUST be re-added, or they are wrongly excluded from the victory snapshot while alive-and-in-radius. Crafting carries an **interim self-sourced re-add** on the player's own `CharacterAdded`+`Humanoid` ready (so the system is correct in isolation while PC is in MAJOR REVISION), superseded by PC's canonical signal when it lands (H.108). **Round-11 (Convergence #2) — post-activation all-dead suppression (BLOCKING):** PC MUST **suppress its generic all-dead `RunEnded(defeat)` path for the whole post-activation lifecycle — gate on `RunSession.beaconActivated == true` alone** (BCT3 until `RunEnded`; build-gate A4 widened this from the narrower `beaconActivated && !beaconWindowSurvived`, which released suppression at the victory write and opened a post-victory defeat-race) and let Crafting's `OnBeaconWindowFailed(reason="wipe")` own the in-window wipe — otherwise PC's synchronous `Humanoid.Died`→`RunEnded(defeat)` races Crafting's drain before the `runOutcomeResolved` latch is set, a same-frame double-cleanup (C.9/C.12.1/H.109). When this is applied DELIBERATELY — **only once the RunController GDD is authored AND `RunController` + `RunEndConditionRaised` are registered in `entities.yaml`** — the migration MUST: (a) re-route **BOTH** victory (`OnBeaconWindowSurvived`) **and** defeat (`OnBeaconWindowFailed{scatter,wipe}`) through the arbiter — the backed-out attempt migrated only the defeat half, which contradicted its own "sole `RunEnded` broadcaster" claim (victory still had PC broadcast `RunEnded(victory)` directly) and left the arbiter's victory-precedence rule unbuildable; (b) add `RunController` to F.1 as a **hard** dependency (run-end is gated on it) + the F.6 back-reference audit; (c) fully specify `RunEndConditionRaised` (payload, arity, `reason` enum, **server-only** trust boundary) and the arbiter's `RUN_END_DEFEAT_HOLD` resolution rule, and reconcile the `runOutcomeResolved` latch with the arbiter's hold window (the latch must not read "resolved" while the arbiter has not yet broadcast); (d) demote `OnBeaconWindowFailed` to a HUD-only cue (PC consumes the arbiter broadcast, not this event). **Until all of the above land, the round-23 model below stands unchanged: PC subscribes to `OnBeaconWindowFailed` and maps both reasons to `RunEnded(defeat)`; PC broadcasts `RunEnded(victory)` on `OnBeaconWindowSurvived`.** | Surfaced in F.6 below; coordinated update after Crafting locks. **PC GDD is itself in MAJOR REVISION (round-6) — fold the T8 retiming + the round-3 `OnBeaconWindowFailed` subscription into that revision.** |

ED.F.2 row for Crafting currently states "TBD pending Crafting GDD" for trigger conditions and magnitude — this GDD's C.2 + Section G resolve the TBD. Registry update in Phase 5 will register `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_*` constants and the `Craft` enum value. **(round-5 R4-1, load-bearing) `BEACON_HALF_LIFE` registry-range tightening:** ED MUST narrow `BEACON_HALF_LIFE` from `[45, 180] s` to **`[64, 128] s`** so the derived `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` (= `BEACON_HALF_LIFE × 0.5476`) stays inside the design-safe `[35, 70] s` band (G.6). This is the CD ruling that lets round-5 claim "zero open design decisions" — OQ.14 is resolved in favour of ED tightening (no Crafting-side decoupling). **(round-5, UX-blocking) WCAG 2.3.1 flora-flash cap:** the beacon-activation flora saturation/pulse (ED-owned flora subsystem, Moment 13) MUST NOT exceed 3 flashes/second (WCAG 2.3.1 general flash threshold) during BC4 — ED's flora author owns the flash-rate cap; Crafting flags it here because the activation cue is a Crafting-triggered moment. This is a hard accessibility requirement, **not** a `/ux-design` deferral (escalated from round-4 Recommended). | Phase 5 registry write (Craft enum + `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_*`); ED next-revision registry edit (`BEACON_HALF_LIFE` → `[64, 128] s`); ED flora author (WCAG flash cap). |
| **HUD GDD** | When the HUD GDD is authored, it MUST reverse-cite Crafting's C.14 contract and consume the client-bound signals listed in F.2 / C.14 (round-2 added `OnBeaconActivated` window-start + `OnBeaconWindowSurvived` victory; removed the Patch/Wrap equipped-effect badges). HUD MUST distinguish the cancellation signals (`OnCraftCancelled` reasons: `player-initiated`, `out-of-range`, `insufficient-materials`, `death`) from `OnCraftRejected` (pre-craft validation failure) and from `OnEquippedEffectExpired` (consumable lifecycle end). | HUD reason taxonomy per E.6. |

The order matters: the gather emission is RN's responsibility (uses `MAGNITUDE_GATHER_LIGHT/MEDIUM/HEAVY`); the inventory delta is Crafting's responsibility. RN MUST NOT re-raise the gather emission on `MATERIAL_STACK_MAX` overflow (E.1 deferred — but the rule applies regardless). | Surfaced for RN author. |
| **Resource Management GDD** | When RM is authored, it MUST own: (a) Oxygen Canister `OnOxygenRestoreRequested(amount)` consumption — Crafting fires from `RequestUseItem`; RM does the math. (b) Crafting does NOT touch RM's oxygen pool directly. **(c) Oxygen-drain floor during BC4 — falsifiable contract (B2 round-3; numeric round-7):** RM MUST define `OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR`, the minimum **per-member** oxygen-drain rate applied while the beacon is in BC4, satisfying the binding inequality. **Per-member vs. squad-pool (round-13 EC-1a precision):** Oxygen Canisters live in `SquadInventory` (shared), so the squad's *total* canister budget is up to `MIN_SQUAD_SIZE × ITEM_STACK_MAX` distributable across members — but the binding inequality is stated **per member at the strictest single-member case**, which makes the pool conflation moot: **even a single member commanding a full `ITEM_STACK_MAX` (10) stack of restores to themselves CANNOT keep their own oxygen above the critical threshold for the full window** — `ITEM_STACK_MAX × canister_restore < BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW × OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` (per member). Because no single member can self-cover even with the maximum stack any one member could command, distributing the shared pool across `MIN_SQUAD_SIZE` members is **no better per member** — so the inequality is sufficient without a separate squad-pool form (the round-12 EC-1a conflation: the round-7 wording read as a squad-aggregate while the algebra is per-member). **`canister_restore` ceiling — UNIT-INDEPENDENT restatement (round-13 EC-1b; round-15 E-1 fix):** the round-13 ceiling `canister_restore < 3.5 s` was **unit-dependent** — it silently assumed `OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` is normalized to `1.0 oxygen/s`, so RM could declare compliance in absolute units while the economy breaks (round-14 economy E-1). The binding inequality is therefore restated **entirely in oxygen units**, with `canister_restore_oxygen` = the oxygen restored per Canister (RM owns the unit) and `OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` = oxygen drained per second during BC4: at the **shortest in-band window (35 s)**, a full stack plus the member's starting pool must NOT cover the window's drain — **`oxygen_pool_start + ITEM_STACK_MAX × canister_restore_oxygen < BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW_min × OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR`** (all terms in the same oxygen unit; `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW_min = 35 s`). Both sides scale with whatever oxygen unit RM picks, so the inequality is true or false independent of normalization — RM cannot satisfy it on paper by rescaling units. **Why `ITEM_STACK_MAX` is the right stress denominator (round-20 economy EC-B2):** the inequality stresses against a *full* `ITEM_STACK_MAX = 10` Canister stack, not the illustrative 2-Canister loadout in the C.2 18-gather sanity check, because the binding case is the **maximum a motivated squad can actually reach before activation**, and that maximum is near the cap: Oxygen Canisters cost `2 BIOMASS / 0 / 0` and BIOMASS is the cheapest, most-plentiful tier (Light nodes, `MAGNITUDE_GATHER_LIGHT = 0.15`), so a pre-activation gather phase that yields even ~18 BIOMASS (9 Light-node pulls at ~2 BIOMASS each) funds ~8 Canisters after the Beacon's 2 BIOMASS (`(18 − 2) / 2 = 8`; round-22 economy EC-22-B2 arithmetic fix — the prior text read "~16 BIOMASS funds ~8 Canisters," which nets only 7 after the Beacon's 2) — i.e. a real run can stack to within two Canisters of `ITEM_STACK_MAX`. RM therefore MUST calibrate `OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` so the inequality holds at the **full-stack** denominator (the reachable worst case for the floor), not at a smaller "typical" stack. *(If RM's own gather-budget analysis shows a winning run genuinely cannot reach near-`ITEM_STACK_MAX` Canisters, restate H.106's stress tier to the true reachable maximum — but the default stress is the full stack.)* *(The seconds figure `canister_restore < ~3.5 s` is now only an illustrative value at a reference drain of `1.0 oxygen/s` and a critical pool reached at window-end; it is NOT the binding form.)* The round-9 provisional `4–6 s` violated even the normalized form; round-11/13 tightened it; round-15 makes the constraint unit-independent. RM finalizes `canister_restore_oxygen`, `oxygen_pool_start`, and `OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` together so this holds at the 35 s window. **RM MUST carry an AC asserting this inequality holds at default tunings.** RM must also provide the provisional Canister restore value so economy-designer can finalize OQ.13. **Provisional `canister_restore` (round-9; tightened round-11 EC-1 to hold at the short-window end):** the round-9 provisional `4–6 s`/Canister gives a full `ITEM_STACK_MAX = 10` stack ≈ `40–60 s` of cover, which **exceeds the short end of the `[35, 70] s` window** — so at a 35 s window the inequality `ITEM_STACK_MAX × canister_restore < BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW × OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` is directionally **violated** (a full stack *can* span the window before the floor bites — the round-10 economy EC-1 finding). The inequality must hold at the **shortest in-band window (35 s — the worst case for the floor)**, so the provisional is tightened to **`canister_restore ≈ 3 s`** (full stack ≈ `30 s < 35 s`), and **`OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` MUST be calibrated against the 35 s window**, not the 49 s default — at the short end a full stack cannot fully cover, and at longer windows the margin only widens. RM finalizes the exact `canister_restore`; the binding AC (H.106) is evaluated at the shortest in-band window. **Inverse-degenerate closure (round-9, Blocker #9; explicit inequality round-19 economy recommended):** the same `OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` MUST also make the *inverse* line — "never use a Canister in BC4, just tank the oxygen damage to stay silent" — **non-survivable** within the window for a stationary clustered squad (i.e. the floor drains a non-breathing member to the critical threshold before window-end). **Explicit inverse inequality (round-19 — write it, don't leave it prose):** a member who **never breathes** must reach the critical threshold before the shortest in-band window ends — **`oxygen_pool_start < BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW_min × OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR`** (`BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW_min = 35 s`, all terms in RM's oxygen unit). This is the over-supplied inequality with the `ITEM_STACK_MAX × canister_restore_oxygen` term **dropped**, so it is the **strictly weaker** of the two (round-21 economy EC-21-1 correction — the prior text mislabeled it "strictly stronger," which is backwards): the over-supplied bound (the form *with* the stack term) is the strictly *stronger* condition and **implies** this one — if even a full stack cannot cover the window, the zero-breath line certainly cannot. The implication runs **over-supplied ⟹ inverse-degenerate, NOT the reverse**, so verifying the inverse-degenerate alone does NOT close the bunker line. RM MUST therefore carry the **over-supplied** inequality (H.106) as the binding AC; `OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` is calibrated so **both** hold at the 35 s window, and because the binding AC is the over-supplied (stronger) form, RM's AC closes BOTH the over-supplied line (full stack can't cover) AND the tank-the-damage line (not breathing isn't survivable). Verified-forward by H.106 (Integration, unverified-until-RM). **FENCE (round-9, per the round-6/round-8 CD direction — stop re-litigating each round):** the *silent-hold* gap (a squad that neither breathes nor moves, untouched by the C.6 voluntary-use emission) and the inverse-degenerate are **known, explicitly-fenced forward obligations on RM + PA**, NOT open Crafting blockers. Crafting's structural contribution (the C.6 in-window emission) is the most it can own without RM/PA; the residual closure is gated on these two ACs and will not be re-surfaced as a Crafting defect until RM/PA exist. Until then, the C.5.6a aid-necessity is explicitly a *target gated on these ACs*, not asserted fact. *(Round-5: the former Squad Relay `OnOxygenTransferRequest` consumption is removed with the Relay cut — the Canister is the only Crafting→RM oxygen interface. Round-7: prose "design target" replaced with this numeric, AC-bearing contract per the Group-B Hybrid-A+B ruling; the Crafting-owned in-window Canister emission (C.6) is the structural complement that makes the bunker line non-free even before RM lands.)* | Server-authority boundary; **B2 forward obligation — half of aid-necessity (the other half is the PA row below); the in-window Canister emission (C.6) is the Crafting-owned third leg.** |
| **Predator AI GDD** | When PA is authored: (a) it should add an AC verifying that the Beacon's stationary-squad cap (ED.C.3.6, `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65`) interacts correctly with PA's locked-target logic (ED.F.2a row 6). **(b) Minimum predator aggression during BC4 — falsifiable contract (B2 round-3; numeric round-7):** PA MUST define `PREDATOR_BC4_MIN_COMMIT`, guaranteeing that while the beacon is in BC4 the predator's approach/attack pressure toward `RunSession.beaconWorldPosition` does not drop below a floor such that **a stationary clustered squad inside `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` taking no defensive action (no Coil reposition, no Anchor read, no kiting) suffers at least one forced damage/displacement event before window-end at default tunings.** **PA MUST carry an AC asserting a stationary-bunker squad is engaged at least once during a full window.** This is the other half of aid-suite necessity (C.5.6a / the RM row above): the Anchor (read approach), Coil (reposition under pressure), and in-window oxygen (now a *loud* expenditure per C.6) only matter if the predator actually applies pressure. No new Crafting interface required — a PA-internal aggression-floor contract, surfaced here because Crafting's "aid is necessary" claim depends on it. Until PA lands, the "predator commits" half is a *target gated on this AC*. **(c) UPPER bound — winnable-by-holding floor (round-15 F6 ruling, BLOCKING):** because the round-15 continuous-hold rule means a held squad **cannot flee** the finale (fleeing IS the line-break that loses the run — C.5.6a), `PREDATOR_BC4_MIN_COMMIT` is now **two-sided**: the predator MUST telegraph its beacon commit with enough reaction lead for a held squad to spend aids / micro-reposition **inside** `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS = 12 studs`, and **MUST NOT one-shot a sole holder (or eliminate `requiredHolders` holders in a single unavoidable event) without a wind-up window** the squad can react to. **PA MUST carry an AC that a correctly-held squad (>= requiredHolders inside the radius, spending aids) can SURVIVE a full window at default tunings** — "BC4 must be winnable by holding." A finale that is an unavoidable execution (hold = guaranteed death) is a design failure, not difficulty. Together (b)+(c) bound PA's BC4 aggression from both ends: hard enough that bunkering-without-aids fails, soft enough that holding-with-aids succeeds. **(c-mobile) Knockback displacement cap — `PREDATOR_BC4_MAX_KNOCKBACK` (round-19 ux B-1 / qa B-2, BLOCKING-forward):** because the continuous-hold rule means most line-breaks are CAUSED by a predator knockback that throws a holder *outward* past `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`, a single BC4 displacement event MUST NOT throw a held member so far that they cannot traverse back inside the radius within the grace minus the mobile reaction + touch-latency budget. PA MUST define `PREDATOR_BC4_MAX_KNOCKBACK` satisfying the H.121 joint inequality **`t_react + t_latency + (PREDATOR_BC4_MAX_KNOCKBACK / MoveSpeed) < BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE`** (at the illustrative `MoveSpeed ≈ 16 studs/s` this caps the post-knockback distance-to-re-enter at ~22 studs, with margin less). This is what makes the 3 s grace **mobile-recoverable** — it resolves the round-14/round-18 game-vs-ux grace disagreement by **bounding the throw, not stretching the grace** (grace stays ≤ 4 s). **PA MUST carry an AC that the worst-case single-event knockback on a held member is recoverable within `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE` at default `MoveSpeed` including mobile touch latency** (the verification half of Crafting's H.121, which is unverified-until-PA/PC). **(d) RESONANT consumer minimum (round-15 E-2 ruling, BLOCKING):** RESONANT's only non-Beacon consumer is the Dampener Coil (G.3), whose positional-freedom value is PA-gated (a Coil matters only if the predator reads the squad's movement/cluster). So pre-PA the RESONANT tier risks being "Beacon-components-only." The CD ruling (user-approved) is a **binding minimum, not a material recut**: PA's BC4 design (jointly with RM via the drain/threat economy) MUST make **≥ 1 Dampener Coil worth crafting in a winning run at default tunings** — i.e. there is a winning line in which repositioning under the Coil's reduced pulse is the rational play, so RESONANT has a live non-Beacon sink. **PA MUST carry an AC that a default winning line crafts and benefits from ≥1 Coil.** If PA/RM playtest cannot satisfy this, the fallback is to honestly declare RESONANT a Beacon-only material and recut the Coil to MINERAL/BIOMASS (deferred decision, not taken now). This converts OQ.15's RESONANT half from "fenced, depends on RM/PA" into a checkable AC RM/PA owe. **(e) Signal Anchor readable pre-activation approach (round-19 R18-2 — the Anchor keep-justification's forward fence):** the Signal Anchor's decisive value is *pre-activation reconnaissance* (C.2/C.7), which is real only if PA's predator has a **readable** pre-activation position/approach the Anchor's single-shot ping can meaningfully surface (not, e.g., a predator that only exists/commits after activation). **PA SHOULD carry an expectation that a pre-activation Anchor read yields actionable threat information at default tunings** — the honest fence on the round-19 decision to KEEP the Anchor (R18-2). If PA shows the pre-activation read is valueless, the Anchor returns to cut-scrutiny. | (a) already an ED.F.2a row; **(b) is a B2 forward obligation, now a numeric AC-bearing contract (round-7) — aid-necessity depends on this + the RM oxygen-drain floor + the Crafting-owned in-window Canister emission (C.6).** |
| **Architecture (`/create-architecture`)** | Five ADRs flagged for authorship: (1) **Bench Proximity Watcher** — server-side cadence + mechanism per E.13 / G5 (recommend `RunService.Heartbeat` accumulator at `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 5`, but pin in ADR). (2) **Placement Validation (Beacon + Signal Anchor)** — the round-3 clamp → **LOS reachability raycast (B4)** → raycast-to-floor → overlap-check order, distances, and thresholds per C.5.5 / E.16 / E.28 / G7 *(round-5: Relay dropped from this ADR with the Relay cut)*. (3) **Crafting RemoteEvent Trust Boundary** — rate limits (incl. the `RequestEquipItem` atomic order), payload schemas, server-authority invariants per C.15 + E.20–E.23 *(round-5: `RequestActivateRelay` removed)*. (4) **Survival-Window Timer + Departure Buffer (B5 round-3; R4-5 round-5)** — pin the window completion to a per-`RunService.Heartbeat` `_clock() - beaconActivationTime >= BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` comparison, NOT `task.delay`; **AND** route all in-window death (`Humanoid.Died`) and disconnect (`Players.PlayerRemoving`) events through a per-Heartbeat `_pendingDepartures` buffer drained *inside* the BC4 Heartbeat handler **after** the window-survival check — so the E.25/H.77 tie-break is deterministic in production without a Heartbeat connection-priority API (which Roblox does not expose). This ADR also covers the BCT4 proximity (`BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS`) evaluation. (5) **Test-Harness Injection Seam (B8/B9 round-3; round-7 adds `_simulateBindToClose`)** — the C.16 DI'd `_clock` / `_step(dt)` / `_simulateRestart` / `_simulateBindToClose` contract that makes the timing-AC cluster + H.74 + H.90 hermetically testable; production handler bodies MUST route all time reads through `_clock` (R4-4). The `_enqueueDeparture` fan-out seam (C.8, round-7 A-3) is also test-injectable here for the same-tick dual-scope departure tests (H.101). | Architecture phase deliverable. |

Cosmetic items that touch this system MUST pass the dual-clause cosmetic-boundary checklist. | Live-ops + monetization defence. |

### F.5 — Engine and Library Dependencies

| Dependency | Used For | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| **Roblox `BasePart` + `Humanoid`** | Bench placement (BasePart in safe rooms); proximity gate via `HumanoidRootPart.Position` distance check; the BC4 hold check reads `HumanoidRootPart.Position` through the `_memberPosition` seam (C.10/C.16) | Engine built-in; verified. **Caveat (round-19 R18-3 — position-replication trust):** a player's own character physics is **client-replicated by default**, so the `HumanoidRootPart.Position` the server reads for the BC4 hold check is influenceable by a determined exploiter (E.34: spoof-into-radius = forge-win, spoof-out = grief-line-break). For the MVP this is an **explicitly fenced, bounded risk** (a client can only move its own one position; the server-only win flags + attribution chain are untouched). The designated post-MVP hardening is a server-side position-delta **velocity sanity check** (`MAX_PLAYER_SPEED` teleport detector) — forward obligation H.126. Anti-cheat is NOT built in the MVP. |
| **Roblox `RunService.Heartbeat`** | Per-Heartbeat craft-progress **closed-form evaluator (D.1, round-5 R4-3 — no accumulator)**; bench proximity watcher (E.13); **the Beacon survival-window completion check + BCT4 `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` proximity evaluation (B5 — Heartbeat-polled, NOT `task.delay`)**; **the `_pendingDepartures` death/disconnect buffer drain + BT3/BCT4-vs-departure tie-break ordering (C.8 / C.9, R4-5 — all in one handler, no connection-priority dependency)**. In production it is the source bound to the C.16 `_step(dt)` seam; tests drive `_step` directly. | Engine built-in; verified |
| **Roblox `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`** | The **production default** of the C.16 `_clock()` seam — all server-side timing (craft start, equipped-effect duration timers, anchor lifetime, beacon midpulse + survival-window comparisons) routes through `_clock()`, never the global directly in handler bodies (R4-4). Forbidden in handler bodies: `os.time()`, `tick()`, `os.clock()`, and direct `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` per ED.D.1 + C.16. | Engine built-in; verified |
| **Roblox `RaycastParams`** | Signal Anchor placement validation (raycast-to-floor per E.16) and Anchor sensor predator-position queries (C.7) | Engine built-in; verified |
| **Roblox `RemoteEvent` via Knit `RemoteSignal`** | All client-bound craft signals (C.14) and all client-to-server craft requests (C.15). Routed through Knit for unified rate-limit + audit surface. | Library; confirmed at engine setup |
| **Knit `Service` + `Controller`** | `CraftingService` (server) + `CraftingController` (client). All cross-boundary state mutations route through `CraftingService`; client owns only render/input. | Library; confirmed at engine setup |
| **No `DataStoreService` / ProfileStore dependency** | All Crafting state is purely runtime (per C.4) — no persistence across sessions. ProfileStore is used for cosmetics only, not Crafting state. | Verified by C.4 + C.10 |

### F.7 — Out-of-Scope (NOT dependencies of this system)

These systems do NOT interact with Crafting & Items, by design:

- **Roblox `DataStoreService` / ProfileStore** — Crafting state is purely runtime; no persistence (per C.4). ProfileStore is used for cosmetics only.
- **Roblox `PathfindingService`** — Predator AI uses pathfinding to reach the Beacon, but Crafting publishes no pathfinding hints. Crafting's interface to PA is purely via the disturbance field.
- **Audio system** (post-MVP) — bench / craft / beacon SFX will be authored when the Audio system is added; no current Crafting → Audio contract. Crafting may emit Knit signals on craft events that the Audio system can subscribe to passively, with no Crafting-side change required.
- **Multiplayer matchmaking** — Crafting operates within a single RunSession; matchmaking is upstream and out of scope.

## Tuning Knobs

All knobs are **server-authoritative** and live in a single config module (consumed by Knit `CraftingService`). Categories: **Feel** (player-perceived responsiveness), **Curve** (drives a formula or progression shape), **Gate** (rate-limit / cap / threshold). Knobs flagged "**ED-locked**" or "**PC-locked**" are owned by the Ecological Disturbance / Player Controller registries and **MUST NOT** be redefined here — listed only to surface their relevance.

### G.1 — Bench Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS` (ceiling) | 2 | 2–4 | Gate | C.3.5, C.8 | n/a — this is the **ceiling** on the squad-scaled effective cap `benchMaxCraftersEffective = min(BENCH_MAX_CRAFTERS, ceil(squadSize/2))` (round-2). Below 2 would force every squad to cap 1 and remove multi-crafting entirely | At 3+, the completion-emission stack (D.3) breaches ED's reserved-band logic — three independent emissions at the same position can saturate the bench-position fieldValue; cap dwell-reduction value also flattens (1/3 vs 1/2 returns). **Round-2: do not raise — the cap now scales with squad size; raising the ceiling re-opens the round-1 reserved-band concern.** Effective cap is 1 for 2-player squads (fixes the "nobody watches" collision), 2 for 3–4-player squads |
| `CRAFT_JOIN_CUTOFF` | 0.85 | 0.50–0.95 | Gate | C.3.5, C.8, E.3 | At 0.50, late-joiners get half the dwell window for full emission attribution — joining becomes a cheap way to halve craft cost | At 0.95, joiners can enter the final 5% of a craft and pay full per-crafter emission for trivial contribution — the deserter-exploit (E.5) reopens |
| `BENCH_RANGE_TOLERANCE` | 2 studs | 1–4 | Feel | C.8, E.13 | At 1, network position jitter creates false out-of-range cancellations on stable players | At 4+, players can sprint freely around the bench without losing craft progress — breaks the "stay at the bench" affordance |

### G.2 — Inventory Caps

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MATERIAL_STACK_MAX` | 20 | 10–50 | Gate | C.4 | At 10, every other gather forces a bench round-trip — pacing collapses to material-shuttle | At 50+, the "drop-at-bench-before-gathering" tension disappears; one player can hoard all squad materials |
| `SQUAD_INVENTORY_TYPE_CAP` | 8 | 5–12 | Gate | C.4 | Below 4, squad cannot hold one of each crafted item simultaneously (4 distinct types exist in MVP post-round-5) | Above 12, no practical effect — only 4 distinct item types exist in MVP (round-5: Squad Relay cut; was 5). The default 8 leaves generous headroom for the 4 types + future content |
| `ITEM_STACK_MAX` | 10 | 3–25 | Gate | C.4 | At 3, hoarding consumables breaks down; squad runs out mid-Hunt-tier with no resupply path | At 25+, late-game runs accumulate item piles that flatten Pillar 4's "rounds not saves" pacing |

### G.3 — Recipe Cost & Craft-Time Knobs

The 4 recipes' material costs and craft times are **structural** — adjusting any single recipe shifts the squad's gather budget for the whole run. Tune as a set, not individually. *(Round-5: Squad Relay cut — 4 recipes remain.)*

| Recipe | Material cost (B/M/R) | Craft time | Cost-time category | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Oxygen Canister | 2/0/0 | 2 s | Curve | Cheapest aid; baseline disturbance-vs-survival exchange. Reducing time below 1.5 s makes the burst feel ungated; raising above 4 s undermines its "quick triage" role. **Round-5: now the squad's sole oxygen line during BC4** (Relay cut) — the OQ.13 oxygen-drain calibration (F.4 RM row) leans entirely on Canister stockpiling |
| Dampener Coil | 0/2/1 | 5 s | Curve | Time-limited 50% Light reduction (D.6). 1 RESONANT cost is the "expensive but stackable" floor |
| Signal Anchor | 2/1/0 | 5 s | Curve | **Round-7 re-weight (was 1/1/1) — Finding-7 BIOMASS-sink fix.** BIOMASS-weighted so all three node tiers keep a non-Beacon consumer: BIOMASS (Canister + Anchor + Beacon), MINERAL (Coil + Anchor + Beacon), RESONANT (Coil + Beacon). Cost-neutral at 3 gathers. Single-shot sensor (C.7); cost reflects coordination value, not raw power |
| ~~Squad Relay~~ | ~~2/2/1~~ | ~~6 s~~ | — | **CUT (round-5, R4-2)** — dominated by stacked Oxygen Canisters under the B1 hold; row retired in place |
| Escape Beacon | 2/3/3 | 10 s | Curve | **Win condition** — total 8 gathers across squad. RESONANT-heavy. Craft time chosen so a 2-crafter run completes in 5 s (D.2) — the longest bench-dwell decision in the game |

**Material-sink honesty note (round-19 — R16-4 hedge propagated to the balance entry point; MINERAL given the same analysis economy gave RESONANT):** this cost table is where the per-tier practical demand is set, so the honesty hedge belongs here, not only at the Overview / C.5.6a / OQ.15. Per-tier non-Beacon consumers: **BIOMASS** → Canister (2/0/0) + Anchor (2/1/0) [two consumers, and the Canister is the dominant oxygen line, so BIOMASS has live practical demand]; **MINERAL** → Coil (0/2/1) + Anchor (2/1/0) [two non-Beacon consumers]; **RESONANT** → Coil (0/2/1) **only** [a single non-Beacon consumer, and the Coil's positional-freedom value is PA-gated]. **RESONANT hedge (R16-4):** pre-PA, RESONANT is functionally a **Beacon-premium material** — its only non-Beacon sink (the Coil) does not become *worth crafting* until PA makes repositioning rational; the binding closure is the F.4 PA row (d) AC ("≥ 1 Coil worth crafting in a winning run"), with the honest fallback of declaring RESONANT Beacon-only and recutting the Coil to MINERAL/BIOMASS if PA/RM playtest cannot satisfy it (OQ.15). **MINERAL analysis (round-19 — same lens):** MINERAL is **less exposed than RESONANT** because it has *two* non-Beacon consumers (Coil + Anchor) rather than one, so even in the dominant oxygen-heavy line (stack Canisters = BIOMASS, craft few Coils/Anchors) MINERAL retains the Anchor as a pre-activation-recon sink independent of the PA Coil gate; its practical-demand risk is the milder "Medium-node content is thin if Anchors are rarely crafted," a pure **playtest-tuning** question (OQ.13/OQ.15), not a structural single-point-of-failure like RESONANT. Net: BIOMASS healthy, MINERAL playtest-tuning, RESONANT structurally gated on the F.4 PA AC.

**Coupled tuning note**: shortening any non-Beacon craft time below 1.5 s collapses the multi-crafter dwell-reduction differentiation (D.2) — at very short dwells, N=1 vs N=2 is sub-second. The Beacon's 10 s craft time is the most-tunable single value in this section: at 6 s, N=2 dwell drops to 3 s and the bench-as-exposure beat (Section B) feels rushed; at 15 s, the squad's defensive window during the craft becomes the longest commitment in the game.

**Hard floor — `CRAFT_DWELL_TIME_FLOOR = 0.5 s` (round-3 D.1 guard):** every recipe's `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME` MUST be `>= 0.5 s`, enforced by a startup config-validation assertion (D.1). This is a *correctness* floor (below it the D.1 `1 / CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME` rate misbehaves; at exactly 0 it divides by zero → instant craft), distinct from the 1.5 s *design* floor above (below which multi-crafter differentiation collapses). A recipe configured below 0.5 s is a hard startup error, not a soft warning. Verified by H.82.

### G.4 — Recipe Burst Magnitude Knobs (Craft emission family)

Five magnitudes drive the disturbance burst published at `BT3` (per D.3), the Beacon midpulse (per D.7), and — round-7 — the in-window Oxygen Canister use (per C.6). All are emitted via `DisturbanceService:Emit("Craft", position, magnitude, sourcePlayerId)` — the single `Craft` enum slot consumes 1 of ED's 4 reserved emission types (all five magnitudes share the one enum value; only the magnitude tier differs). *(Round-2: `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_LIGHT = 0.12` was retired with the Composite Patch + Quiet Step Wrap cut — it was the only tier those two recipes used; no surviving recipe sits in the LIGHT band.)* *(Round-3, 2026-06-01: `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_RELAY = 0.20` was **retired** — the Squad Relay's completion burst was merged down to `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_MID = 0.18`. Number retired, not reused — D.4/D.5 precedent.)* *(Round-5, 2026-06-02: the **Squad Relay recipe itself is cut** (R4-2), so the MID-merge is moot — no recipe uses a RELAY tier, and the only MID-tier recipes are Coil + Anchor. `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_RELAY` stays retired.)*

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_XS` | 0.10 | 0.05–0.18 | Curve | C.2 (Oxygen Canister) | At 0.05, oxygen canister becomes "free" — Pillar 1 weakens for the most-frequently-crafted item | At 0.18, lapses toward MID tier — the "cheap craft" tier collapses |
| `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_MID` | 0.18 | 0.12–0.30 | Curve | C.2 (Coil + Anchor — round-5: Squad Relay cut) | At 0.12, MID tier collapses toward XS — recipe tier differentiation weakens | At 0.30, MID approaches HEAVY — Coil/Anchor crafts become predator-magnets |
| ~~`MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_RELAY`~~ | ~~0.20~~ | — | — | — | **RETIRED (round-3, 2026-06-01)** — Squad Relay's completion burst merged into `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_MID = 0.18`. Constant deleted from the registry; number not reused. | — |
| `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_HEAVY` | 0.35 | 0.25–0.55 | Curve | C.2 (Beacon completion) | At 0.25, the Beacon's pre-activation craft burst feels indistinguishable from MID-tier — the win-condition's "loudest pre-activation craft" framing collapses | At 0.55, the completion burst contributes ≥0.50 to bench-position fieldValue at zero distance, pushing the bench into Hunt-tier on completion alone |
| `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE` | 0.18 | 0.10–0.35 | Curve | C.2, D.7 (Beacon midpulse) | At 0.10, the midpulse fades below "warning" perceptibility — the squad doesn't hear the halftime beat | At 0.35, equals HEAVY — squad effectively gets two completion bursts during the craft, which double-fires the predator-investigation event at the bench |
| `MAGNITUDE_CANISTER_INWINDOW_USE` (round-7, Group-B bunker counter) | 0.18 (MID) | 0.10–0.35 | Curve | C.2, C.6, C.11 (in-window Oxygen Canister use) | At 0.10 (XS), in-window Canister use is near-cosmetic and the quiet-bunker line partially returns — the breath the predator should hear barely registers | At 0.35 (HEAVY), a single emergency Canister spikes the squad's position to Hunt-floor on its own, punishing legitimate emergency use into "never use a Canister" (which would mint a new degenerate line — see H.93 / the round-7 economy-gate risk flag). At MID, ~3 in-window uses sum at the **beacon's position** (round-11 EC-3) into a Hunt-floor-saturating fixed point the committing predator reads. Tunes with the RM `OXYGEN_DRAIN_BC4_FLOOR` (OQ.13) |

**Coupled tuning note**: the magnitude tier ratios (XS:MID:HEAVY ≈ 1 : 1.8 : 3.5, with the Beacon midpulse sharing MID at 0.18) encode the "every craft is a noise bargain" framing from Section B. *(Round-5: with the Squad Relay cut, the MID tier holds exactly two recipes — Coil + Anchor — so the squad learns one "cheap" (Canister, XS), two "medium" (Coil/Anchor, MID), and one "loud" (Beacon, HEAVY). The former round-3 RELAY 0.20 sub-tier and the Relay recipe itself are both gone.)* Adjusting any one magnitude in isolation breaks the squad's mental model of which recipes are "loud." Tune the family proportionally.

**`MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE` is intentionally a separate constant from `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_MID`** even though they share value (0.18) at default. The midpulse and the Beacon completion have different acoustic functions: the midpulse is a warning beat, the completion is the loudest pre-activation event. A future tuning pass may want to diverge them (e.g., midpulse 0.15, MID 0.20) without consequence — keep them split.

### G.5 — Aid Item Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DAMPENER_DURATION` | 60 s | 20–180 | Curve | C.6, D.6 | At 20 s, the Coil's window barely covers two Light-pulse intervals — feels worthless given the 5-s craft cost | At 180 s, the Coil persists across multiple Hunt-tier transitions; effectively permanent silent-lantern for that player |
| `DAMPENER_REDUCTION` | 0.50 | **0.30–0.56 (interim, round-11)**; 0.30–0.70 post-PC | Curve | C.6, D.6, D.6a | At 0.30, the Coil's perceived effect feels marginal (0.08 → 0.056 — a thin slice) | **Interim ceiling 0.56 (round-11, systems BLOCK-3):** the original 0.70 ceiling's rationale — "a *moving* Coil player matches max stationary attenuation, hence near-silent" — only holds once **PC applies the D.6a Stage-2 stationary factor** (PC in MAJOR REVISION; the Stage-2 half is unverified-until-PC, D.6a). Until PC lands, a moving Coil player at 0.70 emits `0.08 × (1−0.70) = 0.024` (above `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02` — NOT silent), so the silencing claim is premature and 0.70 over-tunes the Coil. The tunable ceiling is held at **0.56** in the interim; **0.70 is the post-PC ceiling**, restored once PC's Stage-2 is verified. (A *stationary* Coil player can still fall below the floor via Stage-2, D.6 — that path is PC-owned.) |
| `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_LIFETIME` | 45 s | 15–120 | Curve | C.6, C.7, E.16 | At 15 s, the Anchor expires before the squad can reposition to react to its trip event | At 120 s, the Anchor becomes a near-permanent perimeter sensor — approaches base-building behaviour (game-concept anti-pillar). The single-Anchor-at-a-time rule (E.16) mitigates this; lifetime is the secondary brake |
| `SIGNAL_ANCHOR_DETECTION_RADIUS` | 30 studs | 12–60 | Gate | C.7 | At 12, the predator can pass within 15 studs of the Anchor undetected — Anchor feels broken | At 60+, the Anchor covers ~2.5× INFLUENCE_RADIUS and becomes a "predator-tracking minimap" — over-powers the squad's spatial awareness loop |
| `SENSOR_CHECK_HZ` | 5 Hz | 2–10 | Gate | C.7 | At 2 Hz, fast predator transits past the Anchor can slip between samples (an accepted false-negative per systems-designer review) | At 10 Hz, doubled server CPU cost for marginal accuracy improvement |

### G.6 — Beacon Lifecycle Knobs

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_PROGRESS` | 0.5 | 0.4–0.6 | Feel | D.7 | Below 0.4, the midpulse fires too early — feels like a "craft started" cue, not a halftime warning | Above 0.6, midpulse and completion cluster too tightly — the halftime-beat cadence collapses |
| `BEACON_PLACE_MAX_RANGE` | 8 studs | 4–16 | Gate | C.15 | At 4, players cannot place the Beacon at the most strategic plinth corner of a safe room — placement feels overconstrained | At 16, players can place the Beacon while sprinting past the plinth — "ritual placement" framing breaks |
| `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` (round-3, B1; continuous-hold round-13; **tightened round-15, SENTRY fix**) | **12 studs** | 8–20 | Gate | C.5.6a, C.9 BCT4/BCT-DEFEAT | At 8, the hold is a near-pinpoint last-stand on the plinth with almost no jitter room — combined with the `requiredHolders` rule a brief knockback on any holder dips the count below threshold and starts the grace very frequently; only viable if `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE` is at its upper end | At 20+, a single holder can stand near a defensible corner ~18 studs off the plinth rather than on it, and (with the `requiredHolders` rule relaxed) the round-14 **SENTRY** line — "appoint one member to camp a corner while everyone plays freely" — partially returns; the round-15 ruling set 12 so "in-radius" ≡ "on the beacon" and "hold the line" is literal. **The win snapshot uses the strict `dist3D <= BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` test; the HUD crossing cue is debounced by `BEACON_HOLD_HYSTERESIS` (presentation-only).** |
| `BEACON_HOLD_MIN_HOLDERS` rule (**round-15 SENTRY fix; round-16 R16-1 FROZEN-at-activation; round-19 R18-1 from the max-alive-so-far ratchet — derived, NOT a free knob**) | `requiredHolders = min(requiredHoldersBaseline, #aliveMembers)`, `requiredHoldersBaseline = ceil(peakAliveCount/2)` **frozen at BCT3** | n/a (baseline frozen at activation from the run high-water; per-tick value derived) | Gate | C.5.6a, C.9, C.10 | **`requiredHoldersBaseline` is computed ONCE at window-open (BCT3)** from the run's **high-water alive count** `peakAliveCount` (round-19 R18-1 — NOT the activation-snapshot count) — `ceil(1/2)=1`, `ceil(2/2)=1`, `ceil(3/2)=2`, `ceil(4/2)=2` over the high-water — and held frozen; the per-tick threshold clamps it down to the current alive count (`min(baseline, #aliveMembers)`) but never up. **`held ⟺ #windowAliveInRadius >= requiredHolders`.** Requiring **at least half the largest squad the run fielded** to hold is what closes SENTRY (one of four can no longer hold for the rest) **and the round-18 activate-late lane** (delaying activation until teammates die no longer lowers the bar — the ratchet already recorded the bigger squad). Lone survivor (`min(baseline, 1) = 1`) still wins by holding alone. **A mid-window respawn can NEVER raise the bar above the baseline (round-16 R16-1 — closing the round-16 E.33 fantasy violation; a respawn after deaths can restore the requirement toward, never above, the baseline); a death cannot relax the BASELINE below itself. The baseline is monotone-frozen; the live `requiredHolders` is non-monotone (2→1→2) by design.** This is a structural rule, not a tunable constant; it has no range. See E.33. |
| `BEACON_HOLD_HYSTERESIS` (round-11, Conv #4) | 3 studs | 1–8 | Feel | C.9/C.10 hold-state, C.14 | At 1, the debounce band is narrower than typical network/animation position jitter, so a player at the boundary can still flood `OnBeaconHoldStateChanged` (the round-10 flood the band exists to stop) | At 8+, the HUD "out-of-radius" cue lags a genuine departure by up to 8 studs of travel — a player who actually left the hold still reads as "held" too long. **Presentation-only debounce on the HUD crossing signal: it NEVER affects the win snapshot, which is the strict `<= BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` test (C.9 / H.92).** |
| `BEACON_HOLD_ESCALATION_LEAD` (round-11, Conv #5) | 10 s | 4–20 | Feel | UI.1 surface 13, C.14 | At 4, an out-of-radius player gets the "GET BACK before the timer closes" urgency too late to traverse `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` back to the beacon — the cue cannot prevent the loss it warns of | At 20+, the escalation fires for most of the window and the "almost survived" semantics are lost — the indicator nags as if leaving were already failing. **Presentation-only: drives only the surface-13 escalation styling; never changes the win evaluation (C.9).** |
| `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_GRACE` (round-13, B1 fail-fast; **range capped round-15**) | 3 s | **0–4** | Feel/Gate | C.5.6a, C.9 step b, C.10 `_lineBreakSince` | At 0, the line-break is **instant** — a single predator knockback, a forced reposition, or position jitter at the radius boundary that drops `#windowAliveInRadius` below `requiredHolders` loses the run outright; harsh and exploitable by knockback AI. (0 degenerates the continuous rule back to a hair-trigger.) | **Capped at 4 s (round-15, game-designer anti-kiting):** above ~4 s a squad can deliberately break the hold to dodge a threat and still re-establish, partially reopening the KITING scatter line the continuous rule exists to close. **With `BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS` tightened to 12 (round-15), 3 s is now traversable on mobile** — a member 12 studs out re-enters well inside 3 s (perception ~0.3–0.8 s + orientation ~0.3–0.6 s + a ≤12-stud traverse ~0.7 s), resolving the round-14 game-vs-ux grace disagreement by shrinking the radius rather than stretching the grace. **Gates the scatter-defeat terminal; wipe (aliveMembers empty) ignores it (immediate).** |
| `BEACON_LINE_BREAK_RESTORE_DEBOUNCE` (**round-15 — quadruple-lens debounce fix**) | 6 ticks | 1–30 | Feel | C.9 step d, C.10 hold-state, C.14 `OnBeaconLineBreak`, UI.1 surface 13 | Governs ONLY the **HUD** `OnBeaconLineBreak(broken=false)` restore edge (presentation), never the terminal. `broken=true` fires **immediately** on the first below-threshold tick (fail-fast alarm — you want "BROKEN" instantly); `broken=false` fires only after **`BEACON_LINE_BREAK_RESTORE_DEBOUNCE` consecutive ticks** at-or-above threshold. At 1, a sole holder oscillating at the 12-stud boundary flips the squad-level win-state banner at up to 60 Hz (the round-14 N14-B2 / WCAG 2.3.1 flash) — the band exists to stop that. At 30+ (~0.5 s) the "back to HOLDING" cue lags a genuine recovery. **The `_lineBreakSince` terminal grace clock is the authority and is UNAFFECTED — it uses the strict per-tick threshold test; this knob debounces only the cosmetic banner.** Covered by H.95 (WCAG flash cap). |
| `POSITION_SETTLED_EPSILON` (**round-15 — B-3 re-add positioned predicate**) | 1.0 stud | 0.5–4 | Gate | C.10 `_pendingReadds` | The observable predicate for "the respawned `HumanoidRootPart` has been positioned": `hrp.Position.Magnitude > POSITION_SETTLED_EPSILON` (a freshly-parented-but-unplaced root part reads world origin `(0,0,0)`; a placed character is at a `SpawnLocation` well away from origin). At <0.5 a one-frame origin read could slip through; at >4 a legitimate near-origin spawn would be missed (Terranova spawns are not near origin — see C.10 note). Used only to gate the buffered re-add `_enqueueReadd` (B-3). |
| `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` (**derived — NOT independently tunable**) | ≈ 49 s (at `BEACON_HALF_LIFE = 90 s`) | follows ED's `BEACON_HALF_LIFE`; see note below | Curve | C.5.6a, C.9 | A *shorter* window (from a shorter `BEACON_HALF_LIFE`) is too short to demand aid — squad can bunker through it, and the round-1 Beacon-rush dominance partially returns; the design-target floor is ~35 s | A *longer* window outlasts the predator's peak-threat commit (the beacon has decayed below Hunt-floor) and becomes a dead waiting period; the design-target ceiling is ~70 s |

The wall-clock value `CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_TIME = 5 s` is a **derived** value at default tunings (`CRAFT_BEACON_MIDPULSE_PROGRESS × CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME = 0.5 × 10 s`), not an independently tunable knob. The progress fraction is the registered tunable; the seconds value follows from it.

`BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW ≈ 49 s` is likewise a **derived** value, **owned by Ecological Disturbance** (the interval the Beacon holds at or above `HUNT_THRESHOLD = 0.65`). The closed form is:

`BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW = BEACON_HALF_LIFE × log₂(MAGNITUDE_BEACON / HUNT_THRESHOLD) = BEACON_HALF_LIFE × log₂(0.95 / 0.65) ≈ BEACON_HALF_LIFE × 0.5476` (the closed form `log₂(0.95/0.65) = 0.547488…` is canonical; `0.5476` is the rounded display value).

At the default `BEACON_HALF_LIFE = 90 s` this is `90 × 0.5476 ≈ 49.3 s` ✓. **The window is a strict function of `BEACON_HALF_LIFE` — it is NOT independently tunable**, and the "≈ 35–70 s" *design-target band* is therefore a constraint on `BEACON_HALF_LIFE`, not a free range on the window. Inverting: a 35 s window needs `BEACON_HALF_LIFE ≈ 64 s`; a 70 s window needs `≈ 128 s`. So the design-safe band ⟹ **`BEACON_HALF_LIFE ∈ ~[64, 128] s`**.

**Cross-GDD lock (round-3 G.6 fix; CD ruling R4-1, round-5):** ED's registry currently ranges `BEACON_HALF_LIFE ∈ [45, 180] s`, which would produce windows of `[24.6, 98.9] s` — *wider than the design-safe band on both ends* (a 45 s half-life gives a ~25 s window the squad can bunker through; a 180 s half-life gives a ~99 s dead-wait window). **CD ruling R4-1 (2026-06-02): ED tightens `BEACON_HALF_LIFE` to `[64, 128] s`; Crafting does NOT decouple.** This is now a binding forward obligation on ED (F.4) + a registry lock, **not** a Crafting decision — the "window = ED's lure interval" identity is preserved, and the alternative (Crafting owns `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW` directly, breaking that identity) was rejected by the creative-director. This GDD does **not** redefine the window — it reads it from ED's beacon math (ED.D.7); tuning the window means tuning ED's `BEACON_HALF_LIFE` inside the locked `[64, 128] s` band, not adding a Crafting-side constant. With the tightened band the derived window is `[64 × 0.5476, 128 × 0.5476] = [35.0, 70.1] s`, exactly the design-safe band. *(Round-3: the pre-round-3 G.6 row listed "derived (≈ 35–70 s effective range)" as if the window itself were tunable across that span — unsupported by the decay formula at ED's then-actual range; corrected round-3, locked round-5.)* OQ.14 is therefore **resolved** (pending ED's registry edit applying the tightened band). The remaining open item is the pure-tuning OQ.2/OQ.13 question of where *within* `[35, 70] s` the window should sit — a playtest call, not a structural one.

**Crafting-side fail-fast init assertion (round-9, Blocker #7 — the guard the round-8 panel found missing):** the `BEACON_HALF_LIFE` registry range edit is **ED's** to apply (it is an ED-owned constant — coordination rules forbid Crafting changing it unilaterally), so it remains a fenced forward obligation (F.4 ED row). But Crafting no longer *depends* on that edit landing first: at `CraftingService` init, Crafting computes the derived `BEACON_SURVIVAL_WINDOW = resolved BEACON_HALF_LIFE × log₂(0.95/0.65)` (the exact closed form the service uses — **not** the rounded `0.5476` literal, so the `128 s` upper edge resolves unambiguously to `70.08 s`; H.105) and **asserts it falls within `[35.0, 70.1] s`** (equivalently `BEACON_HALF_LIFE ∈ [64, 128] s`). A resolved value outside the band is a **hard startup error** — the service refuses to start rather than run a degenerate window (same fail-fast pattern as the D.1 `CRAFT_BASE_DWELL_TIME` floor). So even if ED has not yet applied the registry tightening, a half-life that would yield a `~25 s` (bunker-through) or `~99 s` (dead-wait) window cannot silently reach a live run — round-8's "OQ.14 marked CLOSED but the guard is not in place" is closed from the Crafting side. Verified by H.105.

### G.7 — Rate Limit Knobs

All RemoteEvent rate limits are server-enforced per the C.15 trust boundary. The per-event limits gate individual flows; the global limit caps cycle-between-events bypass attempts.

| Knob | Default | Range | Category | Section | Too Low | Too High |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RequestCraft_RATE` | 1/s/player sustained; burst 3 per 3 s | (0.5–2)/s | Gate | C.15 | At 0.5/s, legitimate retry-after-rejection patterns (insufficient-materials race) get throttled | At 2/s+, rapid-fire craft spam approaches global-limit; logging volume increases. Burst ceiling (3/3 s) ≥ sustained rate (1/s) so a same-second retry is permitted (round-2 B12 fix — round-1 'burst 2/3 s' was *below* the 1/s sustained rate, a self-contradiction that throttled legitimate retries) |
| `RequestUseItem_RATE` | 1/s/player sustained; burst 3 per 3 s | (0.5–3)/s | Gate | C.15 | At 0.5/s, a legitimate back-to-back Oxygen Canister sequence during Hunt-tier flight gets throttled | At 3/s+, exploit surface widens for buffered rapid-equip-then-use chains. C.15 sets the canonical value; this knob's tuning range is narrower than other rate limits because item-use is intentionally a deliberate action. Burst ceiling ≥ sustained rate (round-2 B12) so retry-after-rejection is not falsely throttled |
| `RequestPlaceItem_RATE` | 1/(5 s)/player | (3–15) s | Gate | C.15 | At 3 s, players can spam Anchor placements during Hunt-tier (single-Anchor rule limits damage but still increases server load) | At 15 s, players cannot place a second Anchor in a Hunt-tier emergency — Anchor utility diminishes |
| `RequestPlaceBeacon_RATE` | 1/(5 s)/player | (3–10) s | Gate | C.15 | At 3 s, ritual placement is not gated against fat-finger re-fires | At 10 s, legitimate retry after `invalid-placement` rejection feels punishing |
| `CRAFTING_GLOBAL_RATE_LIMIT` | 8 events/s/player | 4–16 | Gate | C.15 | At 4, legitimate squad with 4 simultaneous bench actions hits the cap | At 16, the global cap stops gating — falls back to per-event limits only, which the game-concept tech risk identifies as bypassable |

Solo-play (`squadSize = 1` at start) is unsupported (round-3). |

## Visual/Audio Requirements

This section specifies the visual and audio language for all 24 distinct Crafting moments (numbered 1–28; Moments 16 + 17 retired in the round-2 catalog cut, Moments 24 + 25 retired in the round-5 Squad Relay cut, Moments 26 + 27 added for the round-2 survival window, Moment 28 added for the round-7 in-window Oxygen Canister-use burst — net 24 live moments). Authored 2026-04-30 by `art-director` consult, anchored to `design/art/art-bible.md` Sections 1, 2.4, 2.7, 3.3, 4.2, 4.3, 4.5, 4.8, 7.3, 7.4. The crafting visual vocabulary is intentionally consolidated into 6 effect families (see VA.4) so the implementation surface is much smaller than 23 separate effects suggest.

### VA.1 — Craft Tier Intensity Vocabulary

All burst-at-completion moments (and the Beacon midpulse) reference this tier table. The three tiers correspond 1:1 to the `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_*` family in G.4 (the LIGHT tier was retired with the round-2 Composite Patch + Quiet Step Wrap cut; the **RELAY tier was retired round-3** when the Squad Relay's burst merged into MID 0.18 — no surviving recipe sits in the LIGHT or RELAY bands).

| Tier | Magnitude | Particles (PC / mobile) | PointLight Brightness | Flash duration | Range |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **XS** | 0.10 | 8 / 4 | 1.5 | 120 ms | 2 studs |
| **MID** | 0.18 | 18 / 9 | 2.8 | 200 ms | 5 studs |
| ~~**RELAY**~~ | ~~0.20~~ | — | — | — | — | *RETIRED round-3 (tier merged to MID); the **Squad Relay recipe itself is cut round-5** (R4-2), so no recipe uses this tier and Moments 24/25 are retired* |
| **HEAVY** | 0.35 | 40 / 20 | 5.0 | 350 ms | 8 studs |

All burst PointLights use **cool-white** `Color3.fromRGB(200, 210, 230)` (gap-resolution G-A1, matching wrist lantern — "same light source" vocabulary). Particles are spherical Neon-material Parts spawned at the bench center, traveling outward 1–2 studs, fading over 200–400 ms. Mobile-safe via Neon Part + PointLight Tween (no GPU particle instancing required). No burst PointLight persists beyond its flash duration.

Tool events (Anchor trip — round-5: Relay activation removed with the Relay cut) use **teal-white** `Color3.fromRGB(95, 255, 216)` instead of cool-white, distinguishing "tool event" from "craft event" vocabulary.

### VA.2 — Locked design-gap resolutions

| Gap | Resolution | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **G-A1** Craft burst color | Cool-white `Color3.fromRGB(200, 210, 230)`. Reuses wrist lantern color; consistent with art-bible Principles 1 + 3. | locked 2026-04-30 |
| **G-A2** Bench idle appearance | `SmoothPlastic`, color Equipment Slate `#2E3138`, **no emissive at rest** (BS1 state). | locked 2026-04-30 (level-design phase confirms) |
| **G-A3** Bench/craft/aid SFX family | Mechanical-mineral-organic blend; distinct from predator's purely biological signature. | locked 2026-04-30 (Audio system ADR will detail) |
